Added `collection-card` / `collection-grid`, `item-card` / `item-list`, and one component per dashboard section so the page only composes. `ITEM_TYPES_BY_ID` in `src/lib/item-types.ts` resolves type ids; `formatShortDate` moved into `src/lib/utils.ts`. Color coding now flows through a `--type-color` custom property resolved from `data-type`, replacing the sidebar-specific rules from phase 2. - **2026-08-14 — Prisma 7 + Neon PostgreSQL.** Stood up the database layer. Schema in `prisma/schema.prisma` follows the project-overview draft (NextAuth models, `ContentType` enum, cascade deletes, indexes) plus `ItemType.slug` and `@@index([userId, updatedAt])` on Item. Migration `20260814120000_init` applied to the Neon dev branch and verified end to end through the app. `prisma/seed.ts` seeds the seven system item types idempotently. Prisma 7 specifics: `prisma-client` generator (not `prisma-client-js`) outputting TypeScript to `src/generated/prisma` (gitignored, rebuilt by `postinstall`), the mandatory driver adapter (`@prisma/adapter-neon` over `@neondatabase/serverless`) in `src/lib/prisma.ts`, connection URLs moved to `prisma.config.ts`, and dotenv loading `.env.local`/`.env` since v7 no longer reads env files itself. CLI uses `DIRECT_URL` (Neon's pooler cannot hold the schema engine's advisory locks), runtime uses pooled `DATABASE_URL`. Scripts: `db:migrate`, `db:deploy`, `db:status`, `db:seed`, `db:studio`. Spec: @context/features/database-spec.md
- **2026-08-14 — Seed data.** Rewrote `prisma/seed.ts` to populate a demo account and sample content: `demo@devstash.io` / "Demo User" (password `12345678`, bcryptjs 12 rounds, `isPro: false`), the seven system item types (`isSystem: true`, `userId: null`, find-then-write since Postgres treats NULLs as distinct in the unique index), and five collections holding 18 items — React Patterns (3 snippets), AI Workflows (3 prompts), DevOps (snippet + command + 2 links), Terminal Commands (4 commands), Design Resources (4 links). Re-running rebuilds the demo user's content instead of duplicating it. Added `bcryptjs` as a runtime dependency (auth will reuse it). `scripts/test-db.ts` now prints the seeded data and asserts integrity: no orphaned items, TEXT items have `content`, URL items have `url`, and no item carries both. Spec: @context/features/seed-spec.md
- **2026-08-15 — Dashboard collections from the database.** The dashboard's collection grid now reads from Neon instead of `src/lib/mock-data.ts`. - **2026-08-16 — Documentation cleanup.** A `/cleanup` pass found no code to fix — no `console.log` in `src/`, no unused imports (`tsc --noUnusedLocals` and `eslint` both clean), no TODOs, no stale `@ts-ignore`, no orphaned modules or unused CSS classes — so this was documentation only. `README.md` went from `create-next-app` boilerplate to a real project README: status, stack, setup through seed, the `DATABASE_URL` (pooled) vs `DIRECT_URL` (direct) split, all eleven scripts, folder layout and conventions. `CLAUDE.md` gained the six `db:*` commands, and its "no test script" line was corrected — `db:test` exists but runs the seeded-data integrity checker, not a test suite. Project overview §7 was relabelled **Target** and reconciled with reality: dropped `tailwind.config.ts` (which contradicted the v4 rule in coding-standards), added `context/`, `scripts/`, `prisma/seed.ts`, `prisma.config.ts`, `components/dashboard/`, `lib/db/` and `src/generated/`, plus a "Current state" subsection recording that the `(marketing)`/`(auth)`/`(app)` route groups do not exist yet — everything lives under `src/app/dashboard/` until auth lands — and listing the `lib/` modules still unwritten. `add-pro-badge-sidebar.md` was renamed to `-spec.md` to match the other eight specs. `DIRECT_URL` was added to `.env.production`, which had only `DATABASE_URL`; note that file is gitignored and `prisma.config.ts` reads only `.env.local`/`.env`, so production needs the variable set in the host environment. `/.claude` is now gitignored, keeping local settings and skills untracked.
- **2026-08-16 — Code-scanner follow-up fixes.** Acted on a `code-scanner` pass: the two duplicate-query findings plus the quick wins it rated low risk. Nothing user-visible changed — the dashboard renders identically on fewer queries. **Current user, five lookups → one:** `getCurrentUser` in `src/lib/db/user.ts` is wrapped in React's `cache()` and `getCurrentUserId` now derives from it instead of running its own `findUnique`, so the layout and all four dashboard sections share one query. **Collections, five queries → one:** the private `findCollections` became a cached `getCollections(userId)` fetching the user's collections once; `getRecentCollections` slices it, `getSidebarCollections` partitions it on `isFavorite`, and `getCollectionStats` derives `total`/`favorites` from the rows — dropping both `collection.count` calls. The query is deliberately unbounded (no `take`) because the sidebar needs *every* favorite, which is fine while the free tier caps collections at 3 and `/collections` doesn't exist; it compounds with the deferred finding below, so those two want fixing together. `cache()` is per-request memoization, not a persistent cache, so `force-dynamic` on both routes stays and nothing goes stale between requests. **`initials()`** in `src/components/layout/sidebar.tsx` gave a one-letter avatar to accounts with no `name`, since the email fallback has no space to split on; it now splits the local part on `. _ -` and falls back to its first two characters (`Demo User` still renders `DU`). **`prisma/seed.ts`** throws when `NODE_ENV=production` so the known-password demo account can't be created against a production branch. **`devIndicators: false`** in `next.config.ts` hides the dev overlay's `data-next-badge`, which sat on top of the sidebar's user area — note it disables the whole indicator including the route-type readout, leaves the error overlay alone, and needs a dev server restart. Verified by temporarily enabling Prisma query logging on a separate production server: one `/dashboard` render issues 1 user query and 1 collection query, against output matching the seed exactly (stats 18/5/1/1, no collection listed twice in the sidebar); the logging was reverted. Two scanner findings were deliberately left out as not-quick-wins: `findCollections` still pulls every item row just to count them and rank types (the fix is a `_count` + `groupBy` query rewrite touching shared sidebar/grid logic, rated medium risk), and splitting the 231-line `Sidebar` into brand / types / collections / user subcomponents (mechanical, but a refactor).
- **2026-08-16 — Skeleton loading states.** The dashboard streams instead of blocking on its slowest query. Added the ShadCN `skeleton` primitive (`src/components/ui/skeleton.tsx`, untouched CLI output — the fifth file in `components/ui/`) and wrapped each of the four sections in `<Suspense>`, so the heading paints immediately and stats / collections / pinned / recent each fill in as their own query resolves. `app/dashboard/layout.tsx` is **no longer async**: its fetching moved into a local `SidebarWithData` server component behind its own boundary, which lets the shell, top bar and page render in parallel with the sidebar's queries — previously nothing rendered until the sidebar's types and collections came back. Both routes stay `force-dynamic`, and the `cache()` wrappers on `getCurrentUser` / `getCollections` still memoize across separate boundaries, so splitting the page added no queries. Fallbacks live in `src/components/dashboard/dashboard-skeletons.tsx` (all four sections in one module — they have a single consumer) and `src/components/layout/sidebar-skeleton.tsx`. They **reuse the real components' classes** (`stat-card`, `collection-card`, `item-card`, `sidebar-link`) for their box model, so `globals.css` only had to size the blocks standing in for content; text placeholders sit *inside* the element they replace and take `height: 1lh`, which is exactly one line of that element's type rather than a guessed pixel height. Counts mirror the real limits (6 collections, 10 recent items); pinned is a guess at 2 since the count isn't known until the query returns — and the section returns `null` when nothing is pinned, so that skeleton can vanish rather than swap. The sidebar skeleton keeps the brand static (it needs no data) and gives its rows the real `.sidebar-link-label` class so the collapsed 3.5rem rail hides them exactly as when loaded. `animation: none` for `[data-slot="skeleton"]` joined the existing `prefers-reduced-motion` block, since Tailwind's `animate-pulse` isn't gated on that query. No `app/dashboard/loading.tsx` was added — with the layout sync the shell is static, so a route-level file would only duplicate the per-section boundaries. Verified by temporarily delaying `getCurrentUser` by 1.5s: `/dashboard` answered with a 63 ms TTFB against a 3.19 s total, flushing the shell and all 123 skeleton nodes at byte ~3.3k with the real content arriving at ~103k; the delay was reverted. Also confirmed `1lh` survives Lightning CSS into the built stylesheet.
- **2026-08-18 — Auth phase 1: NextAuth v5 + GitHub OAuth.** Authentication exists for the first time. Installed `next-auth@5.0.0-beta.32` (the `@beta` tag — `@latest` still resolves to v4) and `@auth/prisma-adapter@2.11.3`. The config is **split for edge compatibility**: `src/auth.config.ts` carries the GitHub provider alone (`satisfies NextAuthConfig`), and `src/auth.ts` spreads it while adding `PrismaAdapter(prisma)` over the existing `src/lib/prisma.ts` singleton — not a fresh `PrismaClient` as the Auth.js docs show — plus `session: { strategy: "jwt" }`. The split exists because `src/proxy.ts` runs in the edge runtime, where the Prisma adapter cannot go; the JWT strategy is what makes the session readable there without it. `src/proxy.ts` is Next.js 16's replacement for `middleware.ts` and must use the **named** `proxy` export (the build template dispatches on `mod.proxy` for `/proxy` and `/src/proxy`, falling back to `mod.default`); it wraps `auth()`, returns `undefined` when `req.auth` is set, and otherwise redirects to `/api/auth/signin` with `callbackUrl` set to the requested href. Matcher is `["/dashboard", "/dashboard/:path*"]` — the second pattern alone would cover `/dashboard`, but both are listed so the intent survives a future edit. `src/app/api/auth/[...nextauth]/route.ts` is a two-line `export const { GET, POST } = handlers`. Per the spec no `pages.signIn` was set, so NextAuth's built-in sign-in page is what renders. **The docs' JWT type augmentation does not work on this version:** `declare module "next-auth/jwt"` creates a second, unrelated interface, because that module only re-exports `@auth/core/jwt` — `token.id` stayed the `unknown` of the `Record<string, unknown>` the real `JWT` extends, failing the build with `Type '{}' is not assignable to type 'string'`. `src/types/next-auth.d.ts` therefore augments `@auth/core/jwt` for the JWT claim (with a comment recording why) while `Session.user.id` augments `next-auth` normally; the `jwt` / `session` callbacks in `auth.ts` carry the adapter's user id across, since it only reaches the token on sign-in. Env vars (`AUTH_SECRET`, `AUTH_GITHUB_ID`, `AUTH_GITHUB_SECRET`) live in `.env`, not the `.env.local` the spec named — that file does not exist in this project, and Next loads both. `.env.example` gained empty placeholders plus the OAuth app's Homepage and Callback URLs as comments. `.gitignore` now excludes `/.playwright-mcp` so browser-verification artifacts stay out of commits. Two false trails cost time and are worth remembering: `AUTH_GITHUB_ID` in `.env` was missing its **opening** quote, so the trailing `"` became part of the value and GitHub 404'd the authorize URL (`client_id=…cM%22`); and the OAuth app's callback URL was not yet registered, which GitHub reports as "the `redirect_uri` is not associated with this application" — neither is an app-side bug, and `GET /api/auth/providers` is the quickest way to prove the app is sending the right `callbackUrl` (`http://localhost:3000/api/auth/callback/github`, derived from request origin + default `/api/auth` basePath + provider id, hardcoded nowhere). Verified end to end: `/dashboard` redirects to the sign-in page, and a real GitHub sign-in wrote `User` + `Account` rows (`provider: github`, `type: oauth`) to the Neon dev branch. **Nothing consumes the session yet** — `src/lib/db/user.ts` still resolves the seeded `demo@devstash.io` account, so the dashboard renders demo data no matter who signs in; swapping it is phase 2's job, as is moving `/dashboard` into the `(app)` route group. No unit tests were written: the feature's whole testable surface is three one-line branches inside object literals passed to `NextAuth()` and `auth()`, and reaching them would mean adding a test runner (none is configured) plus refactoring two files purely to make them importable. Spec: @context/features/auth-phase-1-spec.md
- **2026-08-18 — Auth phase 2: credentials (email/password).** Email/password sign-in and registration now sit alongside GitHub OAuth. Four files: `src/lib/validations/auth.ts` (new), `src/app/api/auth/register/route.ts` (new), plus edits to `src/auth.config.ts` and `src/auth.ts`. **No migration** — `User.passwordHash` already existed from the initial schema (`String?`, null for OAuth-only accounts), so the spec's conditional "add password field" requirement was already satisfied; `npm run db:status` stayed clean throughout. **`zod` (^4.4.3) became a direct dependency** — it was absent despite coding-standards mandating it and project overview §7 planning `lib/validations/`; it was already in the tree transitively, which is why `npm install zod` reported "up to date" while still adding the entry. Zod 4 idioms differ from 3: `z.email()` is top-level rather than `z.string().email()`, and refine params take `error` rather than `message` (the latter still works, migrated internally by `normalizeParams`). **The split pattern held:** `auth.config.ts` declares `Credentials({ authorize: () => null })` so both runtimes agree on the provider list, and `auth.ts` swaps in the real provider. The swap needed a `withRealCredentials` helper rather than a one-line `.map`, because providers come in two shapes — `GitHub` is passed as a bare *function* with no `id`, while `Credentials(...)` is an already-called config object that has one; a naive `p.id === "credentials"` test throws on the function. The proxy never runs `authorize` (it only verifies the JWT) and its matcher is `/dashboard*`, so it never intercepts `/api/auth/*`; the placeholder is therefore unreachable in practice. **`/api/auth/register` coexists with the `[...nextauth]` catch-all** because a static App Router segment outranks a sibling catch-all — no config needed. The route validates with Zod, returns `{ success, data, error }`, and hashes at 12 rounds to match `prisma/seed.ts`. **Emails are trimmed and lowercased before the format check**, in one shared `emailSchema` used by both sign-in and registration — without it a `Test@Example.com` registration creates a row that `test@example.com` can never sign into; verified by confirming `TEST@Test.com␣␣` is caught as a duplicate. The duplicate pre-check is racy, so a `Prisma.PrismaClientKnownRequestError` / `P2002` catch backstops it with the same 409 (imported from `@/generated/prisma/client` as `Prisma.` — not the `internal/prismaNamespace` path, which also exports it but is private). **A `/feature review` pass found three issues, all fixed before merge.** (1) An unused `RegisterInput` export — dead per coding-standards, removed; phase 3's register form can re-add it. (2) **bcrypt silently truncates at 72 bytes**, confirmed locally: a 72-byte hash accepts an 86-byte password sharing its first 72 bytes, so a long passphrase would be interchangeable with a truncated variant. The register schema now rejects over-length passwords instead, counting **UTF-8 bytes via `TextEncoder`** rather than `.length`, since one character can encode to four. (3) **A ~235 ms timing oracle on sign-in:** returning early on an unknown email skipped bcrypt entirely, and bcrypt at 12 rounds costs ~220 ms, so response time leaked whether an address was registered — measured live at 0.354 s (registered) vs 0.127 s (unknown), a gap far larger than network jitter. Fixed by comparing against a constant throwaway 12-round hash (`ABSENT_PASSWORD_HASH`) whenever there is nothing real to match, which levels every rejection path; re-measured at 0.354 / 0.353 / 0.350 s for registered / unknown / OAuth-only. Note the register endpoint still leaks the same fact outright via its 409, but that is spec-mandated UX, not a defect. **One false trail worth remembering:** a 19-emoji (76-byte) password appeared to pass the new cap when posted through Git Bash with `curl -d`, which mangles multibyte input before curl sends it — Node confirmed the schema computed 76 bytes and rejected correctly, and re-posting via `--data-binary @file` returned the expected 400. Test multibyte payloads from a file on this shell, never inline. **Verified end to end** against the Neon dev branch: registration 201; duplicate (incl. case/whitespace variants) 409; mismatch, short password, over-length password, bad email, blank name and non-JSON all 400 with the right message; sign-in with a registered account *and* with the seeded `demo@devstash.io` / `12345678` both reach `/dashboard` with the real user id on the session; wrong password, unknown email and an OAuth-only account (no `passwordHash`) all return `CredentialsSignin`; GitHub's authorize redirect stays well-formed; and the browser flow renders both providers on the built-in sign-in page. **Still out of scope and worth deciding before phase 3:** the dashboard continues to render the demo account because `src/lib/db/user.ts` is unchanged, so it shows "Demo User" no matter who signs in; there is **no rate limiting** on either endpoint, leaving brute-force wide open; a GitHub-linked email gets a 409 on register and cannot add a password, which is account-linking territory; and no custom login/register UI exists. Testing left four accounts in the dev branch (`test@test.com`, `two@test.com`, `cap72@test.com`, `capemoji@test.com`) — harmless, but they inflate any user count until removed. No unit tests: still no test runner configured, and the testable surface is one `authorize` closure plus a route handler, both of which were exercised directly over HTTP instead. Spec: @context/features/auth-phase-2-spec.md
- **2026-08-18 — Auth phase 3: auth UI (sign in, register & sign out).** NextAuth's built-in pages are gone and the app finally renders the account that signed in. **`src/lib/db/user.ts` switched fully to the session** — `getCurrentUser` reads `auth()` and looks the row up by `session.user.id` instead of resolving the seeded `demo@devstash.io` account, so the dashboard's collections, items and stats now belong to whoever is signed in; a fresh GitHub account lands on an empty dashboard and the seeded content is reachable by signing in as demo. It reads the row rather than trusting the JWT's `name`/`image` (those are whatever the token was minted with) and returns `null` for a token whose account no longer exists; `CurrentUser` gained `image`. **`pages: { signIn, error }`** in `src/auth.config.ts` point both at `/sign-in`, which also now exports `SIGN_IN_PATH` and `DEFAULT_SIGN_IN_REDIRECT` — `src/proxy.ts` imports the former instead of hardcoding `/api/auth/signin`. Pages live in a new **`(auth)` route group** (`src/app/(auth)/layout.tsx` + `sign-in/` + `register/`), the first of the route groups project overview §7 has as the target layout; URLs stay top-level. Both pages are `force-dynamic` and bounce a signed-in visitor to `/dashboard`. **Sign-in runs on server actions** in `src/actions/auth.ts` (`signInWithCredentials` via `useActionState`, `signInWithGitHub`, `signOutAction`), register posts to the existing `/api/auth/register` from the client per the spec. **A `"use server"` module may only export async functions** — exporting the action's `SIGN_IN_INITIAL_STATE` object from it compiled fine and then failed at runtime with *"can only export async functions, found object"* on the first sign-out; the constant and its `SignInState` interface moved to `src/types/auth.ts`. That state also carries the submitted `email` back, because React resets the form once an action settles and a rejected sign-in would otherwise wipe the field. `AuthError` is only reported as "Incorrect email or password" when `cause.type === "CredentialsSignin"`; anything else is a misconfiguration and gets its own message, and non-`AuthError` throws must propagate since that is how a *successful* `signIn` redirects. `callbackUrl` (set by the proxy) is passed as `redirectTo` rather than used directly, so Auth.js's `redirect` callback drops off-origin values — no open redirect. The register form runs **the same `registerSchema` client-side** that the route enforces, so the 72-byte password cap and the match check surface without a round trip, and the 409 for an existing email is shown verbatim; success does `router.replace("/sign-in?registered=1")`, which renders an "Account created" notice. **`src/components/user/user-avatar.tsx`** is the reusable avatar, absorbing `initials()` out of `sidebar.tsx`; it renders the OAuth picture through `next/image`, which needed `images.remotePatterns` for `avatars.githubusercontent.com` in `next.config.ts` (the host is dictated by the sign-in provider). `.sidebar-user-avatar` became the shared `.user-avatar`, so the skeleton and the real row style from one rule. The account row moved to **`src/components/layout/sidebar-user.tsx`** — which also trims `Sidebar`, one of the two refactors the earlier code-scanner pass had deferred. **The spec contradicted itself** on the sidebar footer: "dropdown on avatar click" against "clicking on the icon should go to /profile". First pass read "the icon" as the existing gear and made it a `/profile` link with the avatar *and* name as the dropdown trigger; on review that became **avatar = dropdown trigger, name = link to `/profile`**, and the gear button is gone. That forced the name out of the trigger — a link nested in a button is invalid and the click would belong to whichever won — so `.sidebar-user-meta` is now a sibling of `.sidebar-user-trigger` rather than a child, the trigger shrank to the avatar alone (`flex: 1` → `flex-shrink: 0`), and it needs an explicit `aria-label="Account menu"` because `UserAvatar` is `aria-hidden` and the button would otherwise have no accessible name. `.sidebar-user-profile` and its entry in the collapsed-rail `display: none` list are gone with the gear. `lucide-react` v1 **dropped its brand icons**, so `Github` cannot be imported — the mark is inlined as `src/components/auth/github-mark.tsx`. **One unrelated fix was forced:** `dashboard-skeletons.tsx` nested the Skeleton `div` inside four `<p>` elements, throwing hydration errors on every dashboard load since the skeleton feature landed. That is pre-existing, but the dev overlay's error badge sits over the sidebar's user area and was intercepting pointer events on the new dropdown trigger — Playwright timed out on it — so the four `<p>` became `<div>` (same classes, same box model). No migration and no new dependency; `db:status` clean throughout. **Verified in the browser** against the Neon dev branch: sign-out redirects to `/sign-in`; a wrong password shows the error with the email preserved and the password cleared; `demo@devstash.io` signs in to the seeded 18 items / 5 collections; a newly registered account shows the empty dashboard; the register form catches mismatched passwords client-side and `DEMO@DevStash.io␣␣` server-side as a 409; GitHub OAuth completes and the avatar image renders from `avatars.githubusercontent.com`; `/dashboard` while signed out redirects with `callbackUrl` and returns there afterwards; signed-in `/sign-in` bounces to `/dashboard`; the collapsed 3.5rem rail shows the avatar alone. **Left open:** `/profile` does not exist, so the name link 404s; there is still **no rate limiting** on either endpoint; account linking (a GitHub email cannot add a password) is untouched; and testing added `phase3@test.com` to the four accounts phase 2 left in the dev branch. No unit tests — still no test runner, and the testable surface is one action closure plus a form handler, both exercised through the running app instead. Spec: @context/features/auth-phase-3-spec.md
- **2026-08-18 — Email verification on register (Resend).** A new credentials account must confirm its address before it can sign in. Eight new files, six edited, **no migration** — `User.emailVerified` and `model VerificationToken` were already in the schema from `20260814120000_init` (they ship with the Auth.js Prisma adapter and had simply never been used); `db:status` stayed clean throughout. Installed `resend` (^6.20.0). **`src/lib/email-verification.ts`** owns the whole token lifecycle plus the message: 32 random bytes as base64url travel in the link, but only their **SHA-256 digest** is stored, so a read of `VerificationToken` yields nothing that can verify an account — SHA-256 rather than bcrypt because 32 random bytes have no low-entropy guess for a slow hash to defend. Issuing first deletes any earlier token for the address, so a resend *invalidates* the previous link instead of leaving a widening set of live ones; TTL is 24h. **The mutation is split from the rendering:** `GET /api/auth/verify` spends the token and redirects to `/verify?status=…`, so the write lives in a route handler rather than in a server component's render. `/verify` (new, in the existing `(auth)` group) reports six states — `sent`, `send-failed`, `verified`, `already`, `expired`, `invalid` — from one `COPY` record, and carries the resend form on the four recoverable ones. Tokens are single-use: the row is deleted whatever the outcome, so a second click on a spent link reads `invalid`, which is worded to cover "already used" as well as "never existed" (there is no column to distinguish them, and Auth.js's own email flow has the same property). **The `emailVerified` check in `authorize` sits *after* the bcrypt comparison, not before it** — an early return there would skip bcrypt for every unverified account and hand back exactly the ~235 ms timing oracle that phase 2's `ABSENT_PASSWORD_HASH` exists to remove. Because it only fires once the password has already matched, naming the reason leaks nothing the caller did not just prove they knew, so it throws an `UnverifiedEmailError extends CredentialsSignin` carrying `code = "email_unverified"`; `credentialsMessage()` in `src/actions/auth.ts` reads `cause.code` defensively (a plain wrong password arrives with the default code) and maps that one value to a distinct message. Confirmed in the browser that the code survives `signIn` → the server action. **`POST /api/auth/resend-verification`** answers with one generic 200 for unknown, already-verified and OAuth-only addresses alike, behind `src/lib/rate-limit.ts` — a fixed-window in-memory counter at 3/email and 10/IP per 15 min, with `Retry-After` on the 429. That limiter is deliberately small: state is per-process and resets on restart, so it is a brake on casual abuse, not a quota; Redis (already pencilled into the stack) is what a real one needs. **Registration keeps the account when the mail fails** rather than rolling it back — undoing the row would free the address for someone else to claim between the two writes and leave the visitor with nothing, whereas an unverified account they can resend from is recoverable; the route returns `emailSent` and the form routes to `?status=send-failed` accordingly. `/register` now lands on `/verify?status=sent&email=…` instead of `/sign-in?registered=1`, so the **`registered` prop and its notice were deleted** from `sign-in/page.tsx` and `sign-in-form.tsx` as dead. New env vars `RESEND_FROM` and `APP_URL` (both in `.env` and `.env.example`); `appOrigin()` in `src/lib/app-url.ts` prefers `APP_URL` and falls back to the request origin, since the link is built in a route handler where nothing rewrites the URL only if nothing is proxying. **`onboarding@resend.dev` only delivers to the Resend account owner** (`jeroenkleuters@gmail.com`) — Resend says so verbatim in its rejection, and registering any other real address creates the account and silently fails to mail it. This surfaced only because the SDK's error object **logs as `{}`**; destructuring `error.name` / `error.message` into the message is what made it readable, and is worth remembering for any Resend call. `delivered@resend.dev` is accepted regardless and is the address the happy path was tested with. **Verified end to end** against the Neon dev branch: registration → `?status=sent`; signing in unverified shows "Verify your email…" while a *wrong* password on the same account still shows the generic "Incorrect email or password"; the link sets `emailVerified` and consumes the token (`live_tokens` 0); replaying it gives `invalid`; a real 43-char token gives `expired` (tested by briefly negating `TOKEN_TTL_MS`); three resends collapse to one live token and none is created for unknown or verified addresses; the 429 fires at the limit; the resend form renders both the success and 429 responses; demo signs in to its 18 items / 5 collections; and the GitHub account (`emailVerified: null`, no password) is unaffected, since the check lives only in the credentials provider. Two temporary instrumentations — a link log and the negative TTL — were reverted, and `grep` confirms no `TEMP` or `console.log` in `src/`. Note the in-memory limiter's state survives HMR unless the module itself is edited, which is what a trailing-newline touch failed to do. **A one-off `UPDATE` backfilled `emailVerified` for the pre-existing credentials accounts** (scoped `WHERE "emailVerified" IS NULL AND "passwordHash" IS NOT NULL`), which turned out to be **7 rows, not the 6** stated when confirming it — `test2@test.nl` was missed in the count; the predicate was right, only the number was wrong. The GitHub-only row was deliberately left unverified, since the credentials check never applies to it. **`scripts/prune-users.ts` / `npm run db:prune`** was added alongside: it deletes every user except `demo@devstash.io` and everything they own, **dry run by default** with `--yes` to execute. Two things the schema forces and which are most of why it is not a one-liner — `VerificationToken` has **no relation to `User` at all** (it is keyed on the email *string*), so its rows never cascade and are deleted explicitly first, while system item types survive precisely because `userId` is null, which the run asserts afterwards. It also refuses when `NODE_ENV=production` and refuses if the demo account is missing, since an un-seeded database would otherwise mean "delete every user". Run with `--yes`: 9 users and 4 tokens gone, leaving 1 user with 18 items / 5 collections / 18 links and all 7 system types — **including the owner's own GitHub account**, so that login must be re-established through OAuth. **`npm run db:prune -- --yes` silently no-ops under PowerShell**, which eats the `--` separator so npm never forwards the flag and the script reads it as a dry run; run it through the Bash tool (or use PowerShell's `--%`). **Left open:** still **no rate limiting** on `/api/auth/register` or sign-in; expired tokens linger until the address is re-requested or the link clicked (no sweep); a verified sending domain is required before anyone but the Resend account owner can actually register; account linking is untouched; and `/profile` still 404s. No unit tests — still no test runner, and the testable surface is one `authorize` branch, two route handlers and a form handler, all exercised over HTTP and through the running app instead.
- **2026-08-18 — Email verification toggle.** One switch turns the whole verification requirement off, so registration works while Resend has no verified sending domain and `onboarding@resend.dev` can only deliver to the account owner. One new file (`src/lib/feature-flags.ts`), six edited, **no migration** and no new dependency. **`isEmailVerificationEnabled()`** reads `EMAIL_VERIFICATION_ENABLED` and is the single source of truth; every consumer is server-side (register route, `authorize`, the resend route, the `/verify` page), so nothing needed `NEXT_PUBLIC_`. It **defaults to on** — off only for `false` / `0` / `off` / `no` — so an incomplete environment keeps the requirement instead of silently dropping it; defaulting off during development was rejected for exactly that reason. The value is read **per call rather than at module load**, so it is the running environment's and not the build's. `.env` has it `false`; `.env.example` documents it with the Resend constraint that motivates it. **New accounts are stored with `emailVerified` set when the flag is off**, which is the whole reason flipping it back on later does not strand them — proven by registering with the flag off, flipping it on, and signing in successfully with no backfill. **The flag never reaches the browser:** the register route returns `verificationRequired` on its response and `register-form.tsx` branches on that, routing to `/sign-in` instead of `/verify?status=sent`; an absent field falls through to the old path, which is the safe reading. **The `emailVerified` gate in `authorize` is gated but not moved** — it still sits after the bcrypt comparison, so both settings cost exactly one bcrypt and the ~235 ms timing oracle that `ABSENT_PASSWORD_HASH` closed stays closed. The resend endpoint returns its usual generic 200 **after** schema validation (so a malformed body still 400s identically either way) without touching Prisma or Resend, which also means a missing `RESEND_API_KEY` cannot break anything while the flag is off, since `getResend()` is never reached. **`/verify` redirects to `/sign-in`** when off: every state it reports resolves to "just sign in", and its resend form would otherwise promise a link that never comes — the trade-off is that clicking an old, still-valid link performs the verification but swallows the "Email verified" confirmation. `SIGN_IN_PATH` comes from `@/auth.config` (the page is server-side, so importing it costs the client nothing); the register form keeps a `/sign-in` literal rather than pulling provider code into the bundle. **Verified against the Neon dev branch, both settings.** Off: register returns `verificationRequired: false` / `emailSent: false`, the row lands with `emailVerified` set and **0 tokens issued**, sign-in reaches `/dashboard`, `/verify` 307s to `/sign-in`, resend returns the generic 200 while a bad address still 400s, and an account left unverified from before signs in fine. On: register mails and returns `verificationRequired: true`, the unverified account is rejected with `code=email_unverified`, `/verify` renders again, and a wrong password still returns the generic `code=credentials` in both settings. Browser: register → `/sign-in` → sign in → dashboard, with no verify page anywhere. **Two environment notes.** Next 16 **refuses to start a second dev server in the same directory** (it exits 1 and points at the running PID), so the existing server was used — its HMR picked up both the code and the `.env` change without a restart, which is what made flipping the flag mid-session viable. And **`next start` cannot be used as a scratch server here**: production mode loads `.env.production`, which points at the **production** Neon branch, so the trick used in earlier features would have written test registrations there. **`npm run db:status` could not be run** — the direct (non-pooler) Neon host is unreachable from this shell, while the pooled host the app uses works fine; the feature changes no schema and `prisma/schema.prisma` is untouched, so there is nothing to be out of sync, but the check itself is unverified. **Left open:** testing added `flagoff@test.com`, `flagoff2@test.com` and `delivered@resend.dev` (the last unverified, with a live token) to the dev branch; `npm run db:prune` clears them but also deletes the owner's GitHub account. With the flag off, registration lands on a bare `/sign-in` with no "account created" notice — the `registered` prop deleted in the previous feature would be the thing to bring back if that reads too abruptly. Everything the previous entry left open stays open: no rate limiting on register or sign-in, no sweep for expired tokens, no account linking, `/profile` still 404s, and a verified sending domain is still what actually fixes the underlying problem. No unit tests — still no test runner, and the testable surface is one predicate plus four call sites, all exercised over HTTP and in the browser.
- **2026-08-18 — Forgot password (emailed reset links).** An account whose password is lost can get back in. Eight new files, eight edited, **no migration** and no new dependency — reset tokens reuse the `VerificationToken` model that has been in the schema since `20260814120000_init`. **`src/lib/password-reset.ts`** mirrors `email-verification.ts`: 32 random bytes as base64url travel in the link, only their **SHA-256 digest** is stored, issuing first drops any earlier token for the address, and the TTL is **1 hour** rather than verification's 24 — a live reset token takes over an account, where a verification token only confirms one that already belongs to whoever clicked. **The two flows share one table and are told apart by a namespaced identifier**, `password-reset:<email>`: `VerificationToken` has no column saying what a token is for, no relation to `User`, and *both* flows `deleteMany` by identifier before writing, so without the prefix a reset request would silently destroy a pending verification link and vice versa. That needed a guard on **both** sides, since either endpoint can find either row by digest — `isPasswordResetIdentifier` is exported from the reset module and consumed by `consumeVerificationToken`, so a reset link clicked against `/api/auth/verify` reports `invalid` and is **left in place** rather than thrown away; the reverse guard is `emailFrom()` returning null. Proven live: a reset request left the pending verification token untouched, and hitting the verify endpoint with a reset token consumed nothing. **The mutation/render split differs from verification**, because the emailed link must land on a *form*: `/reset-password` only **checks** the token (`checkPasswordResetToken`, read-only), so a render, prefetch or reload throws nothing away, and `POST /api/auth/reset-password` spends it on submit. **`deleteMany` guarded on its `count`, not `delete`** — a `/feature review` pass caught that Prisma's `delete` throws P2025 when the row is already gone, so two submissions racing between the read and the delete would answer 500 instead of "link already used"; the count is now what makes single-use hold, and two concurrent posts of one token return 200 and 400. **A successful reset also sets `emailVerified`** (`user.emailVerified ?? new Date()`): clicking a link sent to the address proves exactly what verification asks for, and without it a reset would succeed while sign-in still refused — confirmed by resetting an unverified account and watching the column flip. **Deliberately not gated on `EMAIL_VERIFICATION_ENABLED`** — verification is an optional requirement, but there is no reset without mail, so that flag being `false` no longer implies the app sends no email. **`POST /api/auth/forgot-password`** answers with one generic 200 for unknown, OAuth-only and registered addresses alike (an OAuth-only account gets no mail — handing it a link would let it set a password, which is account linking), behind the existing in-memory limiter at 3/email and 10/IP per 15 min with `Retry-After`; the reset endpoint takes 20/IP, sized against the bcrypt each accepted call costs rather than against guessing a 32-byte token. `callerKey()` moved out of the resend route into `src/lib/rate-limit.ts` so both share it instead of a third copy appearing. **Pages live in the existing `(auth)` group.** `/forgot-password` bounces a signed-in visitor, but **`/reset-password` deliberately does not** — a deviation from the loaded goal, because someone with a session on this device may well be resetting the password they could not remember on another; the trade-off is that resetting while signed in lands on `/sign-in?reset=1`, which bounces to the dashboard and swallows the confirmation. That notice re-adds the prop pattern deleted when `registered` went, and is gated on `!error` so "Password updated" never sits above "Incorrect email or password". `.auth-field-header` / `.auth-field-link` in `globals.css` put the "Forgot password?" link opposite the Password label. The review also replaced a duplicated `"expired" | "invalid"` literal in `ResetLinkProblem` with `Exclude<PasswordResetState, "valid">`, so adding a state can no longer compile with missing copy. **Verified end to end against the Neon dev branch:** unknown vs. registered addresses return byte-identical responses; missing, garbage, expired (TTL briefly negated) and replayed tokens each rejected with the right message on both the page and the endpoint; issuing link B kills link A (400 vs 200, tested directly rather than inferred); the new hash is `$2b$12$`, the account signs in with it immediately and the superseded password is refused; 429 fires on the 4th request for one address with `Retry-After: 660` while a different address passes; and the whole browser loop — sign-in → forgot → emailed link → new password → `?reset=1` notice → dashboard — completes, with the client-side mismatch check firing without spending the token. Demo still signs in to its 18 items / 5 collections. **Two temporary instrumentations were reverted** (a file capture of the link, since only the digest is stored and the raw token is otherwise unrecoverable, and the negated TTL); `grep` confirms no `TEMP` or `console.log` in `src/`. **`npm run db:status` still cannot run** — the direct (non-pooler) Neon host is unreachable from this shell, as in the previous feature; `prisma/schema.prisma` is untouched, so there is nothing to be out of sync, but the check itself is unverified. **Left open:** `consumeVerificationToken` still uses `delete` and keeps the same latent race, which now makes it inconsistent with its sibling — worth fixing together; `escapeHtml` / `greeting` are duplicated across the two mail modules (left, since the messages will diverge and the spec asked for a sibling module); **the forgot endpoint leaks registration by timing** (~1.3 s registered vs ~0.44 s unknown, because the send is inline) — not new, `resend-verification` has the identical property and `/api/auth/register` leaks it outright via its 409; the OAuth-only branch and the reverse cross-flow direction are verified by inspection only, since no `passwordHash: null` row remains in the dev branch and a raw verification token cannot be recovered from its digest. Everything the previous entries left open stays open: still no rate limiting on register or sign-in, no sweep for expired tokens, no account linking, `/profile` still 404s, and **a verified Resend sending domain is still what actually makes this usable** — `onboarding@resend.dev` only delivers to the account owner, so `delivered@resend.dev` is what the happy path was tested with. Testing changed the passwords of three pre-existing test accounts (`delivered@resend.dev`, `flagoff@test.com`, `flagoff2@test.com`), and a live reset token for the owner's own address — created outside this session — was deleted on request. No unit tests: still no test runner, and the testable surface is one module plus two route handlers and two form handlers, all exercised over HTTP and in the browser instead.
- **2026-08-19 — Profile page.** `/profile` exists, so the sidebar's user-name link stops 404ing — the last thing auth phase 3 left dangling. Ten new files, nine edited, **no migration** and **no new dependency**; `npm run db:status` reported "up to date" (the direct Neon host was reachable again this session, unlike the previous two features). The page shows the account — avatar, name, email, join date — its item and collection totals, a per-type breakdown across all seven system types, and the two account actions. **Protected twice:** `/profile` and `/profile/:path*` joined the proxy matcher, *and* the page redirects to `SIGN_IN_PATH` when `getCurrentUser()` returns null, which is the one case the proxy cannot see — a JWT that still verifies against a row that is gone, i.e. exactly what deleting an account leaves behind if the sign-out after it ever failed. **The dashboard shell was extracted, not duplicated:** `/profile` needs the same frame, so `SidebarProvider` + the `SidebarWithData` boundary + top bar + `main` moved into `src/components/layout/app-shell.tsx`, and `app/dashboard/layout.tsx` and the new `app/profile/layout.tsx` are each three lines around `<AppShell>` (both still `force-dynamic`). **Deliberately not the `(app)` route group** project overview §7 targets: that is a route *move* touching typed-route generics, and ai-interaction.md says ask before architectural changes — worth doing as its own change, and it would absorb both layouts when it happens. **`CurrentUser` gained `createdAt` and `hasPassword`**, the latter derived from `passwordHash` inside `src/lib/db/user.ts` so the hash never leaves the module and the only question anything asks — "is changing it even an option" — is the only thing exposed. Usage reuses `getItemStats`, `getCollectionStats` and `getItemTypesWithCounts` (the same query the sidebar already runs, so the breakdown needed no second grouped query), and since `getCurrentUser` / `getCollections` are `cache()`d the page adds no queries the shell was not already making; the counts sit behind their own `<Suspense>` with a skeleton reusing the real classes, while the identity card paints from the user query the page already awaited. **Change password is a server action, not a route handler** — coding-standards puts authenticated form mutations on actions, and the pre-auth flows only used routes because they had no session to read. It asks for the *current* password (holding the session is not proof of knowing it), shares `passwordSchema` and its 72-byte cap through a new `changePasswordSchema`, hashes at 12 rounds, and is rate-limited at 10 attempts / 15 min **keyed on the user id** rather than the IP, since it is the account under attack and the caller is already known. No client-side copy of the rules: every rejection here needs the server anyway, so one round trip reports the byte cap and the mismatch along with the wrong-password case. The form renders only when `hasPassword`; a GitHub-only account gets an explanatory panel instead. **Delete account** uses the ShadCN `alert-dialog` primitive (`src/components/ui/alert-dialog.tsx`, untouched CLI output — the sixth file in `components/ui/`; **no dependency was added**, since it imports the unified `radix-ui` package the nova preset already installed). The confirm button is a **plain submit `Button`, not `AlertDialogAction`** — Radix's Action closes the dialog on click, which would unmount the form mid-submit and leave a failure nowhere to report itself; `AlertDialogCancel` is unchanged. `src/lib/account.ts` owns both mutations and clears the `VerificationToken` rows **before** the user row, while the address they are keyed on is still readable — they have no relation to `User` so they never cascade, and there are now **two** identifiers per address since resets share the table. `password-reset.ts`'s private `identifierFor` became an exported `passwordResetIdentifier` so the prefix is not written out twice (a first pass had `account.ts` rebuild the literal and assert it through `isPasswordResetIdentifier`, which was strictly worse). Both deletes use `deleteMany` guarded on `count`, matching the fix the reset flow already carries, and the action calls `signOut({ redirectTo: SIGN_IN_PATH })` so no cookie outlives the row. Small shared additions: `formatLongDate` in `src/lib/utils.ts` ("August 14, 2026" — the join date wants a year, unlike item cards), and a `size` prop on `UserAvatar` so the 4rem `.profile-avatar` fetches the OAuth picture at that box instead of stretching a 32px one. **`useActionState` needed its type arguments spelled out** for the delete action: it takes no parameters (there is nothing to submit but the intent), and eslint's `after-used` rule flags a lone unused `_prevState` even though `signInWithCredentials` gets away with the same name in front of a used `formData`. **Verified in the browser against the Neon dev branch:** anonymous `/profile` 307s to `/sign-in?callbackUrl=…%2Fprofile`; demo renders 18 items / 5 collections with a 4/3/5/0/0/0/6 breakdown that matches the sidebar and sums to 18; a wrong current password, a mismatch and a real change each report correctly, and after the real change **the old password is refused while the new one signs in** — the demo password was moved to a temp and **put back to `12345678`**, re-verified by signing in. A throwaway account (`deleteme@test.com`, given a live reset token first) was deleted through the dialog: user row gone, **both** token identifiers gone, the 7 system item types and demo's content untouched, session ended on `/sign-in`. The GitHub account shows the no-password panel and its avatar renders sharp at 4rem. Dashboard still renders 18/5/1/1 after the shell extraction, no console errors or hydration warnings, and no horizontal overflow at 390px. Five verification screenshots were deleted afterwards. **Left open:** the 10-attempt limiter was not exercised live (it is the same in-memory helper the mail endpoints use, and 11 bcrypts of proof was not worth it), and the cascade was not observed against an account that actually *owned* items — no UI creates content for a throwaway account yet, so that rests on the schema's `onDelete: Cascade` and the `db:prune` precedent. Everything the previous entries left open stays open: no rate limiting on register or sign-in, no sweep for expired tokens, no account linking, and a verified Resend sending domain is still what makes the mail flows usable. No unit tests — still no test runner, and the testable surface is one module plus two action closures and two form handlers, all exercised through the running app instead. A follow-up spec (@context/features/improvements-profile-page.md) landed while this was being built and is **not** part of it: it moves both account actions into modal dialogs behind `Change Password` / `Delete Account` buttons under an "Account Information" header. Spec: @context/features/profile-spec.md
- **2026-08-19 — Profile page improvements: account actions in dialogs.** The follow-up spec the previous entry flagged. Three new files, six edited, **no migration** and **no new dependency**. The account panel now carries an **"Account Information"** header, the identity block, `Email:` / `Member since:` as labelled rows with icons, then a hairline and the two actions as buttons — `Change Password` (`KeyRound`, outline) and `Delete Account` (`Trash2`, destructive). The standalone panels are gone, and with them `.profile-panels` / `.profile-panel*` in `globals.css`. **Change password moved into the ShadCN `dialog` primitive** (`src/components/ui/dialog.tsx`, untouched CLI output — the seventh file in `components/ui/`; **no dependency added**, it imports the `radix-ui` package the nova preset already installed). Radix **unmounts `DialogContent` on close**, which is the whole reason `ChangePasswordDialog` needs no `open` state: the form remounts on every open, so a "Password updated." from a previous change cannot linger — confirmed live by reopening and finding neither `.auth-notice` nor `.auth-error`. `ChangePasswordForm` kept its `useActionState` and lost only its panel chrome. Delete account is unchanged internally (same `AlertDialog`, same plain-submit-instead-of-`AlertDialogAction` reasoning); only its trigger moved into the button row, so `delete-account.tsx` became `delete-account-dialog.tsx` for symmetry. **Both dialog wrappers are client components.** The change-password one uses no hooks and a first pass left it a server component — reverted on review: `asChild` clones the `Button` rather than rendering it, and while a server-rendered element probably survives Slot's `cloneElement`, it is unproven here and the delete sibling already proves the client version. `profile-identity.tsx` became **`profile-account.tsx`** (`git mv`, so history follows) since it now owns the actions too. **A GitHub-only account still gets no Change Password button**, but its explanation is a muted line in the button row rather than its own panel, and the identity block names the account kind — `hasPassword ? "Email account" : "GitHub account"`, accurate only while account linking does not exist, which is recorded in a comment. **Playwright compared the result against the reference screenshot** (@context/screenshots/profile-page-ui.png) and found four deltas; three were closed on request, one deliberately not. Closed: the usage block became a second `.profile-card` titled **"Usage Statistics"** with **"Total Items"** and a sentence-case **"Items by Type"** subheading (was uppercase with tracking) — the skeleton fallback got the same structure so the card is not conjured only when the counts land, and `.profile-usage-totals` took a `1.5rem` top margin to sit under its title as the identity block does; and the page subtitle became "Manage your account settings". **Not closed:** the reference's solid-red delete button — ours keeps the nova preset's `destructive` variant (translucent, red text) rather than overriding it. The type tiles keep their colour-coded left borders, which the reference lacks, since that is the app-wide `data-type` mechanism. One CSS addition worth knowing: `.auth-form` / `.auth-error` / `.auth-notice` carry standalone top margins for the auth pages, which doubled up against the dialog's own `gap-4` grid, so `[data-slot="dialog-content"]`- and `[data-slot="alert-dialog-content"]`-scoped rules zero them. **Verified in the browser against the Neon dev branch, end to end on a throwaway account** (`dialogtest@test.com`, registered with verification off): a wrong current password reports "That is not your current password." inside the dialog, the real change reports "Password updated.", the **old password is then refused at sign-in while the new one reaches `/dashboard`**, and the account was deleted through the Delete Account dialog — session ended on `/sign-in`, and SQL confirms 0 user rows, 0 token rows under either identifier, the 7 system item types intact and demo's 18 items / 5 collections untouched. Demo was used only to view the page and open both dialogs without submitting, so its password is still `12345678`. No console errors; the one warning is Next's font-preload notice, which predates this work. Seven verification screenshots were deleted afterwards. **Left open:** everything the previous entries list stays open — no rate limiting on register or sign-in, no sweep for expired tokens, no account linking, the `(app)` route group still not adopted, and a verified Resend sending domain still what makes the mail flows usable. No unit tests: still no test runner, and this feature added no logic to test — it moved presentation, leaving the two actions, their validation, the 10-attempt limiter and the delete cascade byte-identical. Spec: @context/features/improvements-profile-page.md
- **2026-08-19 — Auth audit fixes: the High and Medium findings.** A `auth-auditor` pass (@docs/audit-results/AUTH_SECURITY_REVIEW.md — 0 Critical, 2 High, 3 Medium, 2 Low) produced the first five; the two Low ones are deliberately left. One new file, twenty edited, **no migration** and **no new dependency**. **[HIGH] Sign-in is rate limited** — nothing throttled `POST /api/auth/callback/credentials`, so bcrypt's ~220ms was the only friction against a botnet running attempts in parallel. `authorize` now counts 10 per address and 30 per caller per 15 minutes through the existing in-memory limiter (`authorize(credentials, request)` — the second argument is the original `Request`, which is where `callerKey` gets its header) and throws a `TooManyAttemptsError extends CredentialsSignin` carrying `code = "too_many_attempts"`, which `credentialsMessage()` in `src/actions/auth.ts` maps to its own message. **The check sits ahead of the bcrypt comparison rather than levelled with it**, which is the opposite of what `ABSENT_PASSWORD_HASH` and the `emailVerified` gate do, and deliberately: being throttled is what the response says outright, and the window is keyed on the submitted address so it fills at the same rate whether or not an account is behind it — the fast path depends only on the caller's own request count, never on whether the address exists. Verified live: attempts 1–10 return `code=credentials` at ~350ms, the 11th and 12th return `code=too_many_attempts` in 7ms, and a different address still signs in. **[HIGH] Registration is rate limited** at 5 per caller and 3 per address, mirroring `forgot-password`'s shape (429 + `Retry-After`). Verified at the exact boundary rather than by inference — five accounts landed in the dev branch and the 6th, 7th and 8th calls were all refused with `Retry-After: 660`, confirmed by listing the `User` rows afterwards. **[MEDIUM] `APP_URL` is required in production.** `appOrigin()` fell back to `new URL(request.url).origin`, which Next derives from the `Host` header, so an unconfigured deployment let a `POST /api/auth/forgot-password` carrying a spoofed `Host` mail the *real* owner of an address a link pointing at the attacker's domain. It now throws when `NODE_ENV === "production"` and the variable is unset; the fallback stays for local work and previews. Exercised all three branches through `tsx` (the module has no imports, so importing it touches nothing): configured → the configured value, dev-unset → `http://evil.example.com`, production-unset → throws. **[MEDIUM] A password change now invalidates every session opened with the old one.** Sessions are JWTs with Auth.js's 30-day default and nothing tied them to the password, so a stolen cookie outlived the change meant to lock it out. Solved **without a migration**: `src/lib/password-fingerprint.ts` takes a truncated SHA-256 of the stored `passwordHash`, `authorize` returns it as `pwf` on the user, the `jwt`/`session` callbacks carry it across, and `getCurrentUser` compares it against the row — the one place the check is free, since that row is already being read. A `passwordChangedAt` column would have said the same and cost a migration; `User.updatedAt` was rejected because any future profile edit would then sign everyone out. Both sides are null for a GitHub account, so it always matches; a token minted before this carries none and is rejected, which is one sign-out and the fail-closed direction. **Verified as the actual attacker scenario, not by inspection**: a live session cookie was pulled out of the browser context, confirmed to return 200 on `/dashboard`, the password was then changed, and the *same* cookie replayed — `/profile` 307s to `/sign-in` and a browser replay of `/dashboard` lands on `/sign-in`. **The consequence is that changing a password signs out the session that changed it**, so `changePassword` ends with `signOut({ redirectTo: "/sign-in?reset=1" })` rather than reporting success into a dialog that is about to stop being authorised — reusing the "Password updated. Sign in with your new one." notice the reset flow already put there — and `ChangePasswordState` lost its now-unreachable `success` field along with the form's notice. Auth.js's `unstable_update` would have kept the current session alive and was rejected on the "unstable" prefix. **Two knock-on changes the fingerprint forced.** `SidebarWithData` redirects to `SIGN_IN_PATH` when `getCurrentUser` returns null, since a rejected session would otherwise render an empty shell (`NO_COLLECTIONS` and the null-user branch are gone with it); and the three `(auth)` pages ask `getCurrentUser()` instead of `auth()` before bouncing a signed-in visitor — **without that the redirect is an infinite loop**, because `auth()` still considers a fingerprint-rejected token valid and would bounce it straight back to the page that just turned it away. Note `/dashboard` answers **200 rather than 307** for a rejected session: the redirect happens inside the sidebar's Suspense boundary after the shell has flushed, so Next delivers it client-side. No data is served either way — every section short-circuits on a null user id — and making it a real 307 would mean awaiting the user in the layout, undoing the streaming split the skeleton feature deliberately introduced. **[MEDIUM] The timing oracle on `forgot-password` and `resend-verification` is closed.** Both already answered with one generic body, but only the branch that sends awaited Resend, so response time separated registered addresses from unknown ones (and, on resend, verified from unverified). The send moved into **`after()` from `next/server`** — the callback form, so it runs strictly after the response — with the origin captured before the response rather than read from a request already on its way out. Nothing reads the result: a failure is logged inside the issuing function, and the caller was never told whether mail went out. Measured registered vs unknown at **0.112s / 0.114s and 0.119s / 0.124s** against the audit's ~1.3s / ~0.44s, with byte-identical bodies; the server log shows the Resend rejection arriving *after* the 200, and the reset tokens exist in the dev branch for exactly the two addresses that have passwords, so deferring the send did not skip the work. **Left open:** both Low findings — `consumeVerificationToken` still uses `delete` rather than `deleteMany` guarded on `count` (a P2025 → 500 under concurrent redemption, and now inconsistent with its reset-flow sibling), and `callerKey` still trusts the first `X-Forwarded-For` entry, which matters more now that sign-in and register depend on it. The limiter remains per-process and resets on restart, so it is still a brake rather than a quota — Redis is what the two new High fixes actually want. Testing left `audit@test.com` and `rl2`–`rl5@test.com` in the dev branch plus reset tokens for `audit@test.com` and `demo@devstash.io`; `npm run db:prune` clears them but also deletes the owner's GitHub account. Everything the previous entries list stays open: no sweep for expired tokens, no account linking, the `(app)` route group still not adopted, and a verified Resend sending domain still what makes the mail flows usable. No unit tests — still no test runner, and the whole surface is one predicate, two limiter call sites, one fingerprint comparison and two deferred sends, all exercised over HTTP and in the browser instead. Audit: @docs/audit-results/AUTH_SECURITY_REVIEW.md
- **2026-08-19 — Auth rate limiting on Upstash Redis.** The in-process counter that had guarded the auth endpoints since the email-verification feature became a shared one. Three new files (`src/lib/rate-limit-toast.ts`, `src/components/ui/sonner.tsx`, the spec), nineteen edited, **no migration** — `db:status` reported "up to date" and `prisma/schema.prisma` is untouched. Installed `@upstash/ratelimit` (^2.0.8) and `@upstash/redis` (^1.38.2); the four `npm audit` highs are pre-existing (`nanoid` via Next/Tailwind's postcss, `deepmerge-ts` via `@prisma/config`) and none came from Upstash. **The value is not new coverage** — all five endpoints were already limited — but that the old counter kept a separate tally per process and reset whenever the process did, so it was a brake on casual abuse rather than a limit; verified by watching `devstash:rl:*` keys appear in Redis. `rateLimit(key, limit, windowMs)` keeps its signature and becomes async, returning the spec's `{ success, remaining, reset }` (`remaining` has no consumer yet; it is in the shape because the spec asks for it). **Sliding rather than fixed window**, because a fixed one lets twice the limit through when the requests fall either side of one boundary. One `Ratelimit` instance per distinct `limit:windowMs` policy is memoised for the life of the process, which is what makes its own in-memory cache of already-rejected identifiers useful — and that cache is worth knowing about when testing: **a blocked identifier stays blocked until its window rolls, so deleting the key out of Redis does not unblock it**, only waiting or a restart does. That cost real time mid-verification, and is now a comment in the module. **Limits follow the spec's table with the per-email dimension kept** where the live code had it, a decision taken before implementation: the spec's "Key By: IP" on register and forgot-password would have let one address be mail-bombed or probed from many callers, which the code already prevented. Where the spec keys a window on "IP + email" together (sign-in, resend), its number is applied to **the address alone**, which is strictly tighter — 5 per address is a smaller budget than 5 per (address, IP) pair, since every IP attacking one account then draws from one pool. Final table: sign-in 5/15m per email + 30/15m per IP; register 3/1h + 3/1h; forgot-password 3/1h + 3/1h; reset-password 5/15m per IP; resend 3/15m per email + 10/15m per IP; and the sixth call site the spec's table omits, `changePassword`, unchanged at 10/15m keyed on the user id — it shares the utility, so leaving it behind would have meant two backends. Note **register at 3/hour per IP is the spec's number and the one most likely to draw a complaint**: the fourth person behind a shared NAT cannot sign up for an hour. **Fails open on all three paths** — missing configuration, a bad token, and an unreachable host — because a limiter that refuses sign-ins while Redis is down is worse than one that lets a burst through; `timeout: 2000` lets a slow check through rather than holding a sign-in open, and the `try`/`catch` matters most in `authorize`, where a throw is how the provider signals rejection and an outage must not read as a failed password. Exercised all three through `tsx` against a rebuilt module: unconfigured allowed in 1ms with one logged error, a bad token allowed in 196ms, an unreachable host allowed at the 2007ms timeout, and the configured control returned a real reset in 33ms. **`tooManyAttemptsResponse()` replaced four hand-rolled 429 blocks**; `retryAfterSeconds()` deliberately **filters to the windows that actually failed**, so an endpoint checking two does not quote the untouched one's reset and overstate the wait. One honest limitation recorded in the code: **`reset` is when the limiter's window rolls, not when the budget is certainly back** — a sliding window keeps weighting requests across that boundary, so someone well over the limit can still be refused a little after the time reported, which is the usual reading of `Retry-After` as an earliest-worth-trying rather than a promise. **The toast is for the 429 alone**, the other decision taken up front: every other auth failure keeps its inline `.auth-error` slot, because a rate limit is the one rejection that is not about what was typed — no field to correct, and the form is left exactly as it was. Added the ShadCN `sonner` primitive (the eighth file in `components/ui/`) mounted once in the root layout. **It is the only generated `ui/` file that has been edited**: the CLI's version reads the theme from `next-themes`, which this app has no provider for — the root layout hardcodes `.dark` — so the hook would have fallen through to `"system"` and lit the toast up on a dark page; pinned to `theme="dark"` with a comment, and `next-themes` uninstalled rather than left as an unused dependency. **Sign-in needed a different route to the same toast**, because its limit never becomes an HTTP 429: Auth.js reports every credentials failure as one error type, so `TooManyAttemptsError`'s `code` is all that distinguishes it. `credentialsMessage()` became `credentialsFailure()` returning `{ error, rateLimited }`, `SignInState` gained `rateLimited`, and the form toasts on it through a `useEffect` keyed on the state object — which is safe precisely because the action returns a fresh literal each time it settles. The client/server split is clean and deliberate: `rate-limit.ts` imports `next/server` and is reached only from server code, `rate-limit-toast.ts` only from `"use client"` components, so nothing pulls the server module into a browser bundle. **Verified live against the real Upstash instance and in the browser.** forgot-password answered 200/200/200/**429** with `Retry-After: 918`, and both keys were confirmed present in Redis; **the two dimensions count independently** — a fresh IP with a spent address is still refused, which is exactly the protection the spec's IP-only table would have dropped, and is the single result that most justifies the decision. Sign-in capped at 5 with the 6th returning `code=too_many_attempts`; register and reset-password capped correctly, and **rejected attempts count** (a 409 on register, a 400 on reset), which is the point since the 409 is the enumeration vector. Browser: a rate-limited sign-in raised the toast with no inline error and the email preserved — with the **correct** password, proving the check runs ahead of authentication; forgot-password toasted "Too many attempts. Please try again in 10 minutes." with the form untouched; a wrong password still rendered inline with **no** toast; and demo signed in to its 18 items / 5 collections with no console errors. **Left open, and worth a decision:** an unconfigured production deploy silently has no auth rate limiting at all — either env var missing means every check fails open, so the two High findings the audit closed quietly reopen with only one log line as evidence. The spec's "fail open" is about an *outage*, which is transient, whereas a missing variable is permanent, and `appOrigin()` in `src/lib/app-url.ts` already sets the house precedent of throwing in production for exactly that distinction; matching it was recommended at review and deliberately not done here, since the spec reads the other way. Also open: `callerKey` still trusts the first `x-forwarded-for` entry (the remaining Low finding from @docs/audit-results/AUTH_SECURITY_REVIEW.md) — left alone because the spec names that header and changing it on uncertain platform behaviour risks making it worse; the resend-verification 429 was **not** exercised live, since `EMAIL_VERIFICATION_ENABLED=false` short-circuits ahead of the limiter, though its path is identical to forgot-password's; and `consumeVerificationToken` still uses `delete` rather than a guarded `deleteMany`. Testing left `ratelimit@test.com` in the dev branch (`npm run db:prune` clears it, but also deletes the owner's GitHub account) and some short-lived counters in Redis, which expire on their own. Everything the previous entries list stays open: no sweep for expired tokens, no account linking, the `(app)` route group still not adopted, and a verified Resend sending domain still what makes the mail flows usable. No unit tests — still no test runner, and the surface is one module plus six call sites, all exercised over HTTP, through `tsx`, and in the browser instead. Spec: @context/features/rate-limiting-spec.md
- **2026-08-20 — Items list view.** `/items/[type]` exists, so the seven Types links the sidebar has carried since phase 2 of the dashboard UI stop 404ing. Four new files, six edited, **no migration** and **no new dependency**; `npm run db:status` reported "Database schema is up to date!" (the direct Neon host was reachable this session). The page renders the signed-in user's items of one type as a two-column grid of the existing `ItemCard`, which already color-codes its left border from `data-type={item.type.slug}` — so the spec's border requirement needed no code at all. **`getItemTypeBySlug` in `src/lib/db/item-types.ts`** resolves the URL segment and is `cache()`d, since the page and its `generateMetadata` both ask for it and would otherwise each run the query; that module now imports `@/lib/prisma` for the first time, which is safe only because every one of its importers is server-side (`lib/db/items.ts`, `lib/db/collections.ts`, the page) — a client component pulling `ItemTypeSummary` from it with a value import would drag Prisma into a browser bundle. **`getItemsByType(userId, itemTypeId)`** takes the id rather than the slug, so the page's type lookup is not repeated as a join; it orders `isPinned desc, updatedAt desc`, which is the dashboard's two sections collapsed into one list. **An unknown slug 404s** through `notFound()` rather than rendering an empty list — decided up front, on the grounds that `/items/banana` is a wrong URL and an empty list would present it as a real but empty category; `/items/snippet` (singular) 404s for the same reason, since slugs are plural by seed convention. **The heading comes from the slug, not `ItemType.name`.** The name is singular ("Snippet") and the schema carries no plural column, so a local `typeLabel` title-cases the slug — splitting on `-` first, so a future `code-snippets` reads "Code Snippets" rather than "Code-snippets". Note this makes the page title ("Snippets") disagree with the sidebar link that reaches it ("Snippet"); the sidebar was left alone. **`.item-list` was widened directly rather than given a variant prop**, which was the other decision taken before implementing: its only other consumers are the dashboard's Pinned and Recent sections, so **the dashboard is now two-column too** — intended, and it means `/dashboard` no longer matches @context/screenshots/dashboard-ui-main.png in that respect. The rule is a `grid` with `repeat(2, 1fr)` joining `.collection-grid` in the existing `max-width: 47.99rem` block, which is the app's md breakpoint and matches the spec's "medium and up" while keeping the house desktop-first shape. **`ItemListSkeleton` moved out of `dashboard-skeletons.tsx`** into `src/components/items/item-list-skeleton.tsx` now that it has a second consumer — it reuses the real card's classes, so it inherits the column count for free. The page follows `/profile`'s shape: `await` the type for the heading, stream the list behind its own `<Suspense>`, `force-dynamic`, plus the null-`getCurrentUser()` redirect that covers what the proxy cannot (a token that still verifies against a deleted account). `/items` and `/items/:path*` joined the proxy matcher. **A request to rename `page.tsx` to `item.tsx` was declined**: `page` is the reserved basename that makes a folder a route, so the rename would not have renamed the page but deleted it — `pageExtensions` in `next.config.ts` changes the extension only. The offered alternative (all code in `item.tsx`, a three-line `page.tsx` re-exporting it) was turned down and the file stays conventional, matching every other page in the repo. **One environment gotcha cost real time and is worth remembering: never run `npm run build` while `next dev` is live.** They share `.next`, and the build left the dev server's worker corrupted — `/items/snippets` answered 500 with "Jest worker encountered 2 child process exceptions" while `/dashboard` and `/profile`, already compiled, kept working, which reads exactly like a bug in the new route. The code was fine; killing the dev server and restarting it fixed it on the first try. This is a different failure from the two the previous entries record (a second dev server refusing to start, and `next start` loading `.env.production` and therefore the **production** Neon branch), and it is the one most likely to send you hunting a phantom. **Verified in the browser against the Neon dev branch**, signed in as demo: `/items/snippets` renders 4 blue-bordered cards in two columns with the pinned `useDebounce` first, matching the sidebar's count; `/items/links` renders 6 green ones; `/items/notes` (0 items) shows "No notes yet."; `/items/banana` returns a real 404 rather than an empty page; anonymous `/items/snippets` 307s to `/sign-in?callbackUrl=…%2Fitems%2Fsnippets`; the GitHub account, which owns nothing, gets the empty state on every type; 390px is single-column with no horizontal overflow; and the dashboard still shows 18/5/1/1 with Pinned and Recent now two-column. No console errors after the restart. Five verification screenshots were deleted afterwards. **Left open:** the two-column cards wrap titles more than the single-column dashboard did, which was checked at 1400px and 390px but not at every width in between; there is no way to open an item yet (the quick-access drawer project overview §9 calls for does not exist, so a card is not clickable anywhere in the app); the page has no sort, filter or pagination and fetches every item of the type unbounded, which is fine while the free tier caps at 50 items but is the same shape as the deferred `findCollections` finding; and `/collections` still does not exist, so the sidebar's "View all collections" link keeps 404ing. Everything the previous entries list stays open: no sweep for expired tokens, no account linking, the `(app)` route group still not adopted — `src/app/items/layout.tsx` is now the *third* three-line `AppShell` wrapper, which strengthens the case for finally doing that move — and a verified Resend sending domain still what makes the mail flows usable. No unit tests: still no test runner, and the only pure logic added is `typeLabel`, a six-line string transform exercised through the four type pages that render its output. Spec: @context/features/item-list-view-spec.md
- **2026-08-20 — Vitest unit testing.** The repo has a test runner for the first time, ending the "implement unit testing later" line that had been in the workflow since the `context/` docs were written. Ten new files, eight edited, **one dev dependency** (`vitest` ^4.1.11) and **no migration** — nothing about the app changed, only what can be asserted about it. `npm test` (`vitest run`) and `npm run test:watch` join the scripts; **84 tests across 9 files**, with `npm run lint` and `npm run build` clean. **The scope is enforced by configuration, not by convention.** The user's requirement was server actions and utilities, not components, so `include` in `vitest.config.mts` is exactly `["src/lib/**/*.test.ts", "src/actions/**/*.test.ts"]` — a `*.test.ts` anywhere else is never collected. That claim was verified rather than assumed: a deliberately failing test dropped into `src/components/` left the run at 9 files / 84 tests and exit 0. Environment is `node`, since nothing under test renders and there is no DOM to emulate. **Three setup details worth remembering.** (1) The config is **`vitest.config.mts`, not `.ts`** — `package.json` has no `"type": "module"`, so Vite warned that ESM syntax in a config loaded as CommonJS breaks under `configLoader: 'native'`, which becomes the default in a future Vite major; `.mts` matches the existing `postcss.config.mjs` / `eslint.config.mjs`, and `tsconfig.json` already listed `**/*.mts`. (2) **`next-auth` and `@auth/core` must be inlined** via `test.server.deps.inline`: left external they are loaded by Node directly, which does not apply Next's `exports` map, and `next-auth/lib/env.js` fails with `Cannot find module …/node_modules/next/server … Did you mean to import "next/server.js"?`. This was the only real snag and it blocked the one action test while all 72 utility tests already passed.
