(3) The `@/*` alias is duplicated from `tsconfig.json` as a plain `{ "@": <src> }` resolve alias rather than pulling in `vite-tsconfig-paths` — a bare `"@"` key only matches `@/…` (rollup's alias matcher tests `importee === find || importee.startsWith(find + "/")`), so scoped packages like `@upstash/redis` resolve normally. `unstubEnvs` and `restoreMocks` are on, so env stubs and mock implementations cannot leak between tests. **Tests are offline by rule.** `src/lib/prisma.ts` throws at import time when `DATABASE_URL` is unset, which means even importing a `lib/db/` module for a pure function needs `vi.mock("@/lib/prisma", …)` — `src/lib/db/item-types.test.ts` does exactly that to reach `compareItemTypes`, and is worth noting as a small design smell the *previous* feature introduced by adding a Prisma import to a module that had been pure metadata. `@/auth` is mocked wholesale for the action test. Vitest loads no `.env` at all, which is the desired behaviour: `feature-flags.ts` and `app-url.ts` are exercised against `vi.stubEnv` values rather than whatever is on disk. **What was tested was chosen from this history rather than for coverage** — the rules that cost something to learn: the 72-byte bcrypt cap counting **UTF-8 bytes** (18 emoji pass at exactly 72, 19 are refused, and the test asserts the byte count inline so it cannot rot into a tautology); `appOrigin` throwing in production instead of trusting a spoofable `Host`; `retryAfterSeconds` filtering to the windows that actually failed, so an endpoint checking two never quotes the untouched one; the email trim-and-lowercase that keeps registration and sign-in agreeing; `passwordFingerprint` returning null on both sides for a GitHub account; `formatShortDate` pinning UTC so one item card does not show two different days to two readers; and, in `signInWithCredentials`, that a **non-`AuthError` is rethrown** — that is how a *successful* sign-in leaves, and swallowing it would strand the visitor on the form. **The suite was proven non-vacuous**, not just green: swapping the byte cap's `TextEncoder` count for `.length` failed exactly one test — the emoji case, while the ASCII case still passed, which is precisely why the emoji case exists — and the change was reverted (`git diff` on the file empty afterwards). **Docs updated:** `ai-interaction.md` (workflow step 4 now writes tests and runs `npm test`, step 6 waits on them, plus a new Testing section), `CLAUDE.md`, `coding-standards.md` (a Testing section and the test path in File Organization), `project-overview.md` (stack table, folder tree, `src/actions/`) and `README.md`. `.claude/skills/feature/actions/test.md` needed **no** change — it was written ahead of this and already assumed Vitest and `npm test`, which is where both names came from. The README's Status and Project layout paragraphs were also corrected while open: they still claimed authentication was not wired up and that `/items/[type]` did not exist, both untrue for some time. **`npm test` and `npm run db:test` are different things and every doc now says so explicitly** — `db:test` runs `scripts/test-db.ts` against a live database and is not part of the suite. Renaming it to something like `db:verify` would be the real fix and was deliberately not done, since the name is referenced throughout these history entries. **Left open:** coverage is not measured and no threshold is enforced (`@vitest/coverage-v8` is not installed); nothing runs the suite automatically — there is no CI, so `npm test` is a workflow step a human or the agent has to take; the modules with genuinely useful logic still untested are `email-verification.ts` / `password-reset.ts` (token issue-and-consume, and the `password-reset:` identifier namespacing that keeps the two flows from destroying each other's rows), `account.ts`, and `rateLimit` itself, whose interesting behaviour — failing open on a missing config, a bad token and an unreachable host — was exercised through `tsx` in a previous feature and would need a mocked Upstash client to become a test; and the four `npm audit` highs are unchanged and pre-existing. Everything the previous entries list stays open: no sweep for expired tokens, no account linking, the `(app)` route group still not adopted, `/collections` still 404ing, and a verified Resend sending domain still what makes the mail flows usable.
- **2026-08-20 — Item list column ladder.** `.item-list` went from a flat two columns to `< 1024px` → 1 · `1024–1535px` → 2 · `≥ 1536px` → 3. **One source file changed** (`src/app/globals.css`), no dependency, no migration, and **nothing testable added** — a `/feature test` pass confirmed the diff contains zero TypeScript, so there is no export to import and the only observable behaviour is a computed `grid-template-columns` at a given viewport, which needs a browser rather than a node-environment unit test. **Two decisions were taken at load time, both from questions.** "Wide" is **1536px / 96rem** (Tailwind's 2xl) rather than 1280px, so a 1440px laptop keeps two columns and only a 4K or ultrawide display gets three. And it stays **one shared rule**, so the dashboard's Pinned and Recent lists follow — the same sharing chosen when the list went two-column, which avoids a modifier prop on `ItemList`. **The request as typed was self-contradictory** and worth recording: "three columns for wide screens, keep 2 for >= 1024px and 1 column for >= 768px" reads as overlapping `>=` bands. The intended reading is a descending ladder in Tailwind's breakpoint vocabulary (md/lg/xl), which means **the single-column band widens** — 768–1023px was two columns and becomes one. That is a real change rather than an addition, and it is precisely the fix for the "titles wrap more than the dashboard used to" note the items-list-view feature left open: at 900px every card title and description now fits on one line. **Stated mobile-first, against the file's desktop-first convention**, and deliberately: three steps expressed desktop-first would have scattered across two more `max-width` blocks, whereas `min-width: 64rem` and `min-width: 96rem` sit directly under the base rule so the rungs read as one ladder. A comment in the file records the reasoning. The existing `@media (max-width: 47.99rem)` block **keeps `.collection-grid`** and loses only `.item-list` — the collection grid is `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(17rem, 1fr))` and sizes itself, so it was never in scope. **Verified by reading `getComputedStyle(...).gridTemplateColumns` in the browser rather than by eye**, which is what pinned the boundaries exactly: 390 → 1 (and `scrollWidth === innerWidth`, so no horizontal overflow), 900 → 1, 1023 → 1, 1025 → 2, 1534 → 2, 1537 → 3, 1600 → 3. Root font-size was confirmed to be 16px, so `64rem`/`96rem` really are 1024/1536 — worth checking rather than assuming, since the whole ladder is expressed in rem and would shift under a changed root size. The dashboard was confirmed three-across at 1600px with the Collections grid untouched, and **both media queries were confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS minified it** (`min-width:64rem){.item-list{grid-template-columns:repeat(2,1fr)}}` and the 96rem equivalent) — the same class of check the skeleton feature ran on `1lh`. Three verification screenshots were deleted afterwards. **One false alarm worth remembering:** `/dashboard` threw `useSidebar must be used within SidebarProvider` as an SSR error on first load and was clean on reload. It is not a bug and not from this change — a CSS edit cannot produce a React context error, `AppShell` does wrap `SidebarProvider` correctly, and the cause was the dev server having been started against the `.next` directory a previous `npm run build` had written. That is the **third** distinct way `.next` sharing has produced a phantom failure in this project (after the build-during-dev worker crash and `next start` loading `.env.production`), and the rule that avoids all of them is simply: never run `npm run build` while a dev server is live. This time the server was stopped first, on request, before the build. **Left open:** unchanged from the previous entries — `/collections` still 404s, there is still no way to open or create an item, no CI runs the test suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and a verified Resend sending domain is still what makes the mail flows usable.
- **2026-08-20 — Item drawer.** Clicking an item card opens a right-side sheet with the item's full detail, which closes the "no way to open an item" line every entry since the items list view has carried. There is no item page — this is the detail view. Eight new files, six edited, **no migration** (`db:status` reported "Database schema is up to date!") and **no new dependency**. Added the ShadCN `sheet` primitive (`src/components/ui/sheet.tsx`, untouched CLI output — the **ninth** file in `components/ui/`); like `dialog` and `alert-dialog` before it, it imports the unified `radix-ui` package the nova preset already installed. **Two scope decisions were taken from questions before implementing.** The spec put the code editor and per-type rendering out of scope but said nothing about whether the action bar *acts*: **only Copy does**, because it is the one action that never touches the server, while Favorite, Pin and Delete each need a server action plus a revalidation story (and Delete a confirm dialog), and **Edit renders disabled** because the item form it would open does not exist anywhere in the app. The three inert buttons carry a `Coming soon` title rather than silently doing nothing. **`GET /api/items/[id]` is the first route outside `api/auth/`** and is **deliberately kept out of the proxy's matcher**: the proxy answers an unauthenticated request with a redirect to the sign-in page, which is right for a navigation and useless to a `fetch` — it would arrive as an opaque 200 of HTML — so the check lives in the handler and says 401. **`getItemDetail(userId, itemId)` puts `userId` in the `where` rather than checking the result**, so another account's item reads as *missing* instead of forbidden and an id nobody may see is never confirmed to exist; the 404 branch is commented to say it covers both. It is also the one query in `lib/db/` whose result is serialized to JSON by a route handler instead of crossing the RSC boundary, so **it converts its dates to ISO strings there** — that keeps the returned value and the client's type the same shape on both sides of the wire, and `formatShortDate` / `formatLongDate` already accept either. **`src/types/item.ts` spells the `ContentType` union out rather than importing the Prisma enum**, so the module stays safe for a client component to import; adding a member to the schema widens Prisma's union and stops it assigning to this one, which is the loud failure rather than silent drift. (`ItemSummary` still comes from `@/lib/db/items` through `import type`, which is erased — a *value* import there would drag Prisma into a browser bundle.) **One provider for the whole shell, not one per card.** `ItemDrawerProvider` is mounted in `AppShell`, since the pages rendering the cards are server components and a `Sheet` per row would put a hundred portals on the dashboard to show at most one; `/profile` shares the shell, renders no cards and simply never opens it. It holds a **request counter** so a second click, or a close, invalidates whatever is in flight and a slower earlier response cannot land on top of a later one — and it **keeps `summary` on close**, because the sheet animates out and clearing it would blank the drawer on the way. **The header paints from what the card already knew** (title, type, tags, favorite/pin flags), so only the body waits on the network, which is what the spec's "snappy" asks for; the skeleton therefore covers the description and content only. **No detail cache was added** — reopening refetches. It would be four lines and cannot go stale today since nothing mutates an item, but it becomes wrong the moment Favorite or Pin land, and the spec asked for fetch-on-click. **`ItemCard` became a client component** with an invisible button stretched over it rather than wrapping the card in one, so the card keeps its markup and box model and the skeleton — which reuses the same classes and has no button inside — keeps rendering as a card that cannot be clicked. Hover and focus are stated through `:has(.item-card-open:hover)` on the card for that reason: putting them on the transparent button would tint its own text layer. **The CSS finding worth remembering: the `data-type` colour map had to leave `.dashboard-shell`.** Radix portals the sheet to `body`, outside the shell entirely, so the scoped map left the drawer's type icon grey; the seven selectors are now bare and the `--type-color` default moved to `body`. The sheet's own `sm:max-w-sm` cap is beaten by `.item-drawer { max-width: 44rem }` **without `!important`**, because our rules are unlayered while Tailwind's utilities sit in the `utilities` layer — the same mechanism the sidebar's PRO badge has relied on since the Pro badge feature. Two smaller calls: `aria-describedby={undefined}` on `SheetContent`, since the drawer has no one-line summary to point at (its description is a section of the body) and Radix would otherwise warn about a missing `Sheet.Description`; and **only Favorite goes yellow when active** — a first pass gave `data-active` `--type-favorite` for both, which made the pinned `useDebounce` read as favorited, so Pin now lights to `--foreground` and the favorite colour keeps meaning one thing. `formatFileSize` joined `src/lib/utils.ts` for the FILE branch of the content section, with **5 tests** (bytes stay whole, the step is 1024 and not 1000, one decimal above bytes, the loop stops at the last named unit, and a negative or non-finite size returns "—" rather than an invented one); the suite went 84 → **89**, and `npm run lint` and `npm run build` are clean. **A process change landed mid-feature and is the most durable thing here.** Browser verification with Playwright was stopped part-way through: it is slow, and duplicates a check the user prefers to run by hand. It is now its own opt-in action — **`/feature browser`**, at `.claude/skills/feature/actions/browser.md`, added to the skill's table and `argument-hint`. Note `/.claude` is gitignored, so that file is **not in this commit** and lives only on this machine. The action carries the environment traps this project has already paid for (reuse the running dev server, delete `.next` if a build wrote it, never build while dev is live, never `next start` because it loads `.env.production` and therefore the **production** Neon branch) plus two rules from this session: **never add temporary instrumentation to source files to reach a UI state** — an artificial 4s delay in the route handler was rejected outright, and `page.route()` through `browser_run_code_unsafe` is the way to fake a slow or failing response without touching the repo — and stay read-only against the database, so a state needing data the seed does not create is a "not verifiable" rather than a reason to run SQL. **What was actually verified in the browser** before that: the snippet drawer from `/dashboard` (blue type tile, `Snippet` + `typescript` badges, the content in a monospace block, the React Patterns collection chip, Created/Updated) and the link drawer from `/items/links` (green tile, the URL as an external link, no language badge) — which together prove the portal resolves `--type-color`, the whole card is the hit target, and the drawer works from both page types. **Explicitly not verified, and left that way on request:** the **Tags section, because `prisma/seed.ts` creates no tags at all** — every seeded item has an empty tag list, so that path cannot render without writing to the dev branch; also the loading skeleton, the error state, the 390px width, and whether Copy reaches the clipboard. **Left open:** the three inert actions and Edit are the obvious next features, and Edit in particular wants an item form, which nothing in the app has; the seed's missing tags are worth fixing on their own merits, since the item card's tag row is equally unexercised; and everything the previous entries list stays open — `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted (`src/app/items/layout.tsx` remains the third three-line `AppShell` wrapper), and a verified Resend sending domain is still what makes the mail flows usable. Spec: @context/features/item-drawer-spec.md
- **2026-08-20 — Snippet code view with line numbers.** Snippet content in the item drawer is shown with a line-number gutter. **No new file, no new dependency, no migration** — three files changed, and the whole gutter is CSS. The numbers are drawn by a **`counter()` in a `::before`, not written out as text nodes**, which is what keeps them out of the clipboard when the block is drag-selected: `user-select: none` on real text is less reliable across browsers, and generated content is not copied by any of them. (The drawer's Copy button uses `detail.content` and was unaffected either way.) **Gated on the item type slug, not `contentType`** — `NUMBERED_TYPE_SLUGS` in `src/constants/item-types.ts`, alongside the existing `PRO_TYPE_SLUGS` precedent — because TEXT also covers Prompt, Note and Command, where numbering one or two lines of prose is noise; those keep the plain block, and `ItemContent`'s empty-content check moved ahead of the fork so both branches share it. **The per-line element is classed `line` deliberately**: that is the shape *and* the class name Shiki emits (`<pre><code><span class="line">…</span>…`), so when syntax highlighting lands (project overview §9) the `.map` can be swapped for highlighted HTML with the gutter CSS untouched. Highlighting itself was left out of scope: the drawer fetches from a client component, so Shiki would have to run server-side and return HTML on `ItemDetail` rather than ship grammars and themes to the browser — its own feature. **Two things the CSS had to get right, both found while building rather than while advising.** `display: grid` on the `<code>` is the popular recipe and is wrong here: with `white-space: pre` the newline text nodes a highlighter emits between line spans become anonymous grid items and throw the rows off, so lines are block-level spans instead — which then need `min-height: 1lh` or an empty line collapses to zero height and the numbering drifts out of step with the code (the same unit the dashboard skeletons already rely on). And **`padding-left` is inside the scrollport**, so with a sticky gutter at `left: 0` code scrolled sideways showed through the 1rem strip beside the numbers; the numbered variant therefore sets `padding-left: 0` and the `::before` provides its own inset through `width: 3.5rem; padding-right: 1rem` — border-box, so a ~2.5rem number column plus the gap, with room for four digits. **Verified**: `npm test` 89 passed (unchanged — the diff is a component plus a `Set`, and `vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`, so there is no importable logic here to assert), `npx eslint` and `npm run build` clean, and the rules confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS (`min-height:1lh`, `position:sticky`, `content:counter(line)`, with a `-webkit-user-select` prefix added for free) — the same check the skeleton feature ran on `1lh`. Not checked in the browser: per standing preference the workflow now stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. **One process note worth keeping.** A `next dev` *and* a `next start -p 3005` were both live against `.next` when the build was due; per the three phantom failures earlier entries record, both were stopped first (on request) and `.next` was deleted before building. `.next` now holds a valid production build, so `next start -p 3005` serves this immediately — but **going back to `npm run dev` wants `.next` deleted first**, which is the trap the item-list-column-ladder entry hit. Also: the first commit message was written with a PowerShell here-string (`@'…'@`) through the **Bash** tool, which is not PowerShell — the `@` became the subject line and the real subject the second. Amended with a heredoc; the two shells take different syntax and the tool description says so. **Left open:** unchanged from the previous entries — syntax highlighting is the obvious follow-up and the markup is now shaped for it; the drawer's Favorite / Pin / Delete are still inert and Edit still disabled with no item form anywhere in the app; the seed still creates no tags, so the drawer's Tags section stays unexercised; `/collections` still 404s; no CI runs the suite; the `(app)` route group is still not adopted; and a verified Resend sending domain is still what makes the mail flows usable.
- **2026-08-20 — Item drawer edit mode.** The drawer's Edit button stops being inert: it switches the open drawer into an inline form, with no navigation and no second drawer. Eight new files, eight edited, **no migration** (`db:status` reported "Database schema is up to date!") and **no new dependency**. Added the ShadCN `textarea` primitive (`src/components/ui/textarea.tsx`, untouched CLI output — the **tenth** file in `components/ui/`). Title, Description and Tags are editable for every type; Content, Language and URL render only for the types that own them, and item type, collections and the dates stay display-only. **Ownership is enforced by the write itself, not by a check on its result:** `updateItem` in `src/lib/db/items.ts` puts `userId` in the `update`'s `where` alongside the id — which narrows an already-unique `where`, so the row cannot be swapped for another account's between the ownership read and the write — and returns `null` for both "no such item" and "not yours", the same conflation `getItemDetail` makes so an id nobody may see is never confirmed to exist. **Only the payload field the *stored* `contentType` owns is written**, `content` for TEXT and `url` for URL and `undefined` otherwise, read from the database rather than taken from the request: the three are mutually exclusive with no constraint saying so (project overview §10), so a crafted payload sending `content` for a link would otherwise store both and break the integrity check in `scripts/test-db.ts`. That is deliberately *not* in the Zod schema, which cannot see which type the item is — a comment on `updateItemSchema` records where the rule actually lives. **Tags needed the compound unique, not the name:** `Tag` is `@@unique([userId, name])`, so the replace is `set: []` (dropping the item's links while leaving the rows, which other items may use) plus `connectOrCreate` keyed on `userId_name` and carrying `userId`. The schema's tag dedupe is therefore not cosmetic — the same name twice makes `connectOrCreate` attempt a row that violates that index and fails the whole write — and it is **case-sensitive**, because Postgres treats `React` and `react` as two rows. **`toDetail` was extracted** so `getItemDetail` and `updateItem` produce the same shape including the ISO date conversion; without it the drawer would receive `Date`s from the action and strings from the API for one type. The row is typed with `Prisma.ItemGetPayload<{ select: typeof itemDetailSelect }>`. **Edit mode is keyed on the item id rather than a boolean** (`editingId === summary.id`): clicking a second card swaps `summary` without remounting the drawer, so a boolean would carry an open form onto someone else's fields; closing clears it too, so reopening always starts in view mode. **Edit mode renders its own `SheetHeader`** rather than being slotted into the view's, because Save and Cancel replace the action bar *inside* the header while the fields sit in the body — one component owning both keeps the form state in one place instead of hoisting it into `ItemDrawer` for two children in different parents. That made `ItemDrawerHeading` a shared component: it is the half that does not change between the modes, and `SheetTitle` has to be present in both for Radix's labelling. The buttons being in the header and the form in the body also means they reach it by `form={FORM_ID}` rather than by containment. `handleSaved` in the provider **bumps `requestRef` for the same reason a second click does** — a GET issued before the save must not land on top of a newer stored value — updates `summary` so the header and the card behind the drawer do not show the old title next to new content, and calls `router.refresh()` since the lists are server-rendered. **The form runs the same schema the action enforces, for the message alone**; the server parses again because that client copy is the caller's to skip, and a test asserts it by posting a bare `{ title: "Fine" }`. Save is disabled while the title is empty or a save is in flight, and there is no `setSaving(false)` on success — the drawer switches back to view mode on that call, unmounting the form with its state. `LANGUAGE_TYPE_SLUGS` joined `PRO_TYPE_SLUGS` and `NUMBERED_TYPE_SLUGS` in `src/constants/item-types.ts`, slug-gated for the same reason as the line numbers: a prompt and a note are TEXT too and neither has a language. One CSS note — `.item-edit-content` sets **`field-sizing: fixed`** to beat the primitive's own `field-sizing-content`, which would otherwise grow the content box to the height of the whole snippet. **20 new tests take the suite 89 → 109**: 14 over the schema (title trim and the whitespace-only rejection, emptied optional fields becoming `null` rather than `""`, inner whitespace in content surviving, a URL trimmed before the format check while an empty one is "no URL" rather than an invalid one, and the four tag rules — trim/drop empties, dedupe, case-sensitivity, and the cap counted *after* deduping) and 6 over the action (session, validation, the missing/not-yours result, and a rejected write becoming a message rather than a throw). One thing worth remembering about the mocks: `restoreMocks` in `vitest.config.mts` only restores `vi.spyOn` spies, so a `vi.fn()`'s call history carries across tests and the "not called" assertions would read the previous test's call — `vi.clearAllMocks()` in `beforeEach` is what makes them mean anything. `npm test`, `npx eslint` and `npm run build` are all clean. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. The dev server was stopped and `.next` deleted before the build, per the three phantom failures earlier entries record; **`.next` now holds a production build, so going back to `npm run dev` wants it deleted first**. **Left open:** FILE and IMAGE get title / description / tags only, since the spec gives them no editable payload field and uploads are a separate Pro-gated feature; Favorite, Pin and Delete are still inert, and delete in particular is the obvious next one; collection management and changing an item's type are out of scope by the spec, as is a real code editor for the content textarea; there is still **no create flow** — the top bar's New Item button is display-only and nothing in the app makes an item; and the seed still creates no tags, so the tag round-trip is covered by unit tests but has never been exercised against real data. Everything the previous entries list stays open: `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and a verified Resend sending domain is still what makes the mail flows usable. Spec: @context/features/item-drawer-edit-spec.md
- **2026-08-20 — Delete items.** The drawer's Delete button stops being inert: it opens a ShadCN `AlertDialog` naming the item, and confirming deletes it, closes the drawer, toasts and refreshes. One new file (`src/components/items/item-delete-dialog.tsx`), eight edited, **no migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive** — `alert-dialog` came with the profile page and `sonner` with the rate limiting, so both were already in `components/ui/`. Loaded inline rather than from a spec file; the request read "create delete functionality for items", which was taken as *build the delete flow* and not as create-and-delete, since a confirmation dialog and the one inert Trash button both point that way and no create form exists anywhere in the app. **`deleteMany` guarded on its `count`, not `delete`:** Prisma's `delete` throws `P2025` once the row is gone, so a double-click or two tabs racing would surface as a crash rather than as the item already being deleted — the same fix the password-reset flow carries, and which `consumeVerificationToken` still lacks. `userId` sits in the `where` as `updateItem` does, so another account's item reads as *missing* rather than forbidden and the action reuses the existing `MISSING` copy. **Delete is live as soon as the drawer opens**, unlike Edit which waits on the detail: it needs no field from the item, only the id the card already carried, so the confirmation names `detail?.title ?? summary.title` and reads correctly either way. The provider's `handleDeleted` **bumps `requestRef`** for the reason `handleSaved` does — an in-flight GET must not repopulate a drawer for a row that is gone — closes the sheet, and calls `router.refresh()`; `summary` is deliberately left alone so the sheet does not blank mid-animation. `ItemDrawer` clears `editingId` on the same path, or reopening could land in a form for a deleted item. The confirm button is a **plain `Button`, not `AlertDialogAction`**, which closes the dialog on click and would leave a failure nowhere to report itself — the trap `delete-account-dialog.tsx` already documents — and `open` is controlled so the dialog can be closed *before* the drawer, unwinding the two Radix layers innermost-first. **A `/feature browser` pass found one real bug, which is the main thing worth remembering.** `await deleteItem(itemId)` handled a returned `{ success: false }` but not a **thrown** one: a server action whose request cannot complete rejects instead, and with nothing catching it the rejection was unhandled, `deleting` never cleared, and the dialog was **stuck permanently** — both buttons disabled and Escape swallowed by the component's own in-flight guard, recoverable only by reloading the page. Found by faking a 500 on the server-action POST with `page.route()` (which is a `POST` to the current URL carrying a `next-action` header — that header is what distinguishes it from a navigation), fixed with `.catch(() => null)` and an `UNREACHABLE` message, and re-verified against the identical interception: buttons re-enabled, inline error *and* toast, Escape working, drawer intact. **`src/components/items/item-drawer-edit.tsx` has the identical defect** — `await updateItem(...)` with no catch, which would strand `saving` and kill Save the same way — and is left for its own change. **What the delete takes with it:** `ItemCollection` rows cascade, and the implicit `Item` ↔ `Tag` join rows cascade, but **`Tag` rows themselves survive** — they are per-user and shared, so deleting the last item carrying a tag orphans the tag row. No sweep exists and none was in scope. **4 new tests take the suite 109 → 113** (session, the missing/not-yours `false`, the delete itself asserting the user id comes from the session and not the caller, and a thrown write becoming a message); `npm test`, `npx eslint` and `npm run build` are clean. **Verified in the browser** against the Neon dev branch as demo: the confirmation names the item; Cancel leaves the drawer open and changes nothing; a real delete took the stat card 18 → 17, the sidebar's Link count 6 → 5 and the card off the page, with an "Item deleted" toast; **`body` pointer-events returned to `auto` with the inline style cleared** after both layers closed, and another drawer opened straight afterwards to prove the page was genuinely clickable rather than nominally so — so the nested-portal risk the spec flagged did not materialise (`radix-ui@1.6.7` reference-counts it in `DismissableLayer`). Zero console errors or warnings on a fresh load through the whole flow, and 390px showed no horizontal overflow with the dialog inset 35–355px and its buttons stacked. **Two things the verification itself taught.** `npm run db:seed` restores the 18 items but **sets no `isFavorite` or `isPinned` at all** — there is no match for either in `prisma/seed.ts` — so re-seeding to undo the test deletion also cleared flags that had been set by hand: 3 pinned items (`useDebounce script`, `Reclaim Docker Disk Space!`, `GitHub Actions Documentation`), 1 favorite collection (`React Patterns`) and 1 favorite item whose identity is unrecoverable, since item cards render no favorite marker in the accessibility tree. Anyone re-seeding to undo a test should expect that. And re-seeding **rewrites the demo user's `passwordHash`**, which changes the password fingerprint and so invalidated the live session mid-pass — an incidental confirmation that the session-invalidation fix from the auth audit works. **Left open:** the favorite/pinned flags above were not restored; the edit form's missing `catch` is the obvious companion fix; Favorite and Pin are now the only inert actions left in the drawer; delete exists only in the drawer, not on the card; and there is still **no create flow**, which is the natural next feature — the top bar's New Item button remains display-only. Everything the previous entries list stays open: the seed still creates no tags, `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and a verified Resend sending domain is still what makes the mail flows usable.
- **2026-08-20 — Item create.** The top bar's New Item button stops being display-only, so the app can make an item for the first time — the "no create flow" line every entry since the drawer's edit mode has carried. Three new source files (`src/components/items/item-create-dialog.tsx`, `item-create-form.tsx`, `src/lib/db/items.test.ts`) plus the spec, ten edited, **no migration** (`prisma/schema.prisma` untouched — nothing here is a schema change, so `db:status` was not run), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**: `dialog` came with the profile improvements, `sonner` with the rate limiting, and `label` / `input` / `textarea` / `button` were all already in `components/ui/`. **`CREATABLE_TYPES` in `src/constants/item-types.ts` is the new source of truth**, joining `PRO_TYPE_SLUGS`, `NUMBERED_TYPE_SLUGS` and `LANGUAGE_TYPE_SLUGS` — it maps each offered slug to its label, icon and **content kind**, which has to live somewhere because `ItemType` carries no column saying whether a type holds text, a URL or a file (project overview §4.2); it is the same split `contentTypeFor` in `prisma/seed.ts` makes for the seeded content. **File and Image are absent deliberately** — the Pro-gated types have no upload flow anywhere in the app, so there is no payload to create them with, and their absence from the list is what makes a request naming them fail the schema rather than needing a second check. **The payload names a `typeSlug`, never an `itemTypeId`:** the id is the database's to hand out, so the action resolves the slug through `creatableType` (which is what refuses a type the dialog does not offer) and then through the `cache()`d `getItemTypeBySlug`, and it is that resolved `contentType` — not anything the request sent — that decides which field is written. `createItem` in `src/lib/db/items.ts` therefore writes `content` for TEXT and `url` for URL and null for the other, dropping `language` for the types outside `LANGUAGE_TYPE_SLUGS`, which keeps the mutual exclusivity `scripts/test-db.ts` asserts and no DB constraint enforces. Tags reuse the edit path exactly — `connectOrCreate` on the `userId_name` compound unique, with the schema's case-sensitive dedupe still load-bearing rather than cosmetic, since the same name twice makes `connectOrCreate` attempt a row that violates that index and fails the whole write — and the row comes back through the shared `toDetail`, so the ISO dates match what the drawer already reads. **`createItemSchema` and `updateItemSchema` share one `itemFields` object** rather than the create schema being invented separately: the two payloads carry the same set and only create also names a type. The one cross-field rule that *is* in the schema is that a link needs its URL, because a form can act on that; which payload field a type owns stays `lib/db/items.ts`'s call for the same reason it already was — the shape does not say which type the item is. `CreateItemResult` is structurally identical to `UpdateItemResult` and kept separate anyway, so the two actions are free to diverge. **The form carries the `.catch(() => null)` the delete flow's browser pass had to discover the hard way** — a server action answers a failed *write* with `{ success: false }` but a failed *request* rejects, and without the catch the rejection is unhandled and `saving` never clears, leaving the form permanently unsubmittable. `src/components/items/item-drawer-edit.tsx` **still has that defect** and is still left for its own change; only its CSS classes were touched here. The dialog is **controlled**, so the form can close it once the item exists, and Radix unmounting `DialogContent` on close is what makes the next open start empty — the same property `ChangePasswordDialog` relies on to stop a stale notice lingering, so no reset of its own is needed. Success is a toast plus `router.refresh()`, since the lists, the sidebar's per-type counts and the stat cards are all server-rendered. **The CSS split the edit form's rules rather than copying them:** `.item-edit-form` / `-field` / `-hint` / `-content` became `.item-form-*` and are now shared by both forms, which is the whole reason the edit component appears in this diff. New to the dialog are `.item-create-dialog` at `40rem` — beating the primitive's own `sm:max-w-sm` **without `!important`**, because our rules are unlayered while Tailwind's utilities sit in the `utilities` layer, the mechanism the sidebar's PRO badge has relied on since the Pro badge feature — and `.item-create-body`, which scrolls the fields so the footer's buttons stay in view on a long form. The type picker is **real radios hidden inside their labels** rather than removed, so the group keeps its keyboard behaviour and focus lands somewhere visible; selection is stated with `:has(:checked)` and a `color-mix` tint on the existing `--type-color`, which each option resolves from its own `data-type`. **18 new tests take the suite 113 → 131**: 6 over the schema (that it shares the edit schema's field rules, that a slug naming no type is rejected, that File is rejected as a type the dialog does not offer, and the three URL cases), 6 over the action (session, an invalid payload not touching the database, a type the dialog does not offer, a slug with no row behind it, a failed write becoming a message rather than a throw, and that the item is created as the *resolved* type), and 6 over the query — text content written with no URL, a link's URL written with no content, language kept for a type that carries one and dropped for one that does not, tags attached scoped to the owner, and the dates returned as ISO strings. `npm test`, `npx eslint` and `npm run build` are all clean. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. No dev server was running when the build ran, per the three phantom `.next` failures earlier entries record; **`.next` now holds a production build, so going back to `npm run dev` wants it deleted first**. **Left open:** creating is only possible from the top bar, so there is no per-type create on `/items/[type]` and no way to put a new item straight into a collection; File and Image cannot be created at all until an upload flow exists; Favorite and Pin are still the inert actions in the drawer; the edit form's missing `.catch` is still the obvious companion fix; and the seed still creates no tags, though the tag round-trip is at last reachable through the UI rather than only through unit tests. Everything the previous entries list stays open: `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and a verified Resend sending domain is still what makes the mail flows usable. Spec: @context/features/item-create-spec.md
- **2026-08-20 — Fix: a rejected save stranded the item drawer's edit mode.** The companion fix the item-create entry left open, done as its own change. **One file, one behaviour, no test, no dependency, no migration.** `src/components/items/item-drawer-edit.tsx` awaited `updateItem` bare: the action answers a failed *write* with `{ success: false }`, which the form handled, but a failed *request* **rejects** instead — and with nothing catching it the rejection was unhandled, `saving` never cleared, and Save stayed permanently disabled with the drawer stuck in edit mode until the page was reloaded. That is the identical defect the delete flow's `/feature browser` pass found by faking a 500 on the server-action POST, and it had been sitting in the edit form since that mode landed. Now `.catch(() => null)` with `result?.success` and `result?.error ?? UNREACHABLE`, which makes all three item mutations — create, edit and delete — the same shape down to the shared "Could not reach the server. Try again." wording. **No test, and none is possible under the current config:** the defect is in a component's submit handler, and `vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`, so there is nothing importable to assert — the create form's identical `.catch` is uncovered for exactly the same reason. Confirming it takes the browser and a `page.route()` interception, which is how the delete case was proven; that was not repeated here. `npm test` stayed at 131, and `npx eslint` and `npm run build` are clean (`.next` deleted first, no dev server live). **Left open:** unchanged from the previous entry — Favorite and Pin are the last inert drawer actions, File and Image cannot be created until an upload flow exists, creating is only possible from the top bar, `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and a verified Resend sending domain is still what makes the mail flows usable.
- **2026-08-21 — Monaco code editor for snippets and commands.** Snippet and command content is shown and edited in a Monaco editor inside a macOS-style window frame — traffic-light dots on the left of the title bar, the resolved language and a copy button on the right. Three new files (`src/components/items/code-editor.tsx`, `src/lib/code-language.ts` + its test), six edited, **no migration** (`prisma/schema.prisma` untouched, so `db:status` was not run) and **no new ShadCN primitive**. Installed `@monaco-editor/react` (^4.7.0), whose peer `monaco-editor` (^0.56.0) was **declared explicitly as a devDependency** rather than left to npm's peer auto-install: `next build` type-checks against its `.d.ts`, so it is a build requirement, but nothing imports it at runtime and it never reaches a bundle. **The loading strategy was a question asked before implementing, and the CDN path was chosen:** `@monaco-editor/react`'s loader fetches Monaco from jsdelivr, which costs zero bundler and worker configuration and works with Turbopack as-is — the trade-off, accepted knowingly, is that the editor is the app's first runtime dependency on a third-party CDN and will not render offline. Self-hosting would have meant `loader.config({ monaco })` plus the ESM worker wiring, which is the fiddly part under Turbopack. **The hand-rolled line-number gutter is gone.** `NUMBERED_TYPE_SLUGS` was `{"snippets"}` and the new code set is a superset of it, so the CSS `counter()` path from the snippet-code-view feature became unreachable — `NumberedCode`, the constant and the `.item-drawer-code-numbered*` rules were all deleted rather than left dead, and Monaco draws its own gutter. The plain `.item-drawer-code` block stays for prompts and notes. **`CODE_TYPE_LANGUAGES` is a private `Map` in `src/constants/item-types.ts`** behind `isCodeType` / `codeTypeLanguage`, joining `PRO_TYPE_SLUGS` / `LANGUAGE_TYPE_SLUGS` / `CREATABLE_TYPES`; it carries the per-type *fallback language* as well as the membership, so a command with no hint highlights as `shell` and a snippet with none as `plaintext`. Slug-gated for the same reason every one of those sets is: TEXT also covers prompts and notes, and prose in a code editor reads worse than prose in a paragraph. **`src/lib/code-language.ts` is the only pure logic here and the only thing Vitest can reach** — `vitest.config.mts` collects `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**` only, so the component itself has no importable surface. It normalises a free-text hint (trim, lowercase, alias) to a Monaco id and that id to a header label. **Both its lookup tables are `Map`s, not object literals, and that is load-bearing**: the hint is whatever was typed into the Language field, so `ALIASES["constructor"]` on a plain object would return a function off the prototype and hand it to Monaco as a language id. The same fix was applied to `CODE_TYPE_LANGUAGES`. **9 tests take the suite 131 → 140** — alias translation, the five shell dialects collapsing onto Monaco's single `shell` grammar (it has no `bash` or `zsh`, so a hint naming one would silently render as plain text), case and whitespace, the null/empty fallback, the caller's fallback losing to a hint that is present, an unknown hint passing through rather than being discarded, and the label table against the title-cased long tail. **Five editor decisions worth keeping.** (1) **All validators and autocomplete are off** — `typescriptDefaults` / `javascriptDefaults` / `jsonDefaults` / `cssDefaults` are given `validate: false` in `beforeMount`, because a stashed snippet is usually a *fragment* and every validator would cover it in red squiggles that say nothing about whether the snippet is any good; turning suggestions off also means no overflow widget can be clipped by the frame's `overflow: hidden`, and no extra CDN chunks load. (2) **`alwaysConsumeMouseWheel: false`**, or the editor traps the wheel and the drawer around it cannot be scrolled past. (3) **Height opens at an estimate from the line count**, then `onDidContentSizeChange` takes over clamped to `[min, 400]` — the estimate is not decoration: `@monaco-editor/react` renders its `loading` node inside a box of exactly the given height, so starting at the minimum would both jump on mount and clip the fallback. (4) **The read-only fallback is the plain code block**, passed as that `loading` node, so a slow or blocked CDN still shows the content; an edit box has no equally graceful form and gets a "Loading editor…" line instead, which is the accepted cost of the CDN choice. (5) **`fontFamily` is set in the editor's options, never in CSS** — Monaco measures the font it is told about, so overriding the family behind its back drifts the cursor away from the text; `var(--font-mono), ui-monospace, monospace` is passed so the fallback keeps it monospace even if the custom property fails to resolve inside Monaco's measuring element. **The theme is literal hex.** `defineTheme` parses the colours itself and can read neither the app's `oklch()` values nor the custom properties holding them, so `DEVSTASH_DARK` hardcodes the `.dark` tokens converted by hand — `#0a0a0a` background, `#171717` card (the editor's own background), `#262626` muted (the title bar), `#fafafa` foreground, `#a1a1a1` muted-foreground — and must be kept in step with `globals.css` by hand. The scrollbar is themed there too (`scrollbarSlider.*` at 12/20/30% white) with `globals.css` only rounding the slider into a pill, since it is Monaco's own DOM. **In edit mode the highlighting follows the live Language field** rather than the saved value, which is free — the editor takes `values.language` — and makes the field's effect visible as it is typed. **`<Label htmlFor>` had to become conditional**: Monaco has no element to pair a label with, so the code branch renders `htmlFor={undefined}` and the editor names itself through `ariaLabel` instead, while the textarea branch keeps its id. **One build failure worth remembering: `hover.enabled` is `'on' | 'off' | 'onKeyboardModifier'` in Monaco 0.56, not a boolean** — `{ enabled: false }` compiles as a plausible-looking options object and fails type checking only at `next build`. `npm test` 140 passed, `npx eslint` and `npm run build` clean, with `.next` deleted and no dev server live first, per the three phantom `.next` failures earlier entries record. The new rules were confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS — which rewrote `:nth-child(1)` to `:first-child` on the red traffic light, an equivalent selector — the same check the skeleton and column-ladder features ran. **Not verified in the browser:** per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. Two things specifically want an eye on first run — whether `var(--font-mono)` really resolves inside Monaco's measuring element (the failure mode is a drifting cursor, not a crash, and the monospace fallback bounds how bad it gets), and whether the 400px cap and the pill scrollbar look right at the drawer's width. **Left open:** light mode is not addressed — one dark theme is defined and the root layout still hardcodes `.dark`, so a future light-mode toggle needs a second `defineTheme` and a `theme` prop; the editor loads on every drawer open for a code item, which is heavy for a read-only view and would be the thing to reconsider if it drags; and `@monaco-editor/react` exposes no error callback, so a permanently failed CDN load shows the fallback (read-only) or the loading line (edit) forever rather than reporting itself. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still the inert drawer actions, File and Image cannot be created until an upload flow exists, creating is only possible from the top bar, the seed still creates no tags, `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and a verified Resend sending domain is still what makes the mail flows usable. Spec: @context/features/code-editor-spec.md
- **2026-08-24 — Per-type create button on the type pages.** Each `/items/[type]` page carries its own add button beside the heading, labelled from `ItemType.name` ("New Snippet"), opening the existing create dialog with that type already selected — which closes the "creating is only possible from the top bar" line every entry since item create has carried. **No new file**, seven edited, **no migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive** (`badge` came with the sidebar's PRO badge). Loaded inline rather than from a spec. **The decision that shaped it: two of the seven type pages cannot create anything.** `CREATABLE_TYPES` omitted `files` and `images` because they are Pro-gated and nothing uploads a file, but all seven are seeded system types with real pages and sidebar links, so those two pages would get a button leading nowhere. Asked, and told to implement for them too since Pro comes later — implemented as **offered but not storable**: the picker now shows all seven types with a `PRO` badge on File and Image, both are preselectable, and choosing one swaps the payload fields for a note saying uploads come with Pro with Create disabled. **`CREATABLE_TYPES` — what `createItem` validates against — is deliberately unchanged**, because `createItem` writes `content` for TEXT and `url` for URL and nothing for FILE, so actually allowing those slugs would store title-only rows with no file behind them; the server refusing them is the backstop that makes the disabled button safe rather than merely tidy. That constraint was decided rather than asked a second time, and stated at the time. **The constant was restructured into one ordered source.** `ITEM_TYPE_OPTIONS` is a private array of all seven in sidebar order carrying a `creatable` flag; `PICKER_TYPES` exports it whole for the picker and `CREATABLE_TYPES` is `.filter(creatable)`, so the two lists cannot drift and the ordering is stated once. `pickerType(slug)` joins `creatableType(slug)` — the pair is the whole point, and confusing them is what would reintroduce payload-less rows. **The two existing tests asserting `files` and `images` are rejected** (in `src/lib/validations/item.test.ts` and `src/actions/items.test.ts`) were the real check on this refactor and still pass untouched; the suite stayed at **140**. **No new tests, deliberately** — what was added is a filter and two lookups over a literal array, and asserting its contents would be testing the literal, which coding-standards names as not earning a test. Worth knowing for next time: **`src/constants/**` is not collected by Vitest at all** (`include` is `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`), so a test for `pickerType` would have to be misfiled under `src/lib/` to run, and no test in the repo imports from `@/constants` today. The one invariant that would earn a test — every system slug the sidebar can link to resolving in `PICKER_TYPES`, or a type page's button silently opening on Snippet — has no home, because the other list of all seven (`TYPE_SLUG_ORDER` in `src/lib/db/item-types.ts`) is private; exporting it purely to test it was judged the worse trade while an eighth system type is post-launch custom-types territory. **One false start worth remembering, caught before the build rather than by it.** The dialog first took the trigger as a `ReactNode` prop so each caller kept its own button markup, and the type page — a **server component** — passed a `<Button>` into it. That is exactly what the profile-page improvements entry already recorded and reverted: `DialogTrigger asChild` **clones** what it is given through Radix's `Slot`, and a server-rendered element surviving that clone is unproven in this project. Replaced with a `label` string so the button is always constructed on the client, which also collapsed the two call sites to one markup shape. The comment on the prop records why it is a string and not a node. **`.dashboard-topbar-action-label` was renamed to `.action-label`** now that three buttons share it (New Collection, New Item, and the type pages' button) and it is no longer a top-bar concern; the rule is unchanged, so the new button also collapses to its icon below the breakpoint. `.dashboard-heading-row` is a flex row with `flex-wrap`, so the button drops under the heading rather than squeezing it on a narrow screen. The dialog's own copy adapts — "New snippet" / "Fill in what it needs, or switch to another type." against the generic "New item" / "Pick a type…" — and the picker **stays switchable**, so the preselection is a starting point and not a lock. Radix unmounting `DialogContent` on close is what re-applies `typeSlug`, so the picker returns to the page's type after a create rather than keeping whatever was last chosen. **A `typeSlug` naming no known type falls through to the form's default** instead of selecting nothing, though the page only ever passes a slug `getItemTypeBySlug` already resolved. `npm test` 140 passed, `npx eslint` and `npm run build` clean, `.next` deleted and no dev server live first per the phantom-failure entries; the three new rules were confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS, and `grep` confirmed no stale `dashboard-topbar-action-label` reference survived the rename. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. **Left open:** the Pro branch is a dead end by design until an upload flow exists, which is the feature that makes File and Image real; there is still no way to create an item straight into a collection; and the dashboard has no per-type entry point, only the generic one. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still the inert drawer actions, the seed still creates no tags, `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and a verified Resend sending domain is still what makes the mail flows usable.
- **2026-08-24 — Markdown editor for notes and prompts.** Notes and prompts edit through a Write / Preview tabbed editor and **render** in the drawer instead of being shown as their source. Two new files (`src/components/items/markdown-editor.tsx` and the ShadCN `tabs` primitive — untouched CLI output, the **13th** file in `components/ui/`, adding no dependency since it imports the `radix-ui` package the nova preset already installed), five edited, **no migration**. Installed `react-markdown` (^10.1.0) and `remark-gfm` (^4.0.1) — 96 packages, the unified/mdast tree — and unlike Monaco these are **bundled, not CDN**, so they land in the client bundle for real. Snippets and commands keep `CodeEditor` untouched and links keep the URL input. **The spec contradicted itself on colour and the tokens won.** It asked for a `bg-[#1e1e1e]` container and `bg-[#2d2d2d]` header while also saying "match existing dark theme styling"; those disagree twice over — they are Tailwind **arbitrary utility classes**, which CLAUDE.md forbids in markup, and they are near-misses for what `CodeEditor` actually uses (`--card` is `#171717`, `--muted` is `#262626`), so following them literally would have made the two editors visibly *not* match and left the only hardcoded surface colours in the app. Flagged at load, recommended the tokens, and `/feature start` came back with no objection. **The frame is shared through a grouped CSS selector, not copied** — `.code-editor, .markdown-editor` state the border, radius, `overflow: hidden` and background once, and `.code-editor-bar, .markdown-editor-bar` the same for the title bar, so "same style as CodeEditor" is enforced by there being one rule rather than by two rules agreeing. The copy button's five states are grouped the same way. Deliberately **no markup churn in `code-editor.tsx`**: extracting shared `.editor-frame` / `.editor-bar` classes would have been the tidier factoring but meant editing a feature shipped four days earlier, and coding-standards says make the minimal change. **`CODE_TYPE_LANGUAGES` is gone.** Gating Markdown on top of it would have made a *third* parallel list beside it and `ITEM_TYPE_OPTIONS`, so `isCodeType`, the new `isMarkdownType` and `codeTypeLanguage` now all derive from the one ordered list, which gained `editor: "code" | "markdown"` and `fallbackLanguage`. Every signature is unchanged, so no call site moved and the suite stayed green through the refactor — which was the real risk in this change. With this the TEXT types **partition completely**: snippets and commands are code, notes and prompts are markdown, and the pair of predicates covers all four. **Read-only renders a "Preview" label, not a one-tab strip.** The spec says "only show the Preview tab"; a tab strip with a single tab is a control that does nothing, so the label sits exactly where `CodeEditor`'s language label does and the rendered output is all there is. Edit mode opens on Write, per spec. **Raw HTML stays disabled and that is a rule, not a default** — `react-markdown` ignores it unless `rehype-raw` is added, and item content is user-authored and stored, so rendering embedded HTML would be a stored-XSS path; the reasoning is a comment on the preview component so the next person does not "fix" it. Markdown links are overridden through the `components` prop to open in their own tab with `rel="noreferrer noopener"`. **The 400px cap is plain CSS here, not measured.** `CodeEditor` arrives at its cap through Monaco's `onDidContentSizeChange`; the markdown panes just set `max-height: 400px; overflow-y: auto`, and the source textarea inherits `field-sizing: content` from the ShadCN primitive — the same property `.item-form-content` sets to `fixed` to *defeat* — which gives fluid-to-400 for free. "Matching CodeEditor" means the same number, not the same mechanism. **One trap caught by reading the primitive rather than by the build: the ShadCN `Tabs` root is `flex gap-2 data-horizontal:flex-col`**, so the 0.5rem gap would have seamed the title bar away from the pane and broken the frame. `.markdown-editor { gap: 0 }` fixes it and is inert in read-only, where the element is a plain block rather than the Tabs root. The tab triggers are also restyled down from the primitive's `h-8` pill to `1.5rem` so the bar keeps the code editor's height — those overrides win **without `!important`** because our rules are unlayered while Tailwind's utilities sit in the `utilities` layer, the same mechanism the sidebar's PRO badge and the drawer's width cap have relied on. `.markdown-preview` carries the rendered typography as its own class rather than a prose plugin, so the palette is the app's tokens and nothing depends on a `.dark` ancestor: h1–h6 sized down from 1.5rem with a rule under the top two as GitHub draws them, fenced blocks on `--muted` with the inline-code background and padding removed inside them, GFM task lists de-bulleted via `li:has(> input[type="checkbox"])`, blockquotes with a 3px left accent, links on `--type-link`, and tables `display: block` so a wide one scrolls itself rather than the drawer. **Verified**: `npm test` **140 passed** (unchanged — see below), `npx eslint` and `npm run build` clean, `.next` deleted and no dev server live first per the phantom-failure entries; `grep` confirmed no stale `CODE_TYPE_LANGUAGES` reference and no `rehype` import anywhere but the explanatory comment; and the new rules were confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS, including `.markdown-editor{gap:0}` surviving as its own rule rather than being merged into the grouped frame declaration. **No new tests, and none are possible for this surface** — `vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`, and everything added is component rendering plus three predicates in `src/constants/`, which is not collected either (noted in the previous entry). The constants refactor was the part that could have broken something and the existing suite covered it. **Not verified in the browser:** per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in — though the markdown typography is the kind of thing that genuinely wants eyes, and the tab strip's fit inside the bar is the most likely thing to be a pixel off. **Left open:** no syntax highlighting inside fenced code blocks in the preview (the `pre code` is unhighlighted, and Shiki-in-the-preview would be its own feature, as it would for the code editor); the preview re-parses on every keystroke in Write→Preview switching, which is fine at note length but is unmemoised; and light mode is untouched, though unlike the code editor's hardcoded Monaco theme this preview is all tokens, so it would follow a future toggle for free. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still the inert drawer actions, File and Image cannot be created until an upload flow exists, the seed still creates no tags, `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and a verified Resend sending domain is still what makes the mail flows usable. Spec: @context/features/markdown-editor-spec.md
- **2026-08-24 — File & image upload on Cloudflare R2.** The two Pro-gated types stop being a dead end: File and Image items can be created, previewed, downloaded and deleted, which closes the "File and Image cannot be created until an upload flow exists" line every entry since item create has carried. Nine new files (`src/lib/r2.ts`, `src/lib/file-constraints.ts`, `src/lib/content-disposition.ts` and their three tests, `src/app/api/upload/route.ts`, `src/app/api/items/[id]/file/route.ts`, `src/components/items/file-upload.tsx`), fifteen edited, **no migration** and **no new ShadCN primitive** — `Item.fileUrl` / `fileName` / `fileSize` and `ContentType.FILE` have been in the schema since `20260814120000_init` and had never been used, so the spec's storage requirement was already satisfied. One dependency: **`@aws-sdk/client-s3` (^3.1116.0)**, since R2 speaks S3; `region: "auto"` and an endpoint of `https://${R2_ACCOUNT_ID}.r2.cloudflarestorage.com` are what point it at Cloudflare. **`Item.fileUrl` holds the R2 object *key*, not a URL**, which is the decision everything else follows from: nothing in the app ever needs a public URL, because the file is served through `/api/items/[id]/file`, and both that route and the delete path need the key — storing a URL would mean parsing the key back out of it on every use. The consequence worth knowing is that **`R2_PUBLIC_URL` goes unused** by this implementation; it is what a later switch to a public bucket and CDN-served images would want. The schema's own comment on the column ("Cloudflare R2 URL") is now wrong, and `createItem` carries a comment saying so. **Three places conspired to make these types unstorable and had to move together**, exactly as flagged when the spec was loaded: `ITEM_TYPE_OPTIONS` marked them `creatable: false`, `createItem` refused those slugs, and the create form showed a "uploads come with Pro" note. With a real payload the `creatable` flag became always-true, so it is gone and `PICKER_TYPES` / `CREATABLE_TYPES` collapsed back into one list with one lookup (`creatableType`); `pickerType` and `.item-create-locked` went with it. **The PRO badge now reads `PRO_TYPE_SLUGS` rather than creatability** — the two happened to coincide before and no longer do, and per project overview §8 the badge is a label while all users get full access during development. The two existing tests that encoded the old rule were **rewritten rather than deleted**: a file type is now refused for *having no file*, not for being a file type. **`POST /api/upload` is an API route and not a server action**, which is the exception coding-standards already carves out: an action gives the browser no upload progress to report, and this is a multipart body rather than a form of fields. It is deliberately outside the proxy's matcher for the reason `/api/items/[id]` records — the proxy answers an unauthenticated request with a redirect to the sign-in page, which a `fetch` receives as an opaque 200 of HTML — so the check lives in the handler and says 401. It is rate limited at **30 per 15 minutes keyed on the user id** (not the IP: it is this account's storage being spent, and the caller is already known), sized against storage rather than against guessing, since every accepted call writes an object nothing else deletes and the app has no quota. **The object is written before any item exists**, so a file picked and then abandoned leaves an orphan — the accepted cost of showing progress before the item is created, recorded in the route. **`GET /api/items/[id]/file` is the download proxy**, and it serves the preview too: `?download` asks for `Content-Disposition: attachment`, and without it the response is inline, which is what the drawer's `<img>` loads. That is why **`next/image` is not used and the lint rule is disabled with a reason** — the optimizer fetches the source server-side without the visitor's cookies, so it would receive the route's own 401, and there is nothing to optimize anyway since the response is per-account and uncacheable. The route sends **`Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`**, which is what covers an uploaded SVG: inside an `<img>` its script never executes, but the URL can be opened directly, and that is the navigation the sandbox stops. **`src/lib/file-constraints.ts` is the shared rule** — 5 MB images / 10 MB files, the spec's extension and MIME lists — run by the form for its message and by the route as the rule. **The extension decides the stored content type, never what the browser reported**: `.md`, `.toml` and `.ini` have no type browsers agree on, so the same file arrives as `text/markdown` on one machine and `""` on another, and storing what the extension says keeps the download route serving the same thing either way. An unreported or `application/octet-stream` type is therefore forgiven, but only *after* the extension has passed, so it is not a way past the rules. Both lookup tables are `Map`s rather than object literals, for the reason `src/lib/code-language.ts` already records. **Delete removes the object first and keeps the row if that fails** — the decision taken when the spec was loaded — with its own message naming which half failed, so the item stays deletable once storage clears; the alternative leaves an object nothing points at and no way to find it again. `deleteItem` reaches storage only for an item that actually carries a file. **Object keys are checked against the caller's own `uploads/{userId}/` prefix** on both create and delete (`ownsObjectKey`), since the key is the one thing in the create payload naming something outside the row being written. The original filename is deliberately **not** part of the key — it is stored as `fileName` instead, which sidesteps sanitizing user input into a path and makes two files of one name two objects. **A `/feature test` follow-up hardened that check and pinned it**: a `..` segment is now refused (not traversal at the store, since S3 keys are opaque strings, but the key arrives from a request), and the tests pin that **the trailing slash in the prefix is load-bearing** — without it `uploads/user-10/…` sits inside `user-1`'s prefix. Writing the tests for `contentDisposition` — moved out of the route into `src/lib/content-disposition.ts` precisely so it could be tested, since `vitest.config.mts` collects nothing under `src/app` — **found a real gap**: a name that is entirely non-ASCII produced `filename=""`, which clients read inconsistently, and it now falls back. The case that matters most is covered directly: a `\r\n` in a filename cannot reach the header. **`FileUpload` uses `XMLHttpRequest`, not `fetch`**, which reports no upload progress — it resolves when the response arrives, by which point the transfer it would have described is over. The zone is a `div` with the real `<input type="file">` stretched over it at `opacity: 0`, so a click anywhere opens the picker while the control keeps its own focus ring and keyboard behaviour, and `data-dragging` states the drag. Switching item type clears the upload, so a `.png` cannot be carried into a File item. **34 new tests take the suite 140 → 185** across five files — the constraint module (extension parsing, the two size caps, the forgiven-unknown-MIME rule, the kinds staying separate), the create schema's file rules, the action's foreign-prefix refusal and the delete ordering (asserted as an actual order, plus that a failed object delete leaves `deleteItemRow` uncalled), the query writing only the file fields for a FILE type, and the two hardened helpers. `npm test`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` are clean, and the new CSS was confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS. **Two things from the session worth remembering.** `npm test` failed **all 16 files** once with `Vitest failed to find the runner` / `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'config')` — no tests ran at all — and an immediate re-run with nothing changed passed 185/185; it is a transient runner-initialisation failure that looks alarming and is cured by re-running. And **a build was run against a live dev server** despite the port check being in the same command: the check printed and the build proceeded anyway because nothing gated on it. That is the fourth time `.next` sharing has bitten this project, and the lesson is that printing a check is not the same as acting on one. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. Nothing here has ever touched a real R2 bucket: every path is exercised through unit tests with the SDK mocked, so **the first real upload is the first proof the credentials and bucket name are right**. **Left open:** an abandoned upload orphans its object and there is no sweep; **a 10 MB upload will exceed Vercel's 4.5 MB serverless request-body limit**, and the fix is presigned direct-to-R2 PUTs, which is exactly what the spec's proxy-to-avoid-CORS requirement steers away from — a deployment-time decision rather than a code bug; the edit drawer still cannot replace an item's file (title / description / tags only, unchanged); Copy is now disabled for file items, since `fileUrl` holds a key that means nothing outside the server, and Download is how a file gets out; `FileUpload` and both route handlers are **untestable by configuration**, not by choice, so the XHR progress and error paths rest on the browser; and the item card shows no thumbnail for an image. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still the inert drawer actions, the seed still creates no tags, `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and a verified Resend sending domain is still what makes the mail flows usable. Spec: @context/features/file-image-spec.md
- **2026-08-24 — Direct-to-R2 uploads with a 100 MB cap.** Uploads stopped passing through the app: `POST /api/upload` no longer *takes* the file, it *authorises* one — JSON metadata in, a 5-minute presigned PUT out — and the browser sends the bytes straight to the bucket. That is the whole point, since a proxied 100 MB body has to fit inside the platform's request-body limit (Vercel's is 4.5 MB, the constraint the previous entry left open) and is paid for while a serverless function holds it. Eighteen files, **no migration** and one dependency, `@aws-sdk/s3-request-presigner` (^3.1116.0, matching the S3 client already in the tree). **Both kinds are capped at 100 MB** — asked and answered before implementing, along with how hard the cap should be — so `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` states it once and the two `UploadConstraint` entries now differ only in their extension and MIME lists. **The cap is enforced in two independent places, which was the deliberate choice over trusting either alone.** `content-type` *and* `content-length` are signed into the URL, so R2 refuses a body of any other type or length; and `createItem` HEADs the object before attaching it, refusing a key with nothing behind it and keeping **R2's own size** rather than the browser's word for it. That second half let **`size` come out of the create payload entirely** — the bytes never pass through the app, so a size in the request could only ever be a claim, and a test now pins that a client-supplied one is dropped rather than merely ignored. `createItem` in `lib/db/items.ts` takes the confirmed file as a fourth argument instead of reading `input.file`, so the query layer never sees the unverified shape. **Two signing details worth keeping.** The presigner puts `content-type` in its *unsignable* set by default, so it must be named in `signableHeaders` to be enforced at all; `content-length` is signed by default once the command carries one, and is named anyway because a guarantee resting on a library default is a quiet thing. Both were confirmed empirically rather than assumed — a throwaway `tsx` script against fake credentials printed `X-Amz-SignedHeaders: content-length;content-type;host` with `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD`, which pins how many bytes may be stored, never which. **The `/feature browser` pass earned its keep by finding a bug no unit test could reach.** The presigned URL carried `x-amz-checksum-crc32=AAAAAA==` — the CRC32 of an **empty** body, because the SDK checksums every `PutObject` and a presigned command has no body to checksum. The browser would have PUT the real file against a checksum of nothing, and it lives in a query string the SDK generates, so there is no export to assert on. Fixed with **`requestChecksumCalculation: "WHEN_REQUIRED"`** on the client, re-inspected to confirm both checksum params are gone while the signed headers stay. **The bucket needs a CORS policy** allowing `PUT` with `Content-Type` from the app's origin, and this is the setting the original file-upload spec avoided by proxying — documented in `README.md` and `.env.example`, because its failure mode is an opaque `net::ERR_FAILED` with status 0 that reads exactly like a code bug. Three things learned getting it right: **R2 reports both a missing policy and a policy that no rule matches as `"CORS not configured for this bucket"`**, so a `GET`-only policy looks identical to no policy at all and a preflight probe (`curl -X OPTIONS` with `Origin` and `Access-Control-Request-Method`) is the quickest way to tell; **`AllowedHeaders` must list `Content-Type`** because that is the one header the client sets explicitly, while `Content-Length` is set by the browser and never appears in the preflight even though it is signed; and **`PutBucketCors` over the S3 API returns 403 `AccessDenied`** with the app's own credentials, since an object read/write token cannot change bucket configuration — the dashboard, or an Admin token nobody wants in `.env`, is the only route. Cloudflare's docs also note that R2 omits CORS headers from the error response for an *expired* URL, so the browser cannot read the reason; that plus the XML error body is why a failed PUT gets one generic message rather than a parsed one. **Verified against the real bucket, not by inspection.** Server-side, outside the browser: a body of exactly the signed length returns **200**, the same URL with a body 500 bytes longer returns **403**, a URL signed for 100 MB used to send 39 bytes returns **403**, a wrong content-type returns **403**, `headFile` returns the true size and `null` for a missing key, and the test object was deleted afterwards. In the browser, once CORS was right: an 8 MB upload showed `POST /api/upload → 200` then `PUT …r2.cloudflarestorage.com → 200` and nothing else, progress ran 0 → 24 → 72 → 100% with the `<progress>` element tracking it, the item stored **`fileSize: 8388608` exactly** with `fileUrl` holding the object key, `GET /api/items/[id]/file` returned 8388608 bytes of `text/markdown` (the type decided by the extension), and deleting the item removed the R2 object too — `headFile` → `null`, no orphan. A 100 MB + 1 byte file is refused client-side with "Files are limited to 100 MB." and no request leaves the page. Zero console errors or warnings, no overflow at 390px. **34 → 195 tests** (185 → 195 for this feature): the 100 MB boundary for both kinds, the presign body schema — which deliberately says nothing about the cap, since shape and rules are separate and the route runs both — and the four HEAD paths (normal, missing object, oversize, and an object R2 reports no length for). `putFile` is gone rather than left dead. **Left open:** an abandoned upload still orphans its object with no sweep, and that orphan can now be 100 MB; **downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`**, so a 100 MB file is a 100 MB serverless response on every view — the mirror image of what this fixes, and the obvious next feature (a presigned GET, or a public bucket behind a CDN); `R2_PUBLIC_URL` remains unused, and the schema's comment on `fileUrl` still says "Cloudflare R2 URL" when it holds a key; the edit drawer still cannot replace a file; and `FileUpload` and both route handlers stay **untestable by configuration**, so the XHR progress and error paths rest on the browser.
