Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still the inert drawer actions, the seed still creates no tags, `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
- **2026-08-24 — Image gallery view.** `/items/images` shows the pictures instead of rows of metadata: a three-column grid of 16:9 thumbnails, each cropped to fill, with a 5% zoom on hover. Three new files (`src/components/items/image-card.tsx`, `image-gallery.tsx`, `image-gallery-skeleton.tsx`), four edited, **no migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. This closes the "the item card shows no thumbnail for an image" line the file-upload entry left open. **The gate is `uploadKindFor(slug) === "image"`**, not a new constant — that predicate is already the app's answer to "does this type hold a picture", since both file types are `ContentType.FILE` and the column cannot say; `/items/files` therefore keeps its row cards, which was verified rather than assumed. **Scope is `/items/[type]` only**, decided rather than asked twice: the dashboard's Pinned and Recent lists mix types, and one grid cannot change shape per card, so a gallery there would have to switch per row. **Its own grid class rather than a variant of `.item-list`**, for two reasons that compound — that ladder is shared with the dashboard's two lists, and it is stated mobile-first (1 → 2 at 1024px → 3 at 1536px) where the gallery is stated **desktop-first** (3 → 2 at 1024px → 1 at 768px), because three columns are the point and the narrower steps are the concession. The consequence is that between 768–1024px the gallery is two columns where the row list is one, which is intended: a thumbnail survives a narrow column better than a title-plus-description row. **The Tailwind names in the spec are effects, not markup** — `aspect-video` and `object-cover` became `aspect-ratio: 16 / 9` and `object-fit: cover` in `globals.css`, per the house rule that JSX carries no utility classes. **The thumbnail source is `/api/items/[id]/file`** with a plain `<img>` and the lint rule disabled, exactly as the drawer's preview does it: `next/image` cannot serve it because the optimizer fetches server-side without the visitor's cookies and would take the route's own 401, and there is nothing to optimize since the response is per-account and uncacheable. **`ItemSummary` carries no `fileUrl`**, and rather than widen the shared select the missing-object case is caught by **`onError`** — which covers strictly more: an object gone from the bucket as well as a row that never had one. The fallback keeps the tile's 16:9 box and shows the type icon in the type colour. The card **reuses the row card's stretched `.item-card-open` button**, so hover and focus are stated through `:has` on the card and the skeleton, which has no button inside, still never looks interactive; the zoom is **dropped entirely under `prefers-reduced-motion`**, since snapping to a scale is still motion. **No new tests, and none are possible for this surface** — the diff is components plus CSS, and `vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`; the suite stayed at **195**, with `tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` clean, and the rules confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS (`aspect-ratio:16/9`, `object-fit:cover`, `transition:transform .3s`, `scale(1.05)`, all three column steps and the reduced-motion override). **Verified in the browser** as demo against three real image items the account already carried, so nothing was written to the database or to R2: 3 equal columns (`421.34px` × 3) at 1600px, computed `aspect-ratio: 16 / 9` against a measured 1.778 box, `object-fit: cover` clipping 21px off a portrait shot, `transform` going `none` → `matrix(1.05, 0, 0, 1.05, 0, 0)` → `none` across the 300ms transition and staying `none` at `0s` under reduced motion, the ladder read from `getComputedStyle` at 390/767/769/1023/1025/1440/1537 (1/1/2/2/3/3/3) with `scrollWidth - innerWidth === 0` at every one, the fallback exercised by aborting `**/api/items/*/file` through `page.route()` rather than by touching source, and a tile still opening the drawer with its preview and Download intact. Console clean on a fresh load; `/items/snippets` (7 row cards, 2 columns) and `/items/files` (1 row card) unchanged. **One environment note worth remembering:** a dev server was started by something outside this session *before* `.next` was deleted for the build, leaving it running on a directory that had been pulled out from under it — the exact setup behind the three phantom failures earlier entries record. It answered 200 and looked fine, and was restarted cleanly anyway rather than trusted; that is the cheap move every time. Also, `rm -rf .playwright-mcp` during artifact cleanup deleted **two tracked** `.yml` files committed back on 2026-08-18, before that path was gitignored — restored with `git checkout`, but a gitignored *path* does not mean the files in it are untracked. **Left open, and it wants a decision rather than a fix:** the portrait crop is aggressive — two of the three test images are portrait and one loses the top of its lettering entirely. That is `object-cover` doing what the spec asked for ("may crop edges"), so it is behaving correctly, but `object-fit: contain` with a letterboxed background, or a taller ratio, is a one-line change if it reads badly against real content. Also open: the gallery has no lightbox — a tile opens the drawer, which is where the full image lives. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still the inert drawer actions, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file` so a 100 MB file is a 100 MB serverless response, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, the seed still creates no tags, `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted. Spec: @context/features/image-display-spec.md
- **2026-08-25 — File list view.** `/items/files` reads as a file manager rather than a grid of cards: one row per file carrying an extension icon, the file name, its size, the upload date and a download button. Three new files (`src/components/items/file-row.tsx`, `file-list.tsx`, `file-list-skeleton.tsx`), six edited, **no migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. Every other type is untouched. **Two decisions were taken at load time, both from questions**, and both are the reason this was more than a CSS change. **A single `layout` variant, not a second boolean:** `TypeItems` took a `gallery: boolean` since the image gallery landed, and a third layout would have meant two flags that can contradict each other, so it now takes `ItemsLayout = "list" | "gallery" | "files"`, derived once by `itemsLayoutFor(slug)` in the same module and shared with the page's Suspense fallback through a local `ItemsSkeleton`. `uploadKindFor` is still what the derivation reads — both file types are `ContentType.FILE`, so the column cannot say which is a picture. **A dedicated select for the file branch:** the shared `itemSelect` carries no `fileName`, `fileSize` or `createdAt`, which is three of the five things a row shows, and widening it would have added three columns to the dashboard's Pinned and Recent queries, which display none of them — so `fileItemSelect` spreads it and `getFileItemsByType` runs against that. **`FileItemSummary` extends `ItemSummary` rather than replacing it**, which is what keeps the row openable: the drawer's `openItem` takes a summary and paints its header from one, so a shape of its own would have needed a second entry point. **The consequence is that the fork moved ahead of the query** — `TypeItems` used to run one `getItemsByType` and then pick a component, and now switches first, because the two branches return different shapes. **Ordered on `createdAt`, not `updatedAt`**: that is the date the row displays, and sorting on a column the list does not show puts the visible dates out of order; pinned still comes first, as everywhere. **The filename leads and the item title follows.** `item-create-form.tsx` does not default the title from the file, so the two usually differ — the spec asks for the file name, so that is the primary line, the title drops underneath when it says something the name does not, and the description takes that slot when they match. A row with no `fileName` (an item stored before uploads existed) falls back to the title and the generic icon. **The spec asked for `stopPropagation` on the download button and it is deliberately absent**, because there is nothing to stop: the hit target is the `.item-card-open` button *stretched over* the row as a **sibling**, not an ancestor, so a click on the link never bubbles to it. What actually makes the link work at all is `z-index: 2`, since that button otherwise covers it — the mechanism is recorded in a comment so the next reader does not add the no-op. A related nicety falls out of the existing `:has(.item-card-open:hover)` pattern: hovering the download button does *not* highlight the row, which is honest feedback that the click does something else. **Two things were caught and fixed before the build rather than shipped.** `text-overflow: ellipsis` on the `h3` did nothing — it is a flex container holding the name plus the pin/star flags, and a bare text node in one is an anonymous flex item that `text-overflow` does not reach; the truncation moved onto a `.file-row-name-text` span with `min-width: 0` on the row above it. And **eslint's `react-hooks/static-components` rejects a component produced by a call during render**, so a `fileIcon(name)` helper was refused twice — first as a function call, then as `FILE_ICONS.get(...) ?? File`; what passes is an indexed `Record`, which is also how `ItemCard` reads `TYPE_ICONS`. That record is a **plain object where the codebase's other lookup tables are `Map`s**, and the difference is real rather than an inconsistency: `file-constraints.ts` and `code-language.ts` are keyed on bare words, where `"constructor"` can answer off the prototype, but every key here carries a leading dot, as does everything `fileExtension` returns. **Verified**: `npm test` **195 passed** (unchanged — the diff is components, CSS and a query, and `vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`; the query's own logic is a `select` and an `orderBy`, which is Prisma's behaviour rather than ours), with `tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` clean, and every new rule confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS — including `.file-row-name-text`, the `z-index:2`, the `47.99rem` mobile block and the `::before` separator dot. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. The most likely thing to be off is the mobile wrap: the metadata line is aligned to `3.25rem` (icon width plus gap) by hand rather than by a grid, so it wants an eye at 390px. **One process note:** a dev server was live on port 3000 when the build was due and the build was **not** run against it — the user was asked and it was stopped first, which is the rule the three phantom `.next` failures earlier entries record. `.next` now holds a production build, so going back to `npm run dev` wants it deleted first. **Left open:** the row has no sort or filter and fetches every file item unbounded, the same shape as every other type page; there is no bulk selection or multi-file download; the size column shows "—" for an item with no `fileSize`, which is only reachable for rows predating uploads; and the dashboard's Recent list still shows file items as ordinary cards, since one grid there cannot change shape per row. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still the inert drawer actions, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file` so a 100 MB file is a 100 MB serverless response, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, the seed still creates no tags, `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted. Spec: @context/features/file-display-spec.md
- **2026-08-25 — Quick copy button on item cards.** An item card carries a Copy icon that puts the item's payload on the clipboard without opening the drawer. Three new files (`src/components/items/item-copy-button.tsx`, `src/lib/item-copy.ts` + its test), five edited, **no migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive** — `sonner` came with the rate limiting. **The decision that shaped everything is where the payload comes from.** `ItemSummary` carries no `content` or `url`, so the button **fetches the detail on click** through the existing `GET /api/items/[id]` — the same route the drawer uses — rather than widening the shared `itemSelect`, which would pull full snippet text (`@db.Text`) into every dashboard and type-page query and ship it to the browser for every row, copied or not. The cost is a round trip before the toast, **measured at ~475–520ms warm in dev against remote Neon** (the route itself 459/461ms), and during it the button is disabled but looks unchanged — a spinner or pressed state on `busy` is the fix if that ever reads as unresponsive. **The gate is the type slug, not `contentType`:** `ItemTypeSummary` carries no content type, and a file and an image are both `ContentType.FILE` whose payload is an R2 object key that means nothing on a clipboard — so `isCopyableType` joins `isCodeType` / `isMarkdownType` / `uploadKindFor` in `src/constants/item-types.ts`, deriving from `uploadKindFor` rather than becoming a fourth parallel list. Those two types get **no button rather than a dead one**. **The button is a sibling of the stretched `.item-card-open` hit target, not a child** — a button cannot nest in a button — with `z-index: 2` putting it on top, which is exactly the shape `file-row.tsx` already uses for its download link; a click never reaches the card, so **no `stopPropagation` is involved**, and hovering the button does not tint the card, which is the honest feedback that this click does something else. **`setBusy(false)` runs in a `finally`**, so a failed request cannot leave the button permanently dead — the identical defect that stranded the delete dialog (found in the browser) and later the edit form, and the reason it was written that way the first time here. `copyText` moved out of `item-drawer-actions.tsx` into `src/lib/item-copy.ts` so the drawer and the card share one rule rather than two that can drift. **6 new tests take the suite 195 → 201**, and the one that matters pins the `||`: an emptied content field is a field with nothing in it, and swapping the operator for `??` would return `""` and report a successful copy of nothing. **Proven non-vacuous rather than assumed** — making that swap failed exactly the two empty-string cases while the other four still passed, and it was reverted. CSS-wise the button is `opacity: 0` at rest, revealed by `.item-card:hover` (not the `:has(.item-card-open:hover)` the border tint uses, so hovering the button itself keeps it visible) and by its own `:focus-visible`; it is **still in the tab order while invisible**, so the focus rule is what makes it reachable rather than a convenience. The check is held at `opacity: 1` for its two seconds, since the pointer has usually left by then and a confirmation that vanishes with it confirms nothing. **Verified in the browser** as demo against the Neon dev branch, reading the DOM rather than screenshots: opacity `0 → 1 → 0 → 1` across rest / card hover / pointer leave / keyboard focus; the clipboard genuinely changed from a sentinel to the real snippet source, a link's `https://www.nrc.nl/` and a note's markdown; exactly one `GET /api/items/{id}` → 200 per click; `data-copied` held **2015ms** with the icon going `lucide-copy` → `lucide-check` → back and the colour `rgb(16,185,129)` (`--type-link`); the drawer never opened on a copy (including Enter on the focused button) while the card body still opened it on the right item. The dashboard's mixed Recent list is the decisive check for the gate — snippets 1/1, prompts 1/1, notes 2/2 with a button, files 0/3 and images 0/3 without — and across the type pages links 6/6, notes 2/2, prompts 4/4, commands 5/5, with `/items/files` and `/items/images` rendering their own components and zero copy buttons. **All four failure paths were exercised through `page.route()` with the source untouched**: 500, an aborted request, 401 and a 200 carrying empty content each produced the right message and, critically, **left the button re-enabled every time**. 390px showed `scrollWidth - innerWidth === 0` with the 28×28 button inside the card bounds and clear of the date; console clean, 0 errors and 0 warnings. Two screenshots were deleted afterwards — and note **`.playwright-mcp` holds two *tracked* `.yml` files** committed on 2026-08-18 before that path was gitignored, so artifact cleanup has to exclude them (a blanket `rm -rf` deleted them once before and needed `git checkout` to restore). **Three browser-tooling traps were recorded in `.claude/skills/feature/actions/browser.md`**, which is gitignored and so not in this commit: **never attach `page.on('request'/'response')` inside `browser_run_code_unsafe`** — it outlives the snippet, closes the MCP connection and resets the browser to `about:blank`, losing the signed-in session (it happened twice); wrap `window.fetch` through `page.evaluate` instead, re-installing after each navigation since it lives in the page; `route.fetch()` inside a handler that also calls `route.fulfill()` raises "Route is already handled!", so build a fixed body rather than relaying the real one; and **a generous `waitForTimeout` steps past transient state** — an 8-second wait read `data-copied: false` and no toast, which looked exactly like a silent failure when the copy had in fact succeeded and both had simply expired. **Left open:** there is no in-flight indicator during the ~0.5s fetch; the reveal is hover-based, so there is **no visible affordance on touch**, where the drawer's Copy stays the way in; each copy refetches with no detail cache, which is deliberate while nothing mutates an item but becomes worth revisiting alongside Favorite and Pin; and the button exists only on `.item-card`, so the file rows and gallery tiles have none. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still the inert drawer actions, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, the seed still creates no tags, `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
- **2026-08-26 — Audit fixes: the prune script's token filter and the duplicated item form fields.** A `code-scanner` pass over `src/`, `prisma/` and `scripts/` — run with the history's list of deliberate trade-offs as an explicit exclusion list, so it would not re-report fail-open rate limiting, the unused `R2_PUBLIC_URL`, Monaco's CDN, `consumeVerificationToken` still using `delete`, or `rankTypes` pulling every item row. **No High findings**, which is the honest result after the `auth-auditor` pass and the Redis migration: every route handler and server action already puts the session's `userId` in the Prisma `where`. Two things were real and both were fixed; four files changed, **no migration**, **no new dependency**, **no new ShadCN primitive**, and the suite stayed at **201 passing**. **The bug: `npm run db:prune` deleted the kept account's own password-reset token.** The exclusion was `identifier: { not: KEEP_EMAIL }`, but the reset flow namespaces its rows as `password-reset:<email>` (`passwordResetIdentifier` in `src/lib/password-reset.ts`), so demo's pending link did not equal `KEEP_EMAIL` and was swept with everyone else's — while a *verification* token for the same address, stored under the bare email, survived. `src/lib/account.ts` already gets this right for the account being deleted (`identifier: { in: [user.email, passwordResetIdentifier(user.email)] }`); the script simply never applied the mirror exclusion for the account being kept. **Confirmed live rather than by inspection, and the bug was present in the dev branch at the time**: it holds a `password-reset:demo@devstash.io` row left from the auth-audit testing, so the old filter deletes 3 tokens and the new one deletes 2. The fix is a shared `KEEPER_TOKENS` used by both the dry-run count and the delete, matching the bare address *or* anything ending in `:<address>` — which covers the current prefix and any later one, with the `:` stopping an address that merely *ends* in the kept one (`evildemo@devstash.io`) being preserved by suffix. **`passwordResetIdentifier` is deliberately not imported, and that was probed rather than assumed:** `@/lib/password-reset` reaches the Prisma singleton at import time, and imports hoist above the script's own `config({ path: [".env.local", ".env"] })` call, so importing it kills the run with `DATABASE_URL is not set` before `main()` starts. The `@/` alias itself resolves fine under `tsx` — it is the import-time side effect, not the path, that forbids it, and a comment in the script records that so nobody "tidies it up" into the import later. **The duplication: ~100 lines of form fields in two places.** `item-create-form.tsx` and `item-drawer-edit.tsx` each rendered the same Title / Description / Content-forking-into-`CodeEditor`-or-`MarkdownEditor`-or-textarea / URL / Language / Tags sequence. The CSS had been unified into shared `.item-form-*` classes when create landed; the JSX had not. New `src/components/items/item-form-fields.tsx` **takes `contentType` and derives the `show*` flags itself** rather than taking the flags, so the duplicated *derivation* went too, not just the markup; the value shape moved with it (`ItemFormValues`, `EMPTY_ITEM_FORM_VALUES`, `itemFormValuesFrom`) so the fields and both their constructors stay together. Create went 338 → 215 lines, edit 277 → 150, net **−283/+53** across the two. The three genuine differences are props: `idPrefix`, `urlRequired` (create demands a URL up front, edit does not, so an existing link stays saveable while its URL is being retyped) and `contentRows`. **No new tests, and none are possible** — both forms and the prune script sit outside what `vitest.config.mts` collects (`src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**` only), and the extraction is a pure move, so the existing 201 passing unchanged is what covers it. **Verified in the browser** on request, reading the DOM rather than screenshots: all seven types render the right fields and editors (snippets/commands → Monaco + Language, prompts/notes → the markdown Write/Preview tabs and no Language, links → a `required` URL, files/images → the upload zone alone); **`autoFocus` still lands on Title in both forms**, which was the one real risk of moving it inside a shared component; every `<label htmlFor>` resolves with no duplicate ids, and the Content label correctly drops its `htmlFor` on the editor branches; values survive a Snippet → Link → Snippet switch; a whitespace-only title still kills Create; the URL is `required` on create and **not** on edit, where clearing it leaves Save alive; the edit form populates from the stored row with tags as `""` rather than `undefined`; console clean (0 errors, 0 warnings) and no overflow at 390px in either form. **Neither form was submitted** — a write to the dev branch, which the browser pass is read-only by default about — but `handleSubmit` is byte-identical in both files (only the type annotation changed) and the DOM confirmed `values` holds exactly the right strings going in. **Two findings recorded and deliberately not acted on.** `contentRows` is **dead**: all four TEXT types in `ITEM_TYPE_OPTIONS` carry an editor (`snippets`/`commands` → code, `prompts`/`notes` → markdown), so `showCode || showMarkdown` is always true when `showContent` is and the plain-textarea branch is unreachable — confirmed `false` for all seven types in the browser. It predates this change, but the extraction has promoted it into a prop, which makes dead code look load-bearing; deleting the branch and the prop is the follow-up. And **"Could not reach the server. Try again." is declared 8 times** — `NETWORK_ERROR` in the four `src/components/auth/` forms, `UNREACHABLE` in four `src/components/items/` ones — same string, same purpose, worth one constant if either is touched again. **One correction to this history:** the repeated "the seed still creates no tags, so the tag round-trip is unexercised" line is now stale — the dev branch's NRC link item carries a `nieuws` tag, so it is reachable against real data. **One process note:** a dev server was started outside the session between the pre-build port check and the browser pass, and `.next` was deleted while it ran — the exact setup behind the three phantom failures earlier entries record. Next had already restarted its workers (the PID changed) and it answered correctly throughout, but the cheap move remains to restart rather than trust. Artifact cleanup removed only *untracked* files from `.playwright-mcp`, since two `.yml` files there were committed on 2026-08-18 before the path was gitignored and a blanket `rm -rf` has destroyed them once before. **Left open:** everything the previous entries list — Favorite and Pin are still the inert drawer actions, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, `/collections` still 404s, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
- **2026-08-26 — Collection create.** The top bar's **New Collection** button stops being display-only: it opens a modal with a name and an optional description, and creating one toasts and refreshes. Seven new files, four edited, **no migration** (`prisma/schema.prisma` untouched, so `db:status` was not run), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive** — `dialog`, `input`, `textarea`, `label` and `sonner` were all already in place. `Collection` has carried `name`, `description`, `isFavorite` and the `userId` relation since `20260814120000_init`, so the storage was already there. **Create is a server action, not an API route**, which was flagged at load and confirmed: the request asked for "api routes for any client-side calls", but in the items pattern that means client-side *reads* (the drawer's `GET /api/items/[id]`), while mutations are server actions — which is also what coding-standards mandates for form submissions. **The whole flow mirrors item create file-for-file**: `CollectionCreateDialog` (controlled `Dialog`, and Radix unmounting `DialogContent` on close is what makes the next open start empty rather than needing a reset) + `CollectionCreateForm` + `createCollection` in `src/actions/collections.ts` + `createCollection` in `src/lib/db/collections.ts` + `createCollectionSchema` in `src/lib/validations/collection.ts` + `CreateCollectionResult` in `src/types/collection.ts`. The form carries the `.catch(() => null)` the delete flow's browser pass had to discover the hard way — the action answers a failed *write* with `{ success: false }` but a failed *request* **rejects**, and without the catch `saving` never clears and the form is permanently unsubmittable. **The owner comes from the session, never the payload**, and a test pins it rather than the comment claiming it: a request naming `userId: "someone-else"` is stripped by the schema and the write still goes to the session's user. **`src/lib/db/collections.ts` got its first write function**, and adding it is what forced the one real refactor here — every export in that module narrows the `cache()`d `getCollections`, whose row→summary mapping was inline, so it was extracted into a shared `toSummary` typed with `Prisma.CollectionGetPayload<{ select: typeof collectionSelect }>` (the same shape `toDetail` in `items.ts` already uses). A new collection is empty, which is why the summary it returns carries `itemCount: 0` and no types. `cache()` is per-request memoization, so it cannot serve a stale list after the write. **`isFavorite` and `defaultTypeId` are deliberately not fields**: a new collection is not a favorite, and nothing in the app sets or reads a default type — both stay schema defaults rather than becoming things a request can name, and a test asserts the schema drops them. The **free tier's 3-collection limit is still not enforced** anywhere (project overview §8 gives all users full access during development), so it was left out rather than introduced here. **Name is capped at 100 rather than the item title's 200** — a collection name is a sidebar row label, not a sentence; description reuses the item schema's 500 and, like every optional text field in this project, stores `null` rather than `""` when emptied. **The CSS is grouped, not copied**: `.item-form, .collection-form` and `.item-form-field, .collection-form-field` share one declaration each and the section comment was rewritten to name all three forms, which is the same sharing-by-one-rule the markdown editor's frame uses; `.collection-form-error` joined `.item-drawer-error`, and `.collection-create-dialog` caps at `32rem` — narrower than the item dialog's `40rem` since there is no content box, and still beating the primitive's own `sm:max-w-sm` **without `!important`**, the unlayered-rules mechanism the sidebar's PRO badge has relied on since the Pro badge feature. `top-bar.tsx` **stays a server component**: the trigger button is constructed inside the client dialog and the caller passes a `label` string, because `DialogTrigger asChild` clones what it is given — the trap the profile-page improvements entry already recorded and reverted once. **12 new tests take the suite 201 → 213** — 6 over the schema (name trim, whitespace-only rejection, both caps, an emptied description becoming `null`, and the strip of anything beyond the two fields) and 5 over the action (session, an invalid payload not touching the database, the session's user winning over a caller-named one, the returned summary, and a rejected write becoming a message rather than a throw). `npm test`, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` are all clean, and the four new CSS rules were confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. **One process note:** a dev server was live on port 3000 when the build was due, and this time the check was *acted on* rather than printed — the previous feature's entry records a build running against a live server because nothing gated on the check. The user was asked, the process was killed, `.next` was deleted, and only then did the build run; the harness reported that background task as "failed with exit code 1", which was the kill and not a build problem. **Left open:** there is no way to add an item *to* a collection yet — `ItemCollection` rows are only ever created by the seed — and nothing edits, deletes, favorites or renames a collection; `/collections` still 404s, so the sidebar's "View all collections" link is unchanged; and creating is only possible from the top bar. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still the inert drawer actions, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
- **2026-08-26 — Add items to collections.** The create dialog and the drawer's edit mode both carry a collections checkbox list, so an item can be filed into any number of the collections the signed-in user owns — which closes the "there is no way to add an item *to* a collection" line the collection-create entry opened, and means `ItemCollection` rows are created by something other than the seed for the first time. Two new files (`src/app/api/collections/route.ts`, `src/components/items/item-collections-field.tsx`), twelve edited, **no migration** (`ItemCollection` has been in the schema since `20260814120000_init`, so `db:status` was not run), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. Collection *pages* were explicitly out of scope: `/collections` still 404s. **The join is an explicit model, and that is the whole shape of the write.** `ItemCollection` is `@@id([itemId, collectionId])` with an `addedAt` column, so it is **not** the implicit m2m the tags use — `set: []` + `connectOrCreate`, the pattern `updateItem` runs two lines below for tags, does not apply. Create writes `collections: { create: ids.map(...) }`. **Edit writes only the diff**, which is the decision worth keeping: `deleteMany: {}` followed by `create` would land the identical selection and **reset `addedAt` on every collection the item never left**, throwing away when it was filed there — so `updateItem`'s existing ownership read was widened to select the current `collectionId`s alongside `contentType` (no extra query), and the write deletes only what was dropped and creates only what was added. **Ownership is the security rule of the feature and it needed its own check.** Scoping the *item* to `userId` says nothing about the collections a payload names, so a crafted request could have filed its own item into another account's collection. `ownsAllCollections(userId, ids)` in `src/lib/db/collections.ts` counts the caller's own rows matching the ids and compares against the length; both `createItem` and `updateItem` call it before the write, the same place and for the same reason `createItem` already checks an upload key with `ownsObjectKey`. It short-circuits on an empty list, and **relies on the ids being deduplicated** — which is why the schema dedupes rather than that being cosmetic: a repeated id would make the count disagree with the length and refuse a legitimate request, quite apart from the repeated id attempting one join row twice and failing the whole write. **`GET /api/collections` is the first route outside `api/items/` and `api/auth/`**, and it is a client-side *read* — writes stayed server actions, per the architecture decision confirmed on the previous feature. Deliberately **outside the proxy's matcher**, for the reason `api/items/[id]/route.ts` records: the proxy answers an unauthenticated request with a redirect, which a `fetch` receives as an opaque 200 of HTML, so the check lives in the handler and says 401. It is served by a new `getCollectionOptions(userId)` — **its own narrow `{ id, name }` query rather than a slice of the `cache()`d `getCollections`**, which pulls every item row of every collection just to rank the types each one holds; a picker needs none of that. **The field fetches its own options instead of taking them as props.** Both forms need the same list and neither has it — the create dialog is opened from a server component and the drawer's edit mode from a client one — so props would have meant two different routes to the same data. Fetching on mount is also what makes a collection created since the page loaded appear in the list, since Radix unmounts both forms on close. The effect carries an `active` flag so a response landing after the form has gone is dropped. It renders **real checkboxes inside their labels**, the same shape the item type picker uses for its radios, so the group keeps its keyboard behaviour and focus lands somewhere visible; the group's `Label` takes `htmlFor={undefined}`, the precedent the Content label already sets over an editor. Loading, failed and empty are three separate states, and **the empty one does not block anything** — a brand-new account owns no collections and must still be able to create an item. **`ItemFormValues` gained its first non-string field**, `collectionIds: string[]`. Rather than widening `setField` to `string | string[]`, its key type narrowed to a new exported `TextField = Exclude<keyof ItemFormValues, "collectionIds">` and an explicit `setCollectionIds` was added beside it — so `setField("collectionIds", …)` now fails to compile rather than silently storing a string where an array belongs. `itemFormValuesFrom` maps the edit form's starting selection out of `detail.collections`, which `itemDetailSelect` has always carried and the drawer already rendered as chips; the read side needed nothing. `collectionIdsSchema` joined the shared `itemFields`, so create and edit accept it from one definition: **an absent field is an empty selection rather than an error** (the forgiving shape `file` already has on create), and it dedupes, drops empties and caps at 50. **Two existing test files needed a one-line fixture change each** and neither is a new test: `src/lib/db/items.test.ts`'s `CreateItemInput` helper gained `collectionIds: []` or it stopped type-checking, and `src/actions/items.test.ts` needed a `vi.mock("@/lib/db/collections")` — the action's new import otherwise drags `@/lib/prisma` in, which throws at import time without a `DATABASE_URL`, and it failed the whole file rather than an assertion. **No new tests were written, at the user's explicit request** ("I let you write new tests later on"), so the suite is unchanged at **213 passing** and the genuinely testable surface added here is uncovered: `collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and the two actions' refusal of a foreign collection id. That is the outstanding work on this feature. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` are clean, and the four new CSS rules were confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. **One process finding worth more than the usual `.next` note:** the first build ran while port 3000 was **still listening**, because stopping the dev server through the harness's `TaskStop` killed the `npm run dev` wrapper and left the Next child alive — so the task reported success while the server did not actually stop. That build was discarded and redone with the port confirmed free by `netstat`, which is the only check that has proven reliable here. Stopping a dev server by task id is not sufficient on this platform; confirm the port. **Left open:** an item can be filed into a collection but there is still nothing that *shows* a collection's contents — `/collections` and `/collections/[id]` do not exist, so the sidebar's "View all collections" link still 404s and a collection card is not clickable; nothing edits, renames, deletes or favorites a collection; there is no way to add an item to a collection from the drawer's *view* mode; and the free tier's 3-collection cap is still unenforced. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still the inert drawer actions, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
- **2026-08-26 — Collection pages.** `/collections` and `/collections/[id]` exist, which closes the "`/collections` still 404s" line that has been in every entry since the sidebar first linked to it back in the stats-and-sidebar feature — the oldest open item in this history. Six new files, six edited, **no migration** (`prisma/schema.prisma` untouched, so `db:status` was not run), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. **The largest finding was that the linking half of the request needed no code at all.** `CollectionCard` has always rendered `href={/collections/${collection.id}}`, both sidebar collection lists have always linked to the same, and "View all collections" has always pointed at `/collections`; all four 404ed purely because the routes did not exist. They were verified rather than rewritten, and the diff touches none of them — worth remembering before "add a link to X" is taken at face value in this codebase, since the cards were written link-first from the start. `/collections` renders the existing `CollectionGrid` over **`getCollections` unsliced**, which needed only an `export`: it was already the `cache()`d unbounded read that `getRecentCollections`, `getSidebarCollections` and `getCollectionStats` each narrow, and the comment on it now records that being unbounded for the sidebar's sake is exactly what makes it the whole-page read too. **Two new reads, and which module each lives in is the decision worth keeping.** `getCollection(userId, id)` sits in `lib/db/collections.ts` and runs the shared `collectionSelect`, so the heading gets the item count and the ranked types without a second query; `userId` is in the `where`, so another account's collection comes back `null` rather than forbidden — the same conflation `getItemDetail` and `updateItem` already make, so an id nobody may see is never confirmed to exist — and the page turns that into `notFound()`, which is also what an id naming nothing gets. `getCollectionItems(userId, collectionId)` deliberately went into **`lib/db/items.ts` instead**, because the shape it returns is an item's and `itemSelect` plus the mapping that narrows it belong to that module; putting it in `collections.ts` would have meant exporting both across the boundary. It filters on the owner **independently of the collection** (`where: { userId, collections: { some: { collectionId } } }`), so being handed a collection id that is not the caller's still cannot read another account's items — the two conditions are belt and braces rather than one implying the other. **Items are ordered `isPinned desc, updatedAt desc`**, decided by the user ("order on date for now") and read as the item's *own* date: that is the one the card actually prints, so the visible dates run in order down the page, and it is exactly what `getItemsByType` uses, so a collection page and a type page sort identically. `ItemCollection.addedAt` was the other honest candidate and is left unused — it records when an item was filed into the collection, which nothing displays, so sorting on it would look arbitrary on screen; revisit if an "added" column or a sort control ever lands. **A collection mixes item types, so only `ItemList` can render it.** `/items/[type]` picks between list, gallery and file-row layouts through `itemsLayoutFor`, but that switch is per *type* and a collection holds several at once — the same reason the dashboard's Recent list has always shown file items as ordinary cards. `TypeItems` was left completely alone. **`CollectionGridSkeleton` was extracted** out of `dashboard-skeletons.tsx` into `src/components/collections/` now that it has a second consumer, which is precisely what happened to `ItemListSkeleton` when the items list view gave it one; `CollectionsSectionSkeleton` is now a section, a title and a call to it. Both pages follow the shape every other route in the app uses: `force-dynamic`, a `getCurrentUser()` null-check redirecting to `SIGN_IN_PATH` (covering what the proxy cannot — a token that still verifies against a deleted account), a `<Suspense>` boundary so the heading paints while the data resolves, and `generateMetadata` on the detail page. `/collections` and `/collections/:path*` joined the proxy matcher. The only new CSS is `.collection-heading-types`, a flex row for the type icons under the heading, which reuses the card's existing icon rule for the colouring. **7 new tests take the suite 213 → 220**, in two files: `collections.test.ts` covers `getCollection` (the owner scoping, `null` rather than a throw, the item count, and the most-common-first type ranking that decides the card's colour), and **`collection-items.test.ts` is deliberately its own file rather than joining `items.test.ts`** — that one mocks a Prisma client carrying only `item.create`, and widening it would change what every test in it is allowed to reach. **The ownership test was mutation-checked rather than trusted**: dropping `userId` from the query failed exactly that one test and nothing else, and the change was reverted with `git diff` confirming the file clean. `npm test`, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` are all clean, and the new rule was confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. **The process note from the previous entry is now confirmed rather than suspected: `TaskStop` does not stop the dev server.** It reported success a second time while the Next child kept listening on port 3000 (PID 15472), so a build would again have shared `.next` with a live server. The reliable sequence is `TaskStop`, then read the PID out of `netstat` and `taskkill //PID <pid> //F` in a loop until the port is genuinely free, and only then build. **`src/app/collections/layout.tsx` is the fourth three-line `AppShell` wrapper** (after dashboard, items and profile), which is now the strongest the case for the `(app)` route group project overview §7 targets has been — it would absorb all four, and it remains deferred as its own change because it is a route move touching typed-route generics. **Left open:** the read side is done and the write side is not — nothing renames, edits, deletes or favorites a collection, there is no way to remove an item from one, and neither page has a create button (a collection is still made only from the top bar); an item can be added to a collection only through the item forms, not from the drawer's view mode or from a collection's own page; both pages fetch unbounded with no sort, filter or pagination, the same shape every type page has; and the free tier's 3-collection cap is still unenforced. The testable surface the previous feature left uncovered is **still uncovered** — `collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id. Everything else the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still the inert drawer actions, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, and no CI runs the suite.
- **2026-08-26 — Collection actions: edit, delete and favorite.** `/collections/[id]` carries a Favorite / Edit / Delete row beside its heading, and the collection cards on `/collections` and the dashboard carry a three-dots dropdown with the same three actions — clicking anywhere else on a card still opens the collection. Four new files, thirteen edited, **no migration** (`prisma/schema.prisma` untouched, so `db:status` was not run), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**: `dropdown-menu` came with the sidebar's account menu, `dialog` with the profile improvements, `alert-dialog` with the profile page and `sonner` with the rate limiting. **The requirement that items survive a collection's deletion needed no code**, and that is the first thing worth knowing: `ItemCollection.collection` is `onDelete: Cascade` and an `Item` carries no foreign key to a collection, so deleting the row takes only the join rows with it. The confirmation says so in as many words (“The items in it are **not** — they stay in your stash”) rather than leaving the reader to trust it. **The card had to be turned inside out**, which was the largest change here and was flagged at load: it was a `<Link>` wrapping its whole body, and a button cannot nest in an anchor. The link is now a stretched, invisible **sibling** at `z-index: 1` with the menu above it at `z-index: 2` — the shape `item-card.tsx` and `file-row.tsx` already use, so a click on the menu never reaches the link and **no `stopPropagation` is involved**. Hover and focus moved to `:has(.collection-card-open:hover)`, which lands the state on the card rather than on the transparent link over its text, keeps the skeleton (which has no link inside) from ever looking interactive, and has the deliberate side effect that **hovering the menu button does not tint the card** — honest feedback that this click does something else. `collection-grid-skeleton.tsx` was reshaped to match, since the real card is now the `li` itself rather than a `div` inside one. **Both dialogs are controlled and carry no trigger of their own.** A `DialogTrigger` inside a `DropdownMenuItem` is unmounted along with the menu the moment an item is selected, so `CollectionEditDialog` and `CollectionDeleteDialog` take `open` / `onOpenChange` and each caller renders its own button — which is also what lets the page's action row and the card's menu drive the same two components identically. One piece of machinery earns its place: the menu tracks an `openingDialog` ref and calls `event.preventDefault()` in `onCloseAutoFocus`, because Radix returns focus to the trigger as the menu closes and that lands in the same commit as the dialog mounting, taking focus straight back out of it. **The create form became the shared `CollectionForm`** (`git mv`, so history follows) taking an optional `collection` prop: create and edit write the same two fields under the same rules, so a second form would have been the first one copied with a different action at the bottom and free to drift from it. Field ids come from `useId()` rather than a literal, since a card's edit dialog and the top bar's create dialog can be mounted at once. `createCollectionSchema` and `updateCollectionSchema` are likewise both built from one `collectionFields` object, the same sharing `itemFields` already does. **Ownership is in the `where` on both writes**, so another account's collection is *missing* rather than forbidden — the conflation `getCollection`, `updateItem` and `deleteItem` all make. `deleteCollection` uses **`deleteMany` guarded on its `count`**, so a double-click or two tabs racing reads as “already gone” instead of throwing `P2025` — the fix the item delete and the reset flow already carry, and which `consumeVerificationToken` still lacks. `updateCollection` has no second query to guard with, so it **catches `P2025` and answers `null`, rethrowing everything else**: swallowing the rest would report a dropped connection as a collection that does not exist. That catch is what turned `import type { Prisma }` into a value import in `src/lib/db/collections.ts`, since `instanceof` needs the runtime class. **The id travels as its own action argument, never inside the payload**, so a request can name neither the collection it edits nor its owner — a test pins both by sending `userId` and `id` in the body and asserting they are dropped. **Favorite is the icon and the button only**, per the spec: `disabled` with a `Coming soon` title, the precedent the drawer's own Favorite and Pin set. Nothing writes `isFavorite`. **17 new tests take the suite 220 → 237** across three files — the owner scoping on both writes, `P2025` → `null` against a rethrow of anything else, `deleteMany`'s count deciding true/false, the two actions' session / validation / missing / returned-value / rejected-write paths, and one parity test over `updateCollectionSchema` that fails if the two schemas are ever pulled apart without the edit rules being restated. `npm test`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` are all clean, and the new rules were confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS, `:has()` selectors intact. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. Two things want an eye on first run: whether the dropdown → dialog focus handoff behaves as intended (the `onCloseAutoFocus` guard above is written for it but unobserved), and the three-button row at 390px, where `.action-label` collapses the labels to icons. **One process note:** a dev server was live on port 3000 when the build was due; the user was asked, the PID was killed, **the port was confirmed free with `netstat` before building** — the check the previous two entries record as the only reliable one, since `TaskStop` reports success while leaving the Next child listening — and `.next` was deleted first. `.next` now holds a production build, so going back to `npm run dev` wants it deleted first. **Left open:** favorite is the obvious next feature and needs a server action plus a revalidation story, and the same is true of the item drawer's Favorite and Pin, which have been inert since it landed; there is still no way to *remove a single item* from a collection, and no create button on either collections page (a collection is still made only from the top bar); renaming from the sidebar does not exist; and the free tier's 3-collection cap is still unenforced. The testable surface two features back is **still uncovered** — `collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id. Everything else the previous entries list stays open: the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
- **2026-08-26 — Settings page.** `/settings` exists, and the two account actions moved there from `/profile`: **Change Password** and **Delete Account**, each behind the dialog it already had. Three new files (`src/app/settings/layout.tsx`, `page.tsx`, `src/components/settings/settings-account.tsx`), three moved, five edited, **no migration** (`prisma/schema.prisma` untouched, so `db:status` was not run), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive** — `dialog`, `alert-dialog` and `dropdown-menu` were all already in `components/ui/`. **The spec named "forgot password", which the profile page does not have**: its two actions are `ChangePasswordDialog` and `DeleteAccountDialog`, while the forgot-password flow is the pre-auth `/forgot-password` page and was never on the profile. Flagged at load and confirmed to leave it alone, so only the two real actions moved. **Protected twice**, the shape every authenticated page in this app uses: `/settings` and `/settings/:path*` joined the proxy matcher, *and* the page redirects to `SIGN_IN_PATH` when `getCurrentUser()` returns null — the one case the proxy cannot see, a token that still verifies against a row that is gone, which is exactly what deleting an account leaves behind if the sign-out after it ever failed. **The three components moved with `git mv`** into `src/components/settings/` (93%/100%/100% rename similarity in the merge, so history follows) and their behaviour is byte-identical — same `src/actions/profile.ts` server actions, same 10-per-15-minutes limiter keyed on the user id, same password-fingerprint sign-out, same `VerificationToken` cleanup under **both** identifiers on delete. **`src/actions/profile.ts` and `src/types/profile.ts` deliberately stayed put**: they act on the *account*, which is true wherever the button lives, and renaming them to `account.*` would collide with `src/lib/account.ts`, which holds the mutations themselves. **The card frame is one grouped rule, not a copy** — `.profile-card, .settings-card` and `.profile-card-title, .settings-card-title`, the same sharing-by-one-rule `.code-editor, .markdown-editor` uses — while `.profile-actions` / `-note` were *renamed* into a new Settings section rather than left behind, so `grep` finds nothing dead. **A follow-up request reshaped the sidebar's account row**, and it is the part worth remembering: Profile joined Settings in the dropdown, and the name stopped being a link. That name sat *outside* the trigger only because it linked to `/profile` and a link cannot live inside a button — with the link gone the constraint went too, so the trigger now covers the whole row (`display: grid` around the avatar → `display: flex; flex: 1`), the name is a plain span, and `.sidebar-user-meta` lost the `flex: 1` it no longer needs. The dead `a.sidebar-user-name:hover` underline and the span-only `color: inherit` / `text-decoration: none` went with it, and **`aria-label="Account menu"` was dropped**: the button now contains the name and email, so it has a real accessible name, and keeping the override would have hidden the visible label from it (the avatar stays `aria-hidden`, so "DU" is not part of it). One CSS consequence that would have been a visible bug: the collapsed 3.5rem rail centres `.sidebar-user`, and a `flex: 1` trigger fills that row — so `.sidebar-user-trigger` had to join the rail's centring `:is(...)` list or the avatar would have sat against the left edge. Menu order is Profile · Settings · separator · Sign out. **No new tests, and none are possible for this surface** — the diff is pages, components and CSS, and `vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`; the suite stayed at **237 passing**, which is what covers the move. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` clean, and the new rules were confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS. **Verified in the browser** as demo against the Neon dev branch, reading the DOM rather than screenshots: anonymous `/settings` and `/settings/anything` both 307 to `/sign-in` with their own `callbackUrl`; the page renders inside the shell with both buttons; **submitting a wrong current password returned "That is not your current password." and re-enabled the button**, which is what proves the moved form is still wired to its action, and reopening showed no stale error and empty fields (Radix unmount-on-close); the delete dialog names `demo@devstash.io` and was **cancelled, never confirmed**; `/profile` shows zero Change Password buttons, zero Delete Account buttons and zero `.profile-actions` nodes while keeping its identity card and the 7/4/5/2/3/3/6 breakdown; the account row holds **zero `<a>` elements**, the name is a `SPAN`, clicking *the name* opens the menu, the trigger spans 224px of the 232px row, Enter opens it with focus landing on Profile and Escape returns focus to the trigger; both menu links navigate; and the collapsed rail is 56px with the meta hidden and the avatar centred to the pixel (offset `0`). 0 console errors or warnings throughout, no overflow at 390px, where the two buttons stack. Screenshots deleted afterwards — and note `.playwright-mcp` holds two **tracked** `.yml` files committed on 2026-08-18, so cleanup must skip them. **`TaskStop` again reported success while the Next child kept listening on port 3000**, the third entry in a row to record it: the reliable sequence is `TaskStop`, then read the PID out of `netstat` and `taskkill` in a loop until the port is genuinely free, and only then build. **`src/app/settings/layout.tsx` is the fifth three-line `AppShell` wrapper** (dashboard, items, profile, collections), which is the strongest the case for the `(app)` route group in project overview §7 has been — it would absorb all five, and it stays deferred as its own change because it is a route move touching typed-route generics. **Left open:** the page's own null-user redirect is **not verifiable read-only** — it needs a session token whose row is gone — and neither is the GitHub-only branch (the muted "no password to change" note), since demo has a password and no GitHub credentials were to hand; the settings page holds nothing but the account card, so theme, export and the other things a settings page usually carries are still absent; and at 1440px the card description wraps with "it." alone on the second line, which is `max-width: 44rem` behaving correctly rather than a defect. Everything the previous entries list stays open: favorite is still inert on collections and on the item drawer along with Pin, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, no CI runs the suite, and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature — `collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id — is still uncovered.
- **2026-08-27 — Global search command palette.** A ⌘K / Ctrl+K palette searches the signed-in user's items and collections, which turns the top bar's search field — display-only since the dashboard's phase 1 shell — into a real control. Eleven new files (ten source plus the spec), eight edited, **no migration** (`prisma/schema.prisma` untouched, so `db:status` was not run). One dependency: **`cmdk` (^1.1.1)**, pulled by `shadcn add command`, which created **two** `components/ui/` files rather than one — `command.tsx` and the `input-group.tsx` its `CommandInput` uses (the 14th and 15th) — and is the first primitive here to bring a real dependency, every earlier one riding the unified `radix-ui` the nova preset installed. **Search runs on the server, which is a deviation from the spec and was asked for at `/feature start`.** The spec's Technical section says "Client-side fuzzy search (no server round-trips)" and "Pre-fetch searchable data on app load"; the request overrode both, and the consequences were flagged before implementing. **It is not fuzzy** — Prisma's `contains` compiles to Postgres `ILIKE`, which is case-insensitive substring and yields no score, so results keep the app's usual ordering (pinned first, then most recent) and real ranking would mean the `pg_trgm` extension and a migration. **Nothing is pre-fetched**: `GET /api/search?q=` is queried on a 200 ms debounce, so `AppShell` stays synchronous (the skeleton feature made it so deliberately) and no client snapshot can go stale after a create or delete. What the server buys in exchange is that **content is searched in full** rather than only to a preview cut-off, which the client-side design could not have done — verified live, where three of four hits for "react" matched on content rather than title. **`searchItems` matches across every field that carries meaning**: title, description, the payload — whichever of `content`, `url` or `fileName` the item's type uses — its tags, and its **type's name**, so "command" finds the user's commands, which is what project overview §3C means by types being searchable. `userId` is in the `where` rather than a check on the result, as everywhere else in `lib/db/`. Two things are recorded in comments rather than fixed: Prisma does not escape `%` or `_` inside `contains`, so a query carrying one broadens the match (parameterized either way — it widens results, it does not inject), and matching the type name can crowd a short query's results since there is no relevance to rank by. **`searchCollections` uses its own narrow select with `_count`**, deliberately not the shared `collectionSelect`, which pulls every item row of every collection just to rank the types it holds — a result row shows a name and a count and needs neither. **A result carries a whole `ItemSummary` plus a preview**, not the spec's narrower `(id, title, type, content preview)`. Reading the drawer showed it only actually consumes `id`, `title`, `type`, `isFavorite` and `isPinned` off the summary — `description`, `tags` and `updatedAt` are in the type because that is what a *card* carries — so passing the whole thing means `openItem(item)` works verbatim with **no change to the drawer at all**, and costs nothing extra since the same select already returns those fields. `content` is the only addition, **sliced to a 160-character preview server-side** with whitespace collapsed, so a snippet's newlines do not render as a long gap in a one-line row and the full `@db.Text` never reaches the browser. **`updatedAt` leaves as an ISO string** (the conversion `getItemDetail` already makes for the same reason) and the palette revives it with `new Date()` before handing it over — the type says so, and a test pins which side is which. **`cmdk` gets `shouldFilter={false}`**: the server decides the result set, and cmdk's own scoring would drop rows that matched on content it cannot see. **`ItemDrawerProvider` moved up one level in `app-shell.tsx`** to wrap the top bar as well as `main`, since the palette lives up there and opens items with it; the `Sheet` portals to `body`, so tree position affects only who can reach the context. **eslint's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rejected the obvious shape** — synchronous `setState` in an effect body — and the fix is the better design: the response is stored **keyed to the query it answers** (`{ query, status, results }`), so comparing it against the box says whether it is current, nothing has to be cleared as the query changes, and the previous results stay on screen while the next are in flight rather than flickering through an empty list on every keystroke. Staleness is handled by aborting on effect cleanup, so a slower earlier response cannot land on top of a later one — no request counter needed, unlike the drawer's. **No rate limiting on `/api/search`**, matching the other authenticated read routes (`/api/collections`, `/api/items/[id]`); the debounce and the two-character minimum are the brake, and the minimum exists because a single character matches most of a stash and would return the first `take` rows rather than anything the caller meant. **16 new tests take the suite 237 → 253** across two files: `searchQuerySchema` (the trim running *before* the length check, so a whitespace-only query is too short rather than a search for a space) and a dedicated `src/lib/db/search.test.ts` — its own file because search spans two modules and each of theirs mocks a client carrying only what its own subject reaches — covering the owner scoping on both queries, the exact `OR` list, and the five preview rules (collapse, truncate, the content → url → fileName fallback, null with no payload, null when it collapses to nothing). **A `/feature browser` pass verified every goal and found two real bugs.** The debounce was measured rather than assumed — five keystrokes produced exactly **one** request — and the interaction most likely to break behaved: selecting a result closes a Radix Dialog and opens a Radix Sheet in the same tick, and the `body` `pointer-events` lock stays only while the drawer is open (correct, it is modal) then clears to `auto`, with the top bar button confirmed genuinely hit-testable again. Keyboard navigation crosses the group boundary from Items into Collections, the type icon resolves `rgb(59,130,246)` through the portal (the bare `data-type` map the drawer feature already had to un-scope), and a collection row reads "3 items". **Bug one: the palette went edge-to-edge at 390px.** `.search-palette { max-width: 40rem }` beat not just the primitive's `sm:max-w-sm` but its `max-w-[calc(100%-2rem)]` mobile gutter, so it sat 0→390 with no inset; now `min(40rem, calc(100% - 2rem))`, measured at 24px gutters on mobile and 639.998px at 1440. The override is load-bearing either way — this shadcn version caps at `sm:max-w-sm`, which is 384px. **Bug two: a stray `<h2>Search</h2>` on every authenticated page.** shadcn's `CommandDialog` renders `DialogHeader` as a **sibling** of `DialogContent`, and a Radix dialog root renders its children whether it is open or not, so mounting the palette in the shell left an sr-only, non-`aria-hidden` heading and description in the accessibility tree of every page. Moved inside `DialogContent`, where Radix expects the title anyway — **the second edit ever to a `ui/` file** (after `sonner.tsx`), commented in place, and re-verified: nothing stray when closed, and both `aria-labelledby` and `aria-describedby` resolve when open. Zero console errors or warnings across the whole pass, no overflow at 390px. **One tooling note worth remembering: a quoted heredoc through the Bash tool silently failed to write, twice**, dying with "unexpected EOF while looking for matching `''" while leaving the *previous* version of the file in place — which reads exactly like an edit that did not take. Both bodies contained apostrophes in prose or comments (`a snippet's newlines`), which appears to be the trigger despite `<<'EOF'` being quoted. The Write tool handled both first time. Check the file after a large heredoc, or use Write for anything with prose in it. Also, killing the dev server by PID from `netstat` worked directly this time, and the harness reported the background task as "failed with exit code 1" — that is the kill, not a build problem, as earlier entries record. **Left open:** results are capped at 10 items and 5 collections with no "see all" and no pagination; there is no relevance ranking, which is the one thing `pg_trgm` would actually fix along with real fuzzy matching; the palette is search-only despite the name — it runs no commands, has no recent-searches memory, and offers no "create item" action; and the `⌘ K` hint is Mac-flavoured on every platform. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still inert on the item drawer and on collections, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature — `collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id — is still uncovered. Spec: @context/features/global-search-spec.md
- **2026-08-27 — Search palette: browse list and live results.** The palette opens on content rather than on a prompt — every item, then every collection underneath — and narrows to matches once there is enough of a query to be one. **No new file, no dependency, no migration**; eight source files edited, +243/−89, all of it a change to what shipped the same day. Loaded inline. **Four decisions were answered before implementing.** The browse cap is **30**, and **the search cap rose to match** — one `RESULT_LIMIT = 30` in the route replacing the split `ITEM_LIMIT = 10` / `COLLECTION_LIMIT = 5`, so typing cannot appear to *shrink* a list that was already showing everything. The debounce **stays** at 200 ms. The **two-character floor stays**, and the reading that made the rest coherent was stated explicitly rather than assumed: searching begins at 2 characters and *below that the browse list is what shows*, so the "Type at least 2 characters to search." state is **gone entirely** rather than turned into an error, and a first letter is never a dead end. And the shape is the **hybrid** — browse on open, server search on type — which keeps full-content matching, the thing the server-side deviation bought in the first place. **`searchQuerySchema` lost its minimum** and keeps only the 100-character cap: a short query is a request to browse, not a bad request, so the *route* decides which it is by comparing against `MIN_QUERY_LENGTH`, and the endpoint's only remaining 400 is an over-long query. That inverted what the schema's tests assert — they now pin that every length below the floor **parses**, which is the rule that keeps a one-character query from 400ing instead of listing. **`searchItems` and `searchCollections` take `string | null` and omit the `OR` key entirely when there is none**, rather than passing an empty one — **Prisma reads `OR: []` as matching nothing**, so an empty array would have opened the palette on a blank list, and that is the one mistake here that would have looked like a backend outage rather than a bug. It is expressed as a spread of `{}` for exactly that reason, and two tests assert the absent key rather than merely the returned rows. Browsing is the same function as searching rather than a second one, because the ordering, the select and the preview mapping are identical either way — and browse keeps the search ordering (pinned first, then most recent) so the list does not reshuffle as the query crosses the floor. **The palette keys its state on an *effective* query** — the empty string below the floor — rather than on the raw one, so typing a first letter does not refetch the list it is already showing; and the **browse fetch skips the debounce** (`delay = effective ? DEBOUNCE_MS : 0`), since the palette opens on it and there is no keystroke still coming. The stale-while-revalidate behaviour from the previous entry carries over unchanged, so the browse list stays on screen while a search is in flight. **Two UI details the change forced.** `.search-palette [data-slot="command-list"]` raises the list from the primitive's `max-h-72` — about six rows, right for a handful of results and far too short for a 35-row browse list — to `min(24rem, 50vh)`, bounded by the viewport because the dialog is anchored a third of the way down and a fixed height would run off a short screen. And the empty state went from two cases to **four**, since browsing and searching fail differently: loading-browse, loading-search, a search that matched nothing, and an empty stash, which gets "Nothing saved yet" rather than being told its own name did not match. **3 new tests take the suite 253 → 256** (the two browse cases plus the collections one), and the four schema tests were rewritten rather than deleted, since the contract they describe inverted. `npm test`, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` are all clean, and both new CSS rules were confirmed present after Lightning CSS — which **drops the quotes around an attribute value**, so `[data-slot="command-list"]` ships as `[data-slot=command-list]` and a grep pattern written against the source form finds nothing. That cost a rebuild to establish and is worth remembering for the next one of these checks. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. Two things want an eye on first run: whether a single typed letter showing the *unfiltered* list reads as the keystroke being ignored (the alternative reading of the floor — 1 character to search, with only type-name matching requiring 2 — is a small change if so), and whether 30 items plus 30 collections is comfortable to scan at `min(24rem, 50vh)`. **Left open:** **every open now costs a request** for up to 30 items with previews, where before nothing was fetched until two characters were typed — the preview is sliced server-side so the payload is bounded, but there is no cross-open cache and reopening refetches; there is still no relevance ranking, which remains what `pg_trgm` would fix along with real fuzzy matching; and the palette is still search-only despite the name — no commands, no recent searches, no "create item" action. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still inert on the item drawer and on collections, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature — `collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id — is still uncovered.
- **2026-08-27 — Pagination.** `/items/[type]`, `/collections/[id]` and `/collections` show one page at a time with a numbered strip and prev/next underneath, and every paginated read uses `skip` / `take` instead of fetching a whole stash and slicing it. Three new files (`src/constants/pagination.ts`, `src/lib/pagination.ts`, `src/components/layout/pagination.tsx`) plus the spec, twelve edited, **no migration** (`prisma/schema.prisma` untouched, so `db:status` was not run), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. **Three decisions were answered before implementing.** The spec named `/items/[type]` and `/collections/[id]` as the pages to paginate but then defined `COLLECTIONS_PER_PAGE` — and `/collections/[id]` lists *items*, so that constant had no home; read as **all three pages**, with `ITEMS_PER_PAGE` on the two item listings and `COLLECTIONS_PER_PAGE` on `/collections`. A `?page=` past the end **404s** rather than clamping or rendering an empty page, matching how `/items/banana` already treats a URL that names nothing. And the shared `getCollections` read was **left alone**, with a bounded query added beside it. **The one shape everything else follows from: the count is hoisted out of the Suspense boundary.** Validating `?page=99` inside it would throw `notFound()` *after* the heading had already flushed — the same streaming property the auth-audit entry records for `/dashboard` answering 200 rather than 307 for a fingerprint-rejected session — so `countItemsByType`, `countCollectionItems` and `countCollections` are `cache()`d, awaited in the page, and the 404 happens before anything renders. That has three knock-on benefits: the `<Pagination>` controls sit **outside** the boundary and paint with the heading instead of waiting on the rows; the skeletons take `rowsOnPage(...)` and are now **exact** rather than the three `ITEM_SKELETON_COUNT = 6` guesses they had been, since the last page is usually short; and `cache()` means the page and anything else asking in the same request share one `count`. Each `<Suspense>` is **keyed on the page**, so stepping to another one suspends again rather than holding the previous rows on screen. **`/collections` runs three collection queries and that is the accepted cost** of the option chosen: `getCollections` stays unbounded and `cache()`d because the sidebar needs *every* favorite, not the most recent few, so the new `getCollectionsPage` + `countCollections` sit beside it rather than narrowing it — the alternative was breaking that shared read apart and giving the sidebar, the stat cards and the dashboard grid their own queries, which is a real refactor of three consumers for a page that is not the hot path. `countCollectionItems` is likewise **not** `CollectionSummary.itemCount`: that number comes from the unbounded read, and a paginated page must not depend on that read having run.
