A private `collectionItemsWhere` is shared by the count and the rows so the two can never match different items. **`parsePageParam` is deliberately strict**: an absent parameter is page 1, and anything else must be digits only and at least 1 — `Number` would have accepted `"1e3"`, `" 2 "` and `"0x2"` — while a repeated `?page=1&page=2` arrives as an array and is refused, since there is no single page being asked for. `pageCount` floors at 1, so an empty listing is a first page that exists rather than a page 0 nobody can link to. `pageLinks` is **windowed** (first, last, the current page and its neighbours, with an ellipsis for each gap) so a Pro stash cannot render a hundred links. **The component is hand-written, not `shadcn add pagination`**: that primitive renders plain `<a>` elements, which would mean a full reload per page instead of a client navigation, and adapting it would have made it the **third** edited file in `components/ui/` after `sonner.tsx` and `command.tsx`. **A step with nowhere to go renders as a `span` with `data-disabled`, never a greyed anchor** — an `<a>` without `href` still announces as a link and stays in the tab order, so "greyed out" would have been a lie to a screen reader. The current page is a `span` too, which is what stops it hovering or taking focus. Both labels reuse the existing `.action-label`, so they collapse to bare chevrons below the breakpoint exactly as the top bar's buttons do. **The dashboard limits moved out of their components** into the new constants module as `DASHBOARD_COLLECTIONS_LIMIT` / `DASHBOARD_RECENT_ITEMS_LIMIT`, and `dashboard-skeletons.tsx` now *reads* them rather than duplicating `6` and `10` behind a comment saying it matches — which is what that comment had been asking for since the skeleton feature. **Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` clean, and all seven `.pagination*` selectors confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS, `[data-current]` and `[data-disabled]` intact. The suite went **256 → 257**: the only test that had to change was `src/lib/db/collection-items.test.ts`, whose three calls gained a page argument, and it picked up one new assertion that page 3 asks for `skip: 42, take: 21` — worth noting that `tsc` catching *only* that file is what proved no other caller of the three widened queries was missed. **The new pure logic is untested and that is the outstanding work**: `parsePageParam`, `pageCount`, `rowsOnPage` and `pageLinks` all live in `src/lib/`, which Vitest collects, and all four have real branches worth naming — the array case, the digits rule, the floor at 1, a short last page, and the ellipsis placement. `/feature test` was skipped on this one. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. Two things want an eye on first run: whether the strip reads well at 390px, where the labels collapse and only the chevrons and numbers remain, and whether the empty-stash case looks right — `rowsOnPage` returns 0 there, so the skeleton renders nothing at all before the "No snippets yet." message arrives, which is honest but is a change from six placeholder cards. **One process note:** a dev server was live on port 3000 when the build was due; the user was asked, the PID was killed, and **the port was confirmed free with `netstat` before building** — the check the last three entries record as the only reliable one, since `TaskStop` reports success while leaving the Next child listening. `.next` was deleted first and now holds a production build, so going back to `npm run dev` wants it deleted again. **Left open:** nothing sorts or filters a listing, which is the natural companion to paging it; the dashboard is unpaginated by design and still links to pages that now hold 21 at a time; the free tier's 3-collection cap is still unenforced, so `/collections` paginating is currently only meaningful for a Pro-sized stash; and there is no page-size control. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still inert on the item drawer and on collections, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature — `collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id — is still uncovered. Spec: @context/features/pagination-spec.md
- **2026-08-27 — Settings page layout: the account card as labelled rows.** `/settings` went from a description paragraph followed by both buttons in one wrapping flex row, to **three rows of one shape** — a title and a sub-line on the left, whatever acts on them on the right: *Account* / "Manage your account security and preferences" with no action, *Password* / "Update your password to keep your account secure" with `Change Password`, and *Delete Account* in the destructive colour / "Permanently delete your account and all associated data" with `Delete Account`. The page subtitle became "Manage your account settings". **Three files, no new file, no migration, no new dependency and no new ShadCN primitive** — presentation only, so the suite stayed at **257 passing**. Loaded from a reference screenshot (@context/screenshots/Settings-example.png, now tracked alongside the other three). **The buttons were explicitly kept as they are**, confirmed when the spec was loaded: the reference draws Delete Account as solid red and ours keeps the nova preset's `destructive` variant (translucent, red text) with `Change Password` on `outline`, same icons and labels — only their placement moved into the right-hand slot of their row. That is the same call the profile-page improvements recorded and declined to override, so the deviation is now deliberate in two places rather than an oversight in one. **The decision worth keeping is where the hairline hangs.** The obvious `.settings-row + .settings-row { border-top }` is the classic divided-list pattern and is wrong here: it would put a line under *Account*, which the reference does not have. It hangs off **`.settings-row[data-danger]`** instead, so the rule means "set the destructive action apart from the ordinary ones above it" rather than "divide the list" — which reproduces the reference exactly *and* lets a future ordinary row slot in above without gaining a line it should not have. The same attribute carries `color: var(--destructive)` on that row's title, so one hook states one idea rather than a `data-divided` and a `data-danger` needing to agree. **`.settings-row-text` takes `flex: 1 1 20rem` with `min-width: 0`**, and both halves earn their place: the 20rem basis is what drops the button under the text on a narrow viewport (the row is `flex-wrap`), and the `min-width` is what lets a long sub-line wrap rather than refusing to shrink below its content and pushing the button out of the card. **The Account heading deliberately keeps `.settings-card-title`** rather than taking the new `.settings-row-title`: that class is grouped with `.profile-card-title` in `globals.css` and already gives exactly the 1rem/600 the row titles want, so reusing it keeps `/profile` out of the diff and stops either half of that pair going dead. **`.settings-card-description`, `.settings-actions` and `.settings-actions-note` were reshaped into the `.settings-row*` rules rather than left beside them** — the whole point of checking, since layering new rules over old ones is how this file accumulates dead selectors; `grep` confirms none survive, in the source or in the built stylesheet. Heading levels follow the structure (`h2` for the card, `h3` for the two rows under it). **Nothing below the markup moved**: both dialogs, the `changePassword` / `deleteAccountAction` server actions, the 10-per-15-minutes limiter keyed on the user id, the password-fingerprint sign-out and the `VerificationToken` cleanup under both identifiers are byte-identical, and a GitHub-only account still gets its explanatory note — now occupying the Password row's action slot instead of sitting beside a lone Delete button. **No new tests, and none are possible for this surface** — `vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`, and the diff adds no importable logic; the existing 257 passing unchanged is what covers it. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` are clean, and **all nine new rules were confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS** with both old ones gone — worth noting that `flex: 1 1 20rem` minifies to **`flex: 20rem`**, which is the same computed value (`flex: <width>` expands to `1 1 <width>`) and not a dropped declaration, and that the previous entry's finding still holds: Lightning CSS strips quotes from attribute selectors, so a grep written against the source form of `[data-slot="…"]` finds nothing. **Verified by the user in the browser rather than by `/feature browser`** — the dev server was left running deliberately so they could check `/settings` against the reference before anything was built, which is the standing preference working as intended (stop at a green build and tests; the browser pass is theirs). **Two process notes.** The dev server was live on port 3000 when the build was due and the user was asked rather than assumed — the port was then freed with `taskkill //PID //F //T` in a loop and **confirmed free with `netstat`**, which remains the only reliable check here since `TaskStop` reports success while leaving the Next child listening; the kill surfaced as a "background command failed with exit code 1" notification, which is the fourth entry to record that artefact and is the kill, not a build problem. And the commit message was written to a file and passed with `git commit -F` rather than through a heredoc: the previous entry records a quoted heredoc silently failing twice on prose containing apostrophes while leaving the old file in place, and this body was full of them. **Left open:** the settings page still holds nothing but the account card, so theme, export and the other things a settings page usually carries are still absent; the page's own null-user redirect and the GitHub-only branch remain **not verifiable read-only** (one needs a session token whose row is gone, the other a GitHub account to hand), exactly as the settings-page entry recorded. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still inert on the item drawer and on collections, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, nothing sorts or filters a listing, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted — `src/app/settings/layout.tsx` remains one of five three-line `AppShell` wrappers — and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature (`collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id) plus the four pure functions the pagination feature added (`parsePageParam`, `pageCount`, `rowsOnPage`, `pageLinks`) are still uncovered.
- **2026-08-27 — Editor preferences.** `/settings` carries a second card holding five Monaco settings — font size, tab size, word wrap, minimap and theme — stored in a JSON `editorPreferences` column on `User` and applied to every editor in the app, the drawer's read-only view of a snippet included. Nine new files, seven edited, **one migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. Four decisions were settled at load: keep the Monaco built-ins rather than pulling in `monaco-themes`; **`vs-dark` replaces the hand-tuned `devstash-dark`**; preferences apply to read-only editors too; and the values are read on the server. **The schema is deliberately two schemas, and that is the design.** `editorPreferencesSchema` is strict and is what a *write* must satisfy — a font size the dropdown does not offer is a bad request, not something to quietly correct. Reading the column is the opposite problem: a JSON column is untyped at the boundary and may hold `null`, a set saved before a preference existed, or something a later version wrote — so `parseEditorPreferences` falls back **field by field** through `.catch()`, and one unreadable value costs that one preference rather than resetting the other four. **Proven non-vacuous rather than assumed**: removing a single `.catch()` failed exactly the test that names that rule and nothing else, and it was reverted. **`EditorPreferences` is a `type` alias rather than an `interface`, and that is load-bearing** — it goes into a Prisma `Json` column, whose `InputJsonObject` wants an index signature, and TypeScript infers one for an alias but never for an interface; the build fails otherwise, and a comment in the file records why. **`monokai` and `github-dark` are token maps this app defines** in `src/lib/monaco-themes.ts`: Monaco ships only `vs`, `vs-dark`, `hc-black` and `hc-light`, and an unknown name **falls back to the light `vs`**, which would light a dark page up — which is why the theme schema refuses one rather than storing it. `devstash-dark` is gone with its `#171717` background chosen to match the `--card` frame, so **there is now a visible seam where `vs-dark`'s `#1e1e1e` meets the window chrome**; that is `vs-dark` behaving correctly, and adding `devstash-dark` back as a fourth option is a one-line change if it reads badly. **`AppShell` is now async**, which is the one deliberate regression here: the editors read their settings from a context, and reading them in the browser would open every editor on the defaults and then jump. It costs some of the parallel paint the sidebar's Suspense boundary has protected since the skeleton feature, but **no query** — `getCurrentUser` is `cache()`d per request, so `SidebarWithData` shares this one, and its own null-check is now belt and braces. **The provider applies a change and then writes it, both outside the state updater on purpose** — React is free to call an updater twice, which would save twice and toast twice; the first pass had the save inside it and was fixed before the build. It carries the `.catch(() => null)` the delete flow's browser pass had to discover the hard way, since a failed *write* answers `{ success: false }` but a failed *request* **rejects**, and the screen would otherwise keep a value the server never stored; a rejected write puts the previous value back. **Line height now scales with the font size at 1.6**, the ratio the editor was built at (21px against 13px), so the opening-height estimate `estimateHeight` makes before Monaco exists stays honest when the font moves. **Word wrap defaults on** per the spec, where the editor was hardcoded `off` — so every existing snippet renders differently. The settings rows reuse the existing `.settings-row` shape; the controls are a **native `<select>` and a checkbox drawn as a switch through `appearance: none`**, since no `select` or `switch` primitive is installed and the browser's own controls keep the keyboard and touch behaviour a hand-built toggle would have to reimplement. The action is **not rate limited**, matching the item and collection actions rather than the profile ones, which are throttled because each attempt costs bcrypt. **23 new tests take the suite 257 → 280** — 17 over the two schemas (the offered-value rules, the strict path refusing a partial set, and the five lenient-read cases) and 6 over the action (session, validation not touching the database, the account coming from the session rather than the payload, a missing row, and a rejected write becoming a message). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` and `npm run build` are all clean, and the new CSS was confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS — which rewrites **`translateX(1rem)` to `translate(1rem)`**, an equivalent single-argument form, worth knowing for the next one of these checks. **`prisma/migrations/migration_lock.toml` was never committed** with the init migration back in August and is included here. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. Three things want an eye on first run: the `vs-dark` seam above, whether the two custom themes' token maps actually colour real snippets sensibly (they were written by hand against Monarch token names, never rendered), and whether a toast per change reads as noise when several settings are changed in a row. **One process note:** a dev server was live when the build was due, the user was asked, the PID was killed and **the port confirmed free with `netstat`** before building — the check the last four entries record as the only reliable one, since `TaskStop` reports success while leaving the Next child listening; the kill again surfaced as a "background command failed with exit code 1" notification, which is the kill and not a build problem. `.next` holds a production build, so going back to `npm run dev` wants it deleted first. **Left open:** the two hand-written themes are unrendered, as above; there is no reset-to-defaults control; preferences apply only to Monaco, so the markdown editor's Write pane ignores font size and tab size entirely; and the settings page still holds nothing but these two cards. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Favorite and Pin are still inert on the item drawer and on collections, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, nothing sorts or filters a listing, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature (`collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id) plus the four pure functions the pagination feature added (`parsePageParam`, `pageCount`, `rowsOnPage`, `pageLinks`) are still uncovered. Spec: @context/features/editor-settings-spec.md
- **2026-08-28 — Favorites page.** `/favorites` lists everything the signed-in user has starred — items above collections — as a dense scan list rather than the cards the rest of the app uses. Five new files (a page, a layout and three components) plus the spec and its reference screenshot, eleven edited, **no migration** (`prisma/schema.prisma` untouched, so `db:status` was not run), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. `Item.isFavorite` and `Collection.isFavorite` have been in the schema since `20260814120000_init`, along with an `@@index([userId, isFavorite])` on each. **Nothing in the app can favorite anything yet, and that was confirmed rather than assumed** — the drawer's Favorite button and the collection actions row are both `disabled` with a `Coming soon` title, so this is the read side of a feature whose write side is deliberately deferred ("we do that later"). The consequence to expect: against the seeded demo data the page renders whatever was set by hand in the database, and a fresh account always sees the empty state. **`updatedAt` is the sort key as asked, and it is not "most recently favorited"** — there is no `favoritedAt` column, so an old favorite edited yesterday sorts above one starred this morning; recorded as intended rather than as a compromise, since a true ordering would need a migration. **The two reads are asymmetric on purpose.** `getFavoriteItems` in `src/lib/db/items.ts` is a real query (`userId` in the `where`, unbounded like the pinned list beside it); `getFavoriteCollections` in `src/lib/db/collections.ts` is a **filter over the `cache()`d `getCollections`** — the sidebar already runs that read on the same request, so it costs no query at all and `itemCount` comes back with it, which is the same partition `getSidebarCollections` and `getCollectionStats` already make. **No Suspense boundary, which is the one deviation from every other listing page** and is commented in place: every number here — the total in the heading and both section counts — comes out of the two reads, so a boundary would wrap the entire page and leave nothing to paint ahead of it; the sidebar keeps its own boundary in `AppShell`, so the shell is unaffected, and there is no skeleton file as a result. Protected the usual two ways — `/favorites` and `/favorites/:path*` joined the proxy matcher, plus the `getCurrentUser()` null redirect covering what the proxy cannot (a token that still verifies against a row that is gone). **Built to @context/screenshots/Favorites-example.png with one deliberate deviation**: the reference sets row titles in monospace and the app's own sans stack wins instead, on request. Everything else follows it — a filled `--type-favorite` star beside the heading with the combined count in muted parentheses, uppercase letter-spaced section labels carrying their own counts, and each section as **one bordered panel whose rows are divided by hairlines** rather than a card per row, which is how the spec's "clean lines, no heavy borders" reads. An item row shows a type-coloured icon, the title, an outlined badge tinted from `--type-color`, and the date; a collection row shows a folder icon (a collection has no type of its own, only the types of what is in it), its name, a neutral `N items` badge and the date. **A section with nothing in it is left out rather than rendered as an empty panel** — the call `PinnedItemsSection` already makes — and the "nothing favorited yet" state appears only when both are empty. `FavoriteItemRow` is a client component because it needs `openItem`, which takes a whole `ItemSummary` and is why the row passes the summary rather than an id; `FavoriteCollectionRow` stays a server component since it only navigates. Both use the stretched `.item-card-open` hit target the cards and file rows use, so the badge and date sit outside the button and no `stopPropagation` is involved. The focus outline is `outline-offset: -2px` because the panel clips its overflow and an outline drawn outside the first or last row would be cut off. **`formatRelativeDate` in `src/lib/utils.ts` is the only genuinely testable logic the feature adds**, and it counts **UTC calendar days rather than dividing an elapsed duration**: 23 hours spanning midnight is "Yesterday" and so is one hour spanning it, where a duration would call both "Today" — the same reason `formatShortDate` pins its time zone, so one row does not read differently to two viewers. It falls back to the short date at seven days, reads a future date as "Today" (clock skew, and counting backwards would be worse), and takes `now` as a parameter so the boundaries are testable without moving the clock. **6 tests take the suite 280 → 286.** **Two follow-ups landed after the first pass, both asked for.** A collection page's Favorite button now reflects state — `CollectionActions` takes an `isFavorite` prop, kept separate from the `collection` object because that shape is what the edit dialog's form reads and the form has no favorite field, and renders `data-active` / `aria-pressed` with a filled star, matching the drawer's precedent. **It stays `disabled`**, but the new rule lifts the primitive's 50% disabled opacity, or the selected state would be barely readable; it wins without `!important`, our rules being unlayered against Tailwind's `utilities` layer. And the dashboard's **Favorite items** stat card links here through a stretched invisible `Link` — wrapping the card was not an option, since `.stat-card` is a grid whose icon spans both rows and an anchor around the contents would collapse the layout. That forced one TypeScript detail worth remembering: `href: null` sits on the other three entries rather than the key being absent, because with it missing TS infers a union where `.href` is inaccessible, and a widened `string` is not a typed route — `"/favorites" as const` plus a present key on every entry is what keeps it one. Only the items card links; **Favorite collections points at the same page and was deliberately left alone**, since only the one card was asked for. **Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` (286 passed) and `npm run build` all clean, with `/favorites` in the route table as dynamic, and every new rule confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS — the `:has()` selectors, the `+` sibling hairline, the `[data-neutral]` badge, the mobile media query and `position: relative` on `.stat-card`. Note Lightning CSS emits a plain fallback declaration ahead of each `color-mix` one, so a grep for a single rule finds two. **Not verified in the browser by `/feature browser`** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests; the user checked `/favorites` against the reference on the running dev server themselves mid-feature. **One process note:** a dev server was live at each of the two points a build was due. The first time the user was asked and it was killed with the port confirmed free by `netstat` (the only reliable check here, since `TaskStop` reports success while leaving the Next child listening); the second time the user chose to keep it and the build was deferred to `/feature complete`, by which point the port was free on its own. The "background command failed with exit code 1" notification after the first kill is the kill, not a build problem — the fifth entry to record that artefact. **Left open:** the write side — favoriting an item or a collection — is the obvious next feature and is what makes this page reachable through the UI at all; the card dropdown's Favorite item still shows no state, unlike the page's button, since only the page was asked for; `Favorite collections` on the dashboard is unlinked; the page fetches both lists unbounded with no pagination, sort or filter, which is fine while nothing can create favorites in bulk; and `src/app/favorites/layout.tsx` is now the **sixth** three-line `AppShell` wrapper (dashboard, items, profile, collections, settings), which is the strongest the case for the `(app)` route group in project overview §7 has been — it would absorb all six and stays deferred as its own change because it is a route move touching typed-route generics. Everything the previous entries list stays open: Pin is still inert on the item drawer, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, nothing sorts or filters a listing, no CI runs the suite, and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature (`collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id) plus the four pure functions the pagination feature added (`parsePageParam`, `pageCount`, `rowsOnPage`, `pageLinks`) are still uncovered. Spec: @context/features/favorites-spec.md
- **2026-08-28 — Favorite and pin toggles.** Favoriting and pinning finally *write*: `Item.isFavorite`, `Item.isPinned` and `Collection.isFavorite` have been in the schema since `20260814120000_init` and nothing had ever set any of them, so `/favorites` could only show rows edited by hand in the database. Two new files (`src/hooks/use-flag-toggle.ts` and `src/components/items/item-flag-button.tsx`), nineteen edited, **no migration** (`prisma/schema.prisma` untouched, so `db:status` was not run), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. This removes the last `Coming soon` placeholders in the app. **Six controls went live**: the drawer's Favorite and Pin, a collection page's Favorite button, a collection card's menu item, and the pin *and* star on all three item card shapes (`item-card.tsx`, `image-card.tsx`, `file-row.tsx`). The drawer's two work **in view mode and never need edit mode**, which was the explicit ask — they act on flags the card already carried, so neither waits on the detail fetch. **Pin was not in the request and was added on a question at `/feature start`**: it is the same action shape, and shipping a drawer with one live and one dead button beside each other is worse than the extra action. **Set, not toggle.** `setItemFavorite(userId, itemId, isFavorite)` and its two siblings take the value being asked for rather than flipping what is stored, so a double-click or two tabs on one item converge on a single answer instead of racing to opposite ones — a test pins it by asserting both directions pass through, and **a mutation check proved it non-vacuous**: hardcoding the action's value to `true` failed exactly that one test and nothing else, and was reverted. The writes are **`updateMany` guarded on `count`**, not `update`, which throws `P2025` once the row is gone — the same reasoning `deleteItem` and `deleteCollection` carry, and it is why the collection version needs none of the `P2025` handling `updateCollection` above it does. `userId` is in the `where` rather than checked against a prior read, so "already gone" and "someone else's" are the same `false` and there is no window to swap the row. **One consequence worth knowing: starring an item moves `updatedAt`**, since Prisma maintains it on every write — so a freshly starred item lifts to the top of every list that sorts on it, `/favorites` included. That is *closer* to "most recently favorited" than that page could otherwise get, there being no column recording it, but it also reorders the dashboard's Recent list and the type pages. **`src/hooks/` exists for the first time** (project overview §7 planned it long ago) and holds the one hook all six controls share. It is **optimistic, which is a deliberate break from every other mutation in this app** — those await, toast and refresh — because a star that waits on a round trip before it moves reads as a click that did not register. `useOptimistic` paints the new value and React puts it back on failure, so nothing is undone by hand; `router.refresh()` runs **inside** the transition, which is what holds the optimistic value up until the fresh server data lands instead of flickering through the old one in between. It carries the `.catch(() => null)` the delete flow's browser pass had to discover the hard way — a failed *write* answers `{ success: false }` but a failed *request* **rejects**, and without the catch the control is left mid-transition. **Success is silent and only a failure toasts**, decided when the spec was loaded: the star is its own confirmation and one toast per click is noise. **The drawer needed its own plumbing**, and the reasoning is the durable part: `router.refresh()` cannot update the drawer's props, because the open item lives in `ItemDrawerProvider`'s client state. So the provider gained `handleFlagsChanged`, which patches `summary` and **deliberately does not bump `requestRef`** — dropping the in-flight detail fetch would leave the body loading forever over a change that does not touch it — and **`ItemDrawer` now reads both flags from `summary` rather than `detail?.isFavorite ?? summary.isFavorite`**, because the buttons are live from the moment the drawer opens and a detail response carrying the older value would otherwise land on top of a toggle made while it was in flight. The provider's callback also runs no refresh of its own; the hook already does one. **A follow-up request reshaped the card controls after the first pass.** Pin was to behave like the star on hover, and both were to align with the *first* line of a title that wraps. `ItemFavoriteButton` became **`ItemFlagButton`** with a `flag: "favorite" | "pin"` prop — the two differ only in icon, action and label, so a small `FLAGS` record carries the difference rather than two near-identical components. Both are hidden at rest (`opacity: 0`), revealed by card hover or their own `:focus-visible`, and held visible once set, which is the whole of what the old read-only indicators said; the `.item-card-flag` / `.item-card-star` classes and their three usages are gone. **Only the star goes yellow** — an active pin takes `--foreground`, the same distinction the drawer already makes, or a pinned item would read as a favorited one. The alignment fix is **`height: 1lh` on the buttons against an `align-items: flex-start` row**: a box exactly one line tall centres its icon against *that* line rather than against the whole block, which is what `align-items: center` was doing to a two-line title. `1lh` is the unit the dashboard skeletons already rely on. `.file-row-name` was left centred, since it is `nowrap` with an ellipsis and can never wrap. **17 new tests take the suite 286 → 303** across the two action files — session, the owner coming from the session and never the payload, the value passing through both ways, the missing/not-yours result, success with no `data` half, a rejected write becoming a message, and, because the two item flags share one helper, that favoriting does not touch the pin and vice versa. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` and `npm run build` are all clean, and every new rule was confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS — including `height: 1lh` and `align-items: flex-start` surviving, the four-selector hover-reveal rule intact, and **no `item-card-flag` or `item-card-star` left in the output**. The `opacity: 1` override on `.collection-favorite[data-active="true"]` was removed with the `disabled` attribute it existed to counteract. **Not verified in the browser by `/feature browser`** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests; the user had the dev server running throughout and checked the toggles themselves, which is what produced the pin and alignment follow-up. **One process note:** a dev server was live at both points a build was due. The first time the user chose to keep it and the build was deferred; the second time `/feature complete` was invoked, which itself ends by restarting the dev server, so it was stopped — `taskkill` by PID with the port then **confirmed free by `netstat`**, the only reliable check here since `TaskStop` reports success while leaving the Next child listening. Both kills surfaced as "background command failed with exit code 1" notifications, which are the kills and not build problems — the sixth and seventh entries to record that artefact. **Left open:** the optimistic path is **not exercised by any test** — `vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`, so the hook in `src/hooks/` is uncollected by configuration, and its interesting behaviour (the revert on failure, the refresh inside the transition) needs the browser; there is no undo or confirmation on unfavoriting, which is deliberate but means a mis-click is silent; the collection card's menu names the action ("Add to favorites" / "Remove from favorites") while the page's button shows a pressed state, which is the right idiom for each but does mean two vocabularies for one flag; pinning is available in the drawer and on cards but not from a collection card, which has no pin to give; and the search palette still cannot favorite anything. Everything the previous entries list stays open: there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, nothing sorts or filters a listing, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature (`collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id) plus the four pure functions the pagination feature added (`parsePageParam`, `pageCount`, `rowsOnPage`, `pageLinks`) are still uncovered.
- **2026-08-28 — Favorites page sorting.** `/favorites` sorts client-side: each panel carries its own native `<select>`, items by **Date / Name (A–Z) / Name (Z–A) / Item type** and collections by **Date / Name (A–Z) / Name (Z–A)**. Two new files (`src/lib/favorites-sort.ts` + its test) and `src/components/favorites/favorites-list.tsx`, four edited, **no migration**, **no query change at all**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. **The control is per section rather than one for the page**, because the option sets genuinely differ — a collection has no item type of its own, only the types of what it holds, so a single control would have to disable or silently ignore "Item type" while collections are on screen. That is expressed as two separate key unions rather than one shared union with a member the collection side must skip. **Two decisions came from questions.** "Creation date" for collections became **`updatedAt`**: `CollectionSummary` carries `updatedAt` and the row already *displays* it, so the order matches the dates on screen, whereas `createdAt` would have meant sorting on one date while showing another — and it would have needed a column added to `collectionSelect`, the shared `cache()`d read the sidebar, dashboard grid, stat cards and `/collections` all narrow. And the control is a **native `<select>`**, reusing the `.settings-select` precedent the editor-preferences card set: it keeps the browser's own keyboard, touch and screen-reader behaviour, which a segmented button group would have to reimplement, and three or four buttons per header is a lot of chrome on a page whose whole point is dense scanning. **The page stops being a server component below the heading.** It still awaits both reads, still owns the total and the empty state, and hands the two lists to a `"use client"` `FavoritesList` holding the sort state — `FavoriteSection` and `FavoriteCollectionRow` move into that graph, `FavoriteItemRow` already was one. Both summary types cross as `import type`, which is erased, so no Prisma reaches the bundle. `FavoriteSection` gained an `action` slot and its heading became a flex row. **Three ordering rules worth keeping.** Names compare through `localeCompare` with the locale **pinned to `"en"`** rather than left to the viewer's — the same reasoning `formatShortDate` pins its time zone, so one list does not order itself differently for two readers; `sensitivity: "base"` makes it accent-insensitive as well as case-insensitive. The **type sort tiebreaks by date, newest first**, since a type name says nothing about the order of the items carrying it. And every comparator sorts a **copy**: the arrays arrive as props and `Array.prototype.sort` mutates in place — with the sort being stable, ties keep the query's order, which is most recently updated first. **10 new tests take the suite 303 → 313**, and two mutation checks are the reason they are worth anything. The first **failed**: fixtures of `Apple` / `banana` / `cherry` sort identically under a plain `<` (capital `A` is 65, lowercase `b` is 98), so swapping `localeCompare` out broke almost nothing — retightened to `apple` / `Banana` and `devOps` / `React Patterns`, the same swap now fails three tests. The second **disproved a comment I had written**: descending swaps the comparator's arguments rather than negating its result, and the claim that this is what preserves ties is false — negating passed all ten, because a stable sort only reorders on a *positive* comparison and `-0` is not one. Both forms behave identically, ties included; swapping is simply the plainer read, and the comment now says that instead. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` (313) and `npm run build` all clean, with both new rules confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS. **Not verified by `/feature browser`** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests; the dev server was left running through implementation so the options could be checked by hand. Two things want an eye: whether the select reads well right-aligned against the small uppercase section label, and whether "Item type" grouping is legible without a visible group heading between the groups. **One process note, now confirmed rather than suspected:** the dev server was live when the build was due and was killed by PID with **the port then confirmed free by `netstat`** — the only reliable check here — and the "background command failed with exit code 1" notification that followed is the kill, not a build problem, which is the eighth entry to record that artefact. Note also that the built stylesheet is **not** at `.next/static/css/` but at `.next/static/chunks/*.css`, which is worth knowing for the next one of these checks. **Left open:** the sort choice is component state, so it resets on navigation and is not shareable as a URL — deliberate, since the spec asked for no `?sort=` navigation; there is no ascending option for dates or for item type; the two panels sort independently, so "Name" in one says nothing about the other; and the comparators are covered but `FavoritesList` itself is not, `vitest.config.mts` collecting only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`. One new file (`src/lib/db/item-listing-order.test.ts`), three edited, **no migration** (`isPinned` and its `@@index([userId, isPinned])` have been in the schema since `20260814120000_init`), **no new dependency** and **no component change at all**. **The scope was far narrower than the title suggests, and checking first is the lesson.** Four of the six item reads already ordered `[{ isPinned: "desc" }, { … }]` — `getItemsByType`, `getCollectionItems`, `getFileItemsByType` and `searchItems` — so only `getFavoriteItems` and `getRecentItems` in `src/lib/db/items.ts` were wrong, a one-line `orderBy` change each with no select or signature touched. `getPinnedItems` is out of scope, every row it returns being pinned. **The `/favorites` half needed more than a query change**, and would have been the silent half of a half-done feature: its items panel was made client-sortable earlier the same day, so `sortFavoriteItems` decides the final order and would cheerfully interleave a pinned item into the middle of a name sort no matter what the query returned. **Decided at `/feature start`: the pin outranks the sort** — an item pinned to the top of one ordering and buried in another reads as a bug rather than as a sort — so a `byPinned` comparison now leads all four item comparators and the chosen key orders *within* the pinned block and within the rest. `SortableItem` gained `isPinned`; `sortFavoriteCollections` is untouched, since only `Item` carries the column and `Collection` has `isFavorite` alone. **Also decided: the dashboard duplication stays.** A pinned item now heads Recent as well as appearing in the Pinned section above it — the section is a shortcut, Recent is the full list, and hiding a pinned item from "recent" would be the stranger read; that reasoning is a comment on `getRecentItems` rather than only in this entry. **`item-listing-order.test.ts` is the durable part.** The rule lives in **five separate `orderBy` arguments with no shared helper**, which is exactly the shape one edit quietly drops from a single listing, so the new file asserts it across all five at once (`getCollectionItems` was already covered in its own file, and the header says so). It follows the `collection-items.test.ts` precedent — mock `@/lib/prisma`, inspect the argument the module builds rather than what a database makes of it — and is its own file for the same reason that one is: `items.test.ts` mocks only `item.create`, and widening its client would change what every test there is allowed to reach. **11 new tests take the suite 313 → 324**, and both halves were mutation-checked rather than trusted: dropping the pin from the `name-asc` comparator failed 2 tests, and reverting the favorites `orderBy` to its old single key failed exactly 1, with both files verified clean after restoring. The comparator tests use a fixture (`zulu`, pinned, last by name in both directions, oldest by date, last by type) chosen so it can only lead if the pin genuinely outranks the sort, and `it.each` runs it over all four keys. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` (324) and `npm run build` all clean. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and the dev server was deliberately left running through implementation so the ordering could be checked by hand; the two things worth an eye are a pinned item heading `/favorites` under *every* sort option including Name (Z–A), where it is otherwise last, and whether the dashboard's Pinned-plus-Recent repetition reads as a shortcut rather than a duplicate. **One process note:** the dev server was live when the build was due and was killed by PID with **the port confirmed free by `netstat`** — still the only reliable check, `TaskStop` having reported success while leaving the Next child listening — and the "background command failed with exit code 1" notification that followed is the kill, not a build problem, which is the ninth entry to record that artefact. **Left open:** the pinned block has no visual separator anywhere — a pinned item is marked by its own pin icon and nothing says where the block ends, which matters more now that pinning survives an explicit sort; there is still no way to reorder *within* the pinned block; and the `/favorites` sort choice remains component state, so it resets on navigation. - **2026-08-28 — File & image create: no description, title from the filename.** The create dialog drops the Description field for File and Image, and choosing a file names the item after it. Two new files (`src/lib/file-title.ts` + its test), three edited, **no migration** (`prisma/schema.prisma` untouched, so `db:status` was not run), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. **Description is gated on the type, not on the form**, which was the one decision taken at `/feature start`: the field lives in the shared `ItemFormFields` that the create dialog and the drawer's edit mode both render, so hiding it per form would have meant a file item having a description in one place and not the other. Gating on `uploadKindFor(typeSlug) === undefined` puts it alongside the Content / URL / Language gates already there and makes the two agree — "obsolete for these types" read as always, not just at creation. **Nothing is migrated and nothing is wiped:** `Item.description` stays in the schema, a file item stored before this keeps whatever it holds, and the edit form still submits that value unchanged rather than clearing it — it is simply no longer shown or editable. **`titleFromFileName` went into `src/lib/` rather than inline in the component**, because `vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**` and this is the one piece of the feature with real edge cases. It strips **only the last extension** (`archive.tar.gz` → `archive.tar`), collapses runs of underscores, dashes and whitespace to a single space, trims, and caps at the item schema's own `TITLE_MAX_LENGTH` — which was **made an export** for this, so a filename can never generate a title the schema then refuses. The cap is followed by a **second `trim()`**, since slicing at 200 can land mid-separator and leave a trailing space; a mutation check confirmed removing it fails exactly that test and nothing else. **One deviation from the loaded spec, decided while writing the tests:** the note said a dotfile should return nothing, and it returns `".gitignore"` instead — the leading dot is the whole name rather than a bare extension (`fileExtension` already reads it that way for the upload rules), and it is a better title than none. Only a name reducing to nothing at all (`___.png`, `""`) returns `""`, which the caller reads as having nothing to offer. Moot in practice, since no dotfile extension is in either `UploadConstraint`'s allow-list, but the function should still be sensible. **The fill never overwrites a typed title.** `ItemCreateForm`'s bare `setFile` became a `changeFile` that keeps the last value it generated in state and writes only when the title is empty or still equals that value — so picking the file after typing a name keeps the name, while a second upload can still replace a title the first one produced. Clearing the file (`onChange(null)`) leaves the title alone: the words are the user's whether or not the file is still attached. `changeType` is unchanged and still drops the upload, so a `.png` cannot be carried into a File item; it deliberately does **not** clear a generated title, which stays as editable text. **13 new tests take the suite 324 → 337** — the extension rules, the separator and whitespace collapse, leading/trailing separators, case preservation, a name with no extension, the dotfile, the two empty results, and both cap cases. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` and `npm run build` are all clean. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in; the dev server was deliberately left running through implementation so the dialog could be checked by hand. The thing most worth an eye is the sequence of typing a title, then picking a file, then picking a second one. **One process note:** the dev server was live when the build was due and was killed by PID with **the port confirmed free by `netstat`** — still the only reliable check, since `TaskStop` reports success while leaving the Next child listening — and the "background command failed with exit code 1" notification that followed is the kill, not a build problem, which is the tenth entry to record that artefact. Also worth remembering: `git checkout <path>` does **not** restore a mutation-checked file that is still untracked, so the mutated version stayed on disk until it was put back by hand. **Left open:** a file item can no longer be given a description anywhere, so if one is ever wanted back it is a gate to loosen rather than a field to rebuild; the generated title is literal, with nothing capitalised or reworded; and the edit drawer still cannot replace an item's file. - **2026-08-28 — Bulk drag & drop on the file and image pages.** Dropping one or more files onto `/items/files` or `/items/images` creates one item per file, with no dialog in between — each titled after its filename. Two new files (`src/lib/upload-file.ts`, `src/components/items/item-drop-zone.tsx`), four edited, **no migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. **The transfer logic had to come out of `FileUpload` first.** It already held authorise → PUT → progress, but as a *form field*: it owns one `value` and hands it back to the create form, so a batch would have had to duplicate it or contort around it. `src/lib/upload-file.ts` is that logic without the field — browser-only (`XMLHttpRequest` plus a relative `fetch`), so nothing server-side may import it — and `FileUpload` re-exports `UploadedFile` so `item-create-form.tsx` was untouched. **The 429 needed its own class, and that is the decision worth keeping.** `POST /api/upload` is rate limited at 30 per 15 minutes keyed on the user id (`UPLOAD_LIMIT` — ours, not a platform cap, sized against filling the bucket back when every upload cost a dialog), so a batch of 31 runs into it. Without telling that failure apart from a per-file one, files 31–40 would each collect the same message and the drop would read as broken; `UploadRateLimitedError extends UploadError` is thrown only on a 429, and the loop stops on it, marking everything still queued "Not uploaded — the batch stopped first." **Sequential rather than parallel** for the same reason: a fan-out would hit the limit as a burst of simultaneous failures. **Every file is validated before anything is sent**, so an obviously wrong one is named immediately rather than after the ones ahead of it finish, and an entirely wrong batch costs zero requests. Partial success is a real outcome — one failure never discards the rest, and `router.refresh()` runs **once at the end** rather than per file. **A dragenter/dragleave depth counter, not a boolean:** `dragleave` fires every time the pointer crosses into a child, so a boolean flickers the overlay off the moment the drag moves over a card; counting enter against leave is what makes "left the zone" mean the zone. `carriesFiles` checks `dataTransfer.types` for `"Files"` — contents are unreadable during a drag but the types are not — so dragging selected text or a link does not raise the overlay. **A name that reduces to nothing gets `"Untitled"`**: `titleFromFileName` returns `""` for `___.png`, and where the dialog can leave the field alone for a user to fill, a batch has nobody to ask and the schema requires a title. **The other five type pages are untouched by construction** — a local `DropTarget` returns its children unwrapped unless `uploadKindFor(slug)` says the type takes uploads, which is the same predicate `itemsLayoutFor` already uses. The overlay covers **the list section only**, decided at load, so the pagination controls below it stay clickable. **No new tests, and none are possible for this surface** — `vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`, and while `upload-file.ts` sits in `lib/` it is entirely `fetch` and `XMLHttpRequest` against a live route; the drop zone is a component. The suite stayed at **337 passing**, with `tsc --noEmit`, `eslint src` and `npm run build` clean, and all nine new CSS rules confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. Three things want an eye on first run: whether the overlay raises and clears cleanly when dragging across cards (the depth counter is written for it but unobserved), whether the progress panel above the list pushes the rows down disruptively mid-batch, and the 429 stop, which needs 31 files to reach. **One process note:** a dev server was live when the build was due, and since `/feature complete` restarts one anyway it was killed by PID with **the port confirmed free by `netstat`** — still the only reliable check, `TaskStop` having reported success while leaving the Next child listening — and the "background command failed with exit code 1" notification that followed is the kill, not a build problem, which is the eleventh entry to record that artefact. Also, a heredoc through the Bash tool **failed again on apostrophes in prose**, exactly as the global-search entry records; the Write tool took it first time, and the commit message went through `git commit -F`. **Left open:** an upload that stores but whose `createItem` then fails leaves an orphan in R2, the same accepted cost the dialog already carries, and a batch can now leave several; there is no way to cancel a batch once it starts; a dropped batch cannot be filed into a collection or tagged, so that stays the drawer's job afterwards; and the drop zone exists only on the two type pages — not on a collection page, the dashboard or the drawer. - **2026-08-28 — Upload rate limit as a bounded settings card.** A third card on `/settings`, beside Account and Editor, lets an account raise its own upload rate limit — 30 / 60 / 120 files over 15 minutes, 1 hour or 24 hours. Six new files, five edited, **one migration** (`20260828120948_add_upload_preferences`, adding `User.uploadPreferences Json?`), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. `npm run db:status` reported "Database schema is up to date!" — the direct Neon host was reachable this session. **The request as typed was for an open setting, and it was flagged at load rather than built.** `UPLOAD_LIMIT` is not a convenience preference like font size: the comment on it in `route.ts` says outright that it is sized against *storage*, because every authorised call puts an object in a bucket the app has no quota for and nothing else deletes. Letting the account name its own number means the limit only ever binds someone who chooses to be bound — fine for a single-user stash you own, a hole the moment DevStash has paying strangers on it. Two readings were put up (open, or bounded by a fixed offered set with the ceiling staying the app's) with the second recommended, and `/feature start` chose it. **The ceiling is enforced twice, and the second half is the one that matters.** `uploadPreferencesSchema` refuses a *write* naming anything the card does not offer, which is the obvious half. `parseUploadPreferences` refuses a *stored* value outside the set on the way back out — and **refuses rather than clamps**, because a number outside the set says the value was not written by this app at all: a hand-edited row, or a backup restored from a version offering different numbers. Clamping 100000 to the largest offered count would silently bless it; falling back to the default does not. **Mutation-checked rather than trusted**: swapping the stored schema's `limitSchema` for a bare `z.number()` failed exactly the one test naming that rule and nothing else, and the file was restored from a copy — not `git checkout`, which does nothing for a file still untracked, as the file-title entry records. **Both constants are configurable, since both were named**, and offering a set for each makes the worst case sayable: 120 over 15 minutes is the most anyone can ask for, stated in the module rather than left to whatever a request happens to carry. **The whole shape follows editor preferences deliberately** — a `Json` column (one blob read whole and never queried into, so a third preference is a schema change rather than a migration), a strict write schema against a lenient field-by-field read, `UploadPreferences` as a **`type` alias rather than an interface** because Prisma's `Json` input wants an index signature and TypeScript infers one only for an alias, and a server action taking the account from the session and never the payload. It is **not** rate limited, matching the item and collection actions rather than the profile ones, which are throttled because each attempt costs bcrypt. **Unlike editor preferences there is no provider and no client context:** those are read in `AppShell` and handed to Monaco, whereas the only other reader here is the upload route, on the server — so the card holds its own state and writes optimistically, putting the previous value back if the action refuses. It carries the `.catch(() => null)` that a failed *request* needs, since the action answers a failed *write* with `{ success: false }` but **rejects** when it cannot be reached. **The route now reads `getCurrentUser` rather than `getCurrentUserId`** — the account's limit is on the row, and that helper is `cache()`d, so it is the same one query. **Its two named constants are gone**, which is a small deviation from the request as worded ("keep the constants in route.ts as default") and was called out at the time: left as aliases they would have been unused variables, which coding-standards forbids, and stating 30 / 15 min in two places would let the route and the card drift apart. The numbers themselves are unchanged and are the defaults; what stays in `route.ts` is the comment on why a limit exists at all, that it is keyed on the account, and where the numbers now live. **26 new tests take the suite 337 → 363** across two files — the defaults being pinned *and* being members of the offered sets (or the card would open on a value its own dropdown cannot show), every offered value accepted, a count above the largest and one *between* two offered ones refused, a window shorter than the shortest refused, a partial write refused, the five stored-value fallbacks including the field-by-field one, the ceiling asserted again at the action, and the window labels. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` and `npm run build` are all clean. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. Two things want an eye on first run: whether the two selects sit correctly in the `.settings-row` shape at 390px, and whether the optimistic revert reads sensibly if a save is refused. **One process note:** `npx prisma generate` had to be run by hand after `db:migrate` — the generated client under `src/generated/prisma` did not pick up the new column, so `tsc` failed on three lines in `src/lib/db/user.ts` that had nothing wrong with them. That is the failure to expect after adding a column. Also, the dev server was live when the build was due and, `/feature complete` restarting one anyway, was killed by PID with **the port confirmed free by `netstat`** — still the only reliable check, `TaskStop` having reported success while leaving the Next child listening — and the "background command failed with exit code 1" notification that followed is the kill, not a build problem, which is the twelfth entry to record that artefact. **Left open:** the offered sets are hardcoded in the module, so widening them is a code change rather than configuration — which is the point, but worth saying; there is **no admin or plan-based ceiling**, so a Pro tier wanting different numbers than a free one has nowhere to express that, and `User.isPro` is not consulted; `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` (100 MB) stays global and out of scope, since it is signed into the presigned URL and R2 itself enforces it, making it per-account a larger change; and the bulk drop from the previous feature still stops the whole batch on a 429, which is now a limit the account can raise but not one it can raise *mid-batch*. Everything the previous entries list stays open: an abandoned upload still orphans its object with no sweep, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature (`collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id) plus the four pure functions the pagination feature added (`parsePageParam`, `pageCount`, `rowsOnPage`, `pageLinks`) are still uncovered.
- **2026-08-28 — Marketing homepage mockup.** A standalone prototype in `prototypes/homepage/` — `index.html`, `styles.css`, `script.js`, no build step, no dependency, no CDN, so it opens straight from disk. **Nothing under `src/` changes**, so none of the project's usual rules apply to it: no Next.js, no React, no ShadCN, no Prisma, no `@/*` alias, and Tailwind's no-utility-classes rule is moot since Tailwind is not involved. The hero carries the chaos-to-order idea (a chaos panel of 8 floating icons, a pulsing gradient arrow, a dashboard preview), followed by a six-card features grid, an AI section with a code-editor mockup, pricing with a monthly/yearly toggle, a closing CTA and a footer. **Two decisions were taken at load, both from questions, and both went against the spec as written.** The spec listed its own palette — Prompt amber, Command cyan, Note green, File slate, URL indigo — and **the app's own values won instead**: the seven `--type-*` hex values plus the dark surface tokens (`--background`, `--card`, `--muted`, `--muted-foreground`, `--border`) are copied from the `.dark` block of `src/app/globals.css`, so the preview reads as the real dashboard rather than as a generic dark page, and the spec's seventh type "URL" is called **Link**, as the app calls it. Only Snippet `#3b82f6` and Image `#ec4899` matched to begin with. The `[data-type]` → `--type-color` map is reproduced verbatim from `globals.css`, so the preview cards, the sidebar rows and the feature cards all colour themselves the same way the app does. Separately, the spec drew the preview cards with a **top** border and they were built that way first; on request they became **`border-left: 3px solid var(--type-color)`**, which is what the real item cards use — a comment in the file records the deviation so it does not get "fixed" back. **Icons are an inline SVG sprite.** The type icons are traced by hand from the lucide set the app uses; the brand marks are hand-drawn, since lucide is not available here and, per the auth phase 3 entry, dropped its brand icons in v1 — GitHub is accurate, Notion, Slack and VS Code are recognisable approximations. One detail worth keeping: **the brand marks need `.icon.solid` on the outer `<svg>`, not a class on the `<symbol>`**. CSS always beats a presentation attribute, so `fill="currentColor"` on the symbol's paths loses to the sprite's own `fill: none`; the paths carry no fill of their own and inherit from the referencing `<svg>` instead. Slack's four brand colours are set on its paths and still win over that inheritance, which is exactly the wanted behaviour. **The chaos animation** is a `requestAnimationFrame` loop with per-icon velocity, wall bounce, independent rotation and scale-pulse phases (each on its own random phase, so they never breathe in unison), and cursor repulsion whose force falls off with distance. Three things in it earn their place: the frame delta is **clamped to 50ms** so a backgrounded tab does not resume with one huge jump that throws every icon through a wall; speed is nudged back up below half the base and damped above 8×, so drift neither dies out nor runs away; and the loop **stops entirely** while the field is off-screen (IntersectionObserver) or the tab is hidden. The opening scatter is a jittered grid rather than pure random, which clumps. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the icons take the scattered static layout and the loop never starts, the arrow and tag animations are off, and every reveal is visible immediately. **Verified with Playwright by reading the DOM rather than by eye**, served over a throwaway `python -m http.server` on port 4173 — worth remembering that **the browser blocks the `file:` protocol outright**, so a static prototype cannot be driven straight off disk. All seven type colours resolve to the app's exact `rgb()` values on both the preview cards and the feature cards, with the left border measured at 3× the card's own hairline. Cursor repulsion was measured rather than assumed: the nearest icon sat **55px** from the field centre and **127px** away 700ms after the pointer parked there, with zero icons escaping the box at either width. All 8 icons move over 900ms; the nav goes `oklab(0.145 0 0 / 0.55)` → `/ 0.92` with a border on scroll; 15/15 reveals fire and each is unobserved afterwards; the billing toggle round-trips $8/"per month" ↔ $72/"per year" with the labels swapping emphasis and the knob translating 18px. At 390px every grid is single-column, the hero stacks chaos (y=629) → arrow (978) → preview (1044), and the arrow computes `matrix(0, 1.07, -1.07, 0, 0, 0)` — rotated 90° mid-pulse, running its own `arrow-pulse-down` keyframes, which exist because the pulse's own `transform` would otherwise clobber the rotation. `scrollWidth - innerWidth` is negative at both widths. Under emulated reduced motion: 8 distinct positions at `scale(1)`, zero movement over a second, `animation-name: none` on the arrow. The only console message is a 404 for `/favicon.ico`, an artifact of serving a bare directory. **A full-page screenshot renders everything below the hero blank** — the reveals never fire without scrolling — which looks alarming and is not a bug; capture viewport by viewport instead. **`npm run build` was deliberately skipped**, with permission: a dev server was live on port 3000, the history records three separate phantom failures from sharing `.next` with one, and the diff is entirely outside the Next app. `npx eslint` (which *does* lint `prototypes/script.js` — it is not in the ignore list), `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm test` (363 passing, unchanged) all clean, plus `node --check` on the script. **No unit tests, and none are possible** — `vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`, and nothing here is importable from the app. One unused sprite symbol (`i-dashboard`) was found by a usage sweep and deleted rather than left, per coding-standards. **Left open:** the chaos icons have no collision with *each other*, so two can overlap and briefly obscure one another before drifting apart; the brand marks are approximations rather than official artwork, which matters if this ever ships publicly; there is no favicon, so any real serving of it logs a 404; and nothing links the prototype to the app — moving it into `(marketing)/page.tsx`, which project overview §7 targets, would be its own feature and would mean rebuilding all of this in React against the real tokens. Spec: @context/features/homepage-mockup-spec.md
- **2026-08-28 — Homepage prototype polish.** Five changes to `prototypes/homepage/` — bigger hero icons, gentler cursor repulsion, a labelled dashboard mockup, a lighter features band, and the marketing accent moved from the prompt purple onto the snippet blue. Three files, **no new file**, no dependency, no migration; nothing under `src/` changes, so `npm test` stayed at **363 passing** and none of the app's rules (Next.js, React, ShadCN, Tailwind, `@/*`) apply here. **The icon size lives in two places that have to move together** — `.chaos-icon` (`3rem` → `3.75rem`) and its glyph (`1.5rem` → `1.875rem`) in CSS, and a hardcoded `48` fallback in `script.js` that `measure()` uses before layout; that literal became a named `ICON_SIZE = 60` with a comment tying it to the stylesheet, since a stale fallback would place the first frame against the old box and only correct itself on the next resize. **The repulsion needed two passes, and the first one is the instructive half.** Reading "should not move so fast" as "push less" gave `REPEL_FORCE` 900 → 260 with `DRAG` 0.985 → 0.975, and the measured result was an icon sitting **28px from the cursor and not moving** — the force was no longer enough to clear the pointer at all, which is a worse failure than darting. What the request actually wants is a *speed* limit, not a weaker field, so the force went back up to 800 and a hard `MAX_SPEED = SPEED * 3` (66 px/s) now scales velocity straight back onto the ceiling rather than easing toward it, so no single frame can carry an icon across the field; the radius came down 130 → 115 and the falloff is squared, so the push arrives gradually instead of snapping on at the edge of the radius. Measured with the cursor parked in the centre, the nearest icon glides 16 → 30 → 47 → 64 → 81 → 95 → 109 px over two seconds and then settles back to base drift. **The mockup names its sections** — the sidebar rows read Snippets / Prompts / Commands / Notes / Links in their own type colours (`.mock-nav-row` already set `color: var(--type-color)`, so the label inherits rather than restating each one), the brand row says DevStash, the search box says "Search everything", and the six cards carry titles over one remaining bar as body. The sidebar column widened `6.5rem` → `7.25rem` and every label truncates with an ellipsis; `min-width: 0` on the flex rows plus `flex-shrink: 0` on the icons is what makes that work rather than the row refusing to shrink. **`--accent` is the durable part of the colour change.** The purple was used in ~20 places as the site accent and in one place as the *meaning* of the Prompt type, and those had to be told apart: a scripted rewrite skipped the `--type-*` definitions and the `[data-type]` map and re-pointed everything else at a new `--accent: var(--type-snippet)`, so the accent is now stated once instead of twenty times and `--type-prompt` still colours the prompt sidebar row, mock card and feature tile — verified in the browser as `rgb(139, 92, 246)` against snippet's `rgb(59, 130, 246)`. Two knock-ons: the four `linear-gradient(135deg, snippet, prompt)` gradients would have become flat blue-on-blue, so `--accent-light: #60a5fa` rides along as the second stop; and the hero's two-radial glow had the same problem, so its second radial took the lighter blue. **`.btn-primary` was not purple at all** — it was `--foreground` on `--background` — but "for the buttons use the blue" reads as making it blue, so it now is, with white text. **`#features` gets a top border and no bottom one**, because `.ai` below already draws its own and two adjacent hairlines render as a doubled 2px line. **Verified in the browser** against a `python -m http.server` on 4173 — the `file:` protocol is blocked outright, so a static prototype cannot be driven off disk. Computed values rather than eyeballing: icon box 59.99px and glyph 29.99px, button `rgb(59,130,246)` on white, the headline gradient `rgb(59,130,246) → rgb(96,165,250)`, the features band `oklab(0.181 0 0)` against the page's `oklch(0.145 0 0)`, all six card titles and five nav labels present with zero clipped at 390 and 1440, no horizontal overflow at 390/768/1440, and reduced motion giving 8 distinct static positions with zero movement over a second. **One measurement trap cost real time and is worth remembering: a `page.reload()` restored the previous scroll position, which put the chaos field 2496px off-screen, and the `IntersectionObserver` correctly paused the loop — so every probe read "peak speed 0, nothing moving", which looks exactly like a broken animation.** The fix is to `window.scrollTo(0, 0)` and wait before measuring anything in the hero; the rAF was alive (97 frames) the whole time, which is what proved the loop rather than the page was fine. A `getBoundingClientRect` overhang of ~2.5px past the field edge is **not new** and not from the size change — the physics clamp is on the unrotated box while the icons rotate ±18°, which expands the measured box by ~8px at this size; `.chaos-field` has `overflow: hidden`, so it is clipped either way. `npx eslint prototypes` (which does lint `script.js` — it is not ignored), `node --check`, `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm test` are all clean. **`npm run build` was deliberately skipped, with permission** — a dev server was live on port 3000 and the history records three separate phantom failures from sharing `.next` with one, while this diff is entirely outside the Next app. **Left open:** the sidebar labels are type-coloured where the real app colours only the icon and leaves the label muted, which reads well here but is a small divergence from the dashboard it is imitating; `useDebounce` ellipses at 768px, which degrades correctly and matches how the app truncates titles, so it was left; the chaos icons still have no collision with each other; and nothing links the prototype to the app — a spec for that port now exists at @context/features/homepage-spec.md and is its own feature.
- **2026-08-28 — Marketing homepage.** The prototype became the real page: `/` renders the ported homepage instead of the `<h1>Devstash</h1>` placeholder that had been there since the second commit. Seventeen new files, two edited, one deleted, **no migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive** — `button` and `badge` were already in `components/ui/`. `npm test` stayed at **363 passing**, since the whole diff is components, CSS and a route group and `vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`. **`src/app/(marketing)/` is the first of the three route groups project overview §7 targets** — a much smaller move than the `(app)` group the history keeps deferring, because it is a new group over a placeholder rather than a route move touching six `AppShell` wrappers and typed-route generics. `src/proxy.ts` is untouched: the page is public. **Four client components and no more** — `ChaosField` (the rAF loop), `MarketingNav` (the scroll listener), `Reveal` (the fade-in observer) and `PricingCards` (the billing toggle); everything else is a server component, the footer year included, so there is no effect and no hydration mismatch. **Three name collisions had to be resolved, and they are the durable part of this entry.** (1) **`--accent` is already a ShadCN token** — the hover surface — so the prototype's site accent would have shadowed it and restyled every primitive on the page; it is `--marketing-accent` here. (2) **The `[data-type]` map in `globals.css` is keyed on the plural `ItemType.slug`** (`snippets`), where the standalone prototype used the singular, so the components pass plural slugs and the map is reused with no additions — verified live, with the preview's prompt row computing `rgb(139,92,246)` against snippet's `rgb(59,130,246)`. (3) **A semantic class handed to a ShadCN primitive goes through tailwind-merge, and a bare utility-shaped name can silently delete part of the variant.** The pricing CTA carried `className="block"`, which `cn()` read as the display utility and resolved against the base's own `inline-flex` by *dropping it* — the button became `display: block`, `items-center` stopped applying, and the label sat 1px from the top of a 32px box. Found because the misalignment was visible; the cause was not. Both hooks are now prefixed `cta-`, and the reasoning is a comment in `globals.css`. **The stylesheet is nested under `.marketing` rather than having each selector renamed** — one mechanical wrap instead of ~95 rewrites, which keeps generic names like `.nav`, `.hero` and `.section` scoped to the one page that uses them. Lightning CSS compiled the nesting correctly (`.marketing .nav{position:fixed…}`). Two things could not nest and were handled separately: **`@keyframes` cannot live inside a nested rule**, so the three were hoisted and prefixed (`marketing-arrow-pulse`), and **`scroll-behavior` is a property of the scrolling element**, so smooth anchor scrolling is scoped as `html:has(.marketing)`. `scroll-margin-top: 4rem` on `#features` / `#pricing` clears the fixed nav, measured landing at `top: 64` against a 64px bar. **Every link resolves and the nav reads the session.** The page contains exactly five distinct hrefs — `/`, `#features`, `#pricing`, `/sign-in`, `/register` — confirmed by enumerating the DOM.
