Signed out gives Sign In + Get Started, signed in as demo gives a single Dashboard button, both verified in the browser; sending someone who is already signed in to `/register` was the outcome worth avoiding. **The footer's seven links to pages that do not exist** (Changelog, Documentation, Keyboard shortcuts, Export your data, About, Privacy, Terms) were **deleted rather than pointed at 404s** — the mistake "View all collections" already made once — leaving a Product column and a Get started column. **Icons are lucide except the four brand marks**, which stay inline SVG for the reason `src/components/auth/github-mark.tsx` records: lucide v1 dropped its brand icons. `.icon.solid` fills from `currentColor`, and Slack's four brand colors are set per path because those beat an inherited value where a competing rule would not. **The logo was a follow-up request and is now byte-identical to the sidebar's** — measured against `.sidebar-brand-mark` on `/dashboard`: 31.99px square, 8px `--radius-md`, `linear-gradient(135deg, rgb(59,130,246), rgb(139,92,246))`, white `Layers` at 18px, name 600/17px. **That deliberately puts purple back**, against the accent re-key done earlier the same day, because the blue-to-purple gradient *is* the DevStash mark as the app draws it; the re-key was about buttons, headline and section chrome, and a comment in both `brand.tsx` and `globals.css` records the exception so it is not "fixed" back. **A `/feature review` pass found three things**, all fixed before the merge: `.sr-only` was dead (the prototype hid the switch label that way; the React version uses `aria-label`), `.mock-bar.w-sm` was dead (every preview card renders the long bar), and `reveal.tsx` reached `React.Ref` through the global namespace while importing its other types by name. **Verified in the browser** against the running dev server rather than by eye: 8 chaos icons at 60px with the tuned constants ported, the billing toggle round-tripping $8/per month ↔ $72/per year with the knob translating 18px, all 15 reveals firing on scroll, the arrow rotating 90° at 390px, `scrollWidth - innerWidth` negative at 390 and 1440, and zero console errors or warnings. The pricing buttons were re-measured after the `cta-` fix at `display: flex`, 340px wide, text 6px above and 7.6px below — the remaining difference being the font's own ascender/descender asymmetry, which every other button on the page shares. The built stylesheet was checked after Lightning CSS for all the new rules, and confirmed to contain no `.marketing .sr-only` or `.marketing .mock-bar.w-sm`. **The prototype stays where it is** — it is the reference, not something to delete, and it is now the only place the old singular `data-type` slugs and the standalone token block live. **No unit tests, and none are possible for this surface**; the existing 363 passing unchanged is what covers the refactor-free port. **Left open:** the footer's Get started column still offers Create an account / Sign in to a signed-in visitor, which is harmless but inconsistent with the nav that adapts; there is no standalone `/pricing` page, so the nav's Pricing link is an anchor and would need rethinking if one is ever added; Go Pro goes to `/register` because there is no checkout; the AI section's code block keeps hand-written syntax spans rather than a highlighter; light mode is untouched, the root layout still hardcoding `.dark`; and `globals.css` is now ~4,500 lines with ~1,180 of them this feature, which is the house pattern working as designed but does make splitting the stylesheet a change worth considering on its own. Spec: @context/features/homepage-spec.md
- **2026-08-28 — Homepage top nav on the auth pages.** The five pages in the `(auth)` route group — `/sign-in`, `/register`, `/verify`, `/forgot-password`, `/reset-password` — render the marketing nav instead of the standalone brand lockup that had sat above the card since auth phase 3. **No new file**, four edited, **no migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**; `npm test` stayed at **363 passing**, the diff adding no importable logic. **The finding that shaped it: the nav's CSS is scoped to `.marketing`.** Every `.nav*` rule is nested inside that block, along with `.shell` and `.brand`, so dropping `<MarketingNav>` into the auth layout renders it completely unstyled. The layout therefore wraps its children in a `.marketing` element rather than the rules being lifted out — one wrapper against ~95 selector rewrites, and it keeps generic names like `.nav` and `.hero` meaning nothing outside the pages that use them. **That was checked rather than assumed to be safe**: `.marketing` itself declares only `--marketing-accent`, `--marketing-accent-light`, `--shell` and a `scroll-margin-top`, and every rule nested inside it is class- or attribute-scoped with **no bare element selectors**, so an `a` or `h1` rule cannot leak onto the auth cards. It also means the auth pages inherit anything added to that block later, which is the accepted cost. **`signedIn` is hardcoded `false`.** Every page in the group already redirects a signed-in visitor to `/dashboard`, so the nav's Dashboard branch is unreachable from here — the layout stays **synchronous** and reads no session, where the marketing layout is `force-dynamic` and awaits `getCurrentUser()`. **Two nav changes that also affect the homepage, both harmless there.** The section links became **rooted** (`/#features`, `/#pricing`) because a bare fragment has nothing to jump to off the homepage; Next handles a rooted hash on the current route as an in-page scroll, so `/` behaves exactly as before, and the smooth-scroll and `scroll-margin-top` rules keyed on `.marketing` keep working because of the wrapper. And `usePathname()` now **drops whichever action button points at the page you are on**, so `/sign-in` offers only Get Started and `/register` only Sign In — the same cross-link each form already carries underneath it. `usePathname` was chosen over a prop so the rule is stated once and stays correct across all five pages; on `/` neither branch matches and both buttons render. **`.auth-brand` and `.auth-brand-mark` were deleted rather than orphaned**, per the house pattern, and `grep` confirms no reference survives in `src/` or in the built stylesheet. `.auth-shell` lost the `gap: 1.5rem` that had separated brand from card and took `padding: 6rem 1rem 2rem` — the nav is `position: fixed` at `4rem` and the shell is `min-height: 100dvh` with `justify-content: center`, so without top padding a centered card slides under the bar on a short viewport. **Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` and `npm run build` all clean, with the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS confirmed to contain **no `auth-brand`** and `.auth-shell{…padding:6rem 1rem 2rem…}`. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests. **One process note:** a dev server was live when the build was due; the user was asked, the PID was killed with `taskkill //F //T`, and **the port was confirmed free by `netstat` before building** — still the only reliable check here, since `TaskStop` reports success while leaving the Next child listening. The commit message went through `git commit -F` rather than a heredoc, the history recording two silent heredoc failures on prose containing apostrophes. **Left open:** the nav is translucent with a backdrop blur and only gains its bottom border once scrolled past 16px — and the auth pages barely scroll, so the bar sits over the page background with no separating line; forcing `data-scrolled` on these pages is a one-line change if that reads as floating. The footer is deliberately not included, so an auth page has a nav and no footer. Everything the previous entries list stays open: there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature (`collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id) plus the four pure functions the pagination feature added (`parsePageParam`, `pageCount`, `rowsOnPage`, `pageLinks`) are still uncovered.
- **2026-08-29 — Book item type.** An eighth system type: a cover image carrying a title, summary, optional author and optional link, listed at `/items/books` as the image gallery and sitting in the sidebar beside the other seven. One new file (`src/lib/db/item-update.test.ts`), sixteen edited, **one migration** (`20260829104651_add_item_author`), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. `npm run db:status` reported "Database schema is up to date!" — the direct Neon host was reachable this session. **A book is the first type to fill two of the mutually exclusive payload columns**, which is the whole of what made this more than a new entry in `CREATABLE_TYPES`: `fileUrl` holds the cover, which is what makes it `ContentType.FILE`, and `url` holds the link. The column cannot say that, so `BOOK_TYPE_SLUGS` / `isBookType` does, joining `LANGUAGE_TYPE_SLUGS`, `PRO_TYPE_SLUGS` and `uploadKindFor` in `src/constants/item-types.ts`. Both write paths keep deciding from the **resolved** type rather than the request, so the widening is not a hole — and `updateItem` had to start selecting the item type's **slug** alongside its `contentType`, since a book is `FILE` and still owns a `url` and an `author`, which the content type alone cannot express. **The spec was wrong about `scripts/test-db.ts` and checking beat assuming**: its integrity rule is that no item carries both `content` **and** `url`, and a book sets `fileUrl` and `url`, so it needed no change at all. **`author` is one nullable column following the `language` precedent** — type-specific, on the shared model, written only for the slug that owns it and dropped for every other; a `Json` metadata blob was the alternative and was rejected while the field count is one. It is required-but-nullable in the schema like `language` is, which is why four existing test fixtures needed it added rather than it being optional. **The whole `/items/books` page came free**, exactly as predicted at load: `itemsLayoutFor` and the type page both derive from `uploadKindFor`, which returns `"image"` for books, so the cover gallery, its skeleton, pagination and the bulk drop zone arrived with no new component and no fourth `ItemsLayout` value. `titleFromFileName` was likewise already type-agnostic, so naming a book after its cover filename needed nothing. **Two decisions were put to the user mid-implementation.** The link is **optional**, decided after finding that the drop zone submits `url: ""` and would have uploaded a cover to R2 and *then* failed the create with "A URL is required.", orphaning the object once per dropped file — so a cover dropped on the listing becomes a book straight away and the link is added in the drawer, which is how the zone already treats files and images. And the `ItemType` row was **inserted on its own** rather than through `npm run db:seed`, which rebuilds the demo user's collections and items, wipes hand-set favorites and pins, and rewrites the password: 8 system types now, everything else untouched. **The Neon MCP `run_sql` tool could not be called** — its declared schema requires `projectId` while the server rejects that key and wants `project_id`, and there is no spelling satisfying both — so the row went in through a one-off `tsx` script using the project's own Prisma client and the seed's own dotenv loading, deleted immediately afterwards. `prisma/seed.ts` gained the row too (and `contentTypeFor` gained `books`, so a seeded book would not be written as TEXT), which is what a fresh database gets. **Small display calls**: the Description field and its drawer heading read **"Summary"** for a book and the URL field reads **"Link"**, since those are the names the type actually has; the create dialog labels its upload **"Cover"**; the drawer extracts an `ItemLink` component so the link renders identically for a link item and for the one under a book's cover; and `--type-book` is `#06b6d4`, cyan being the distinct hue left between the existing green and blue. **`npx prisma generate` had to be run by hand after `db:migrate`**, as the upload-preferences entry records — the generated client did not pick the column up and `tsc` failed on lines that had nothing wrong with them. **10 new tests take the suite 363 → 373**: the schema taking a book with a cover and no link and trimming its author, `createItem` writing both payload fields for a book while dropping the author for an image, and a new `item-update.test.ts` covering the edit path. **That is its own file rather than joining `items.test.ts`**, which mocks a client carrying only `item.create` — widening it would change what every test in it is allowed to reach, the same reasoning `collection-items.test.ts` already carries. **Both book branches were mutation-checked rather than trusted**: removing `isBookType` from the `url` write in each path failed exactly the two tests naming that rule and nothing else, and the file was restored with `git diff` confirming only the intended change. `npm test`, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` are all clean, and the three new CSS rules were confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS — which strips the quotes from `[data-type="books"]`, as earlier entries record. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests. The dev server was live when the build was due; the user was asked, PID 13964 was killed with `taskkill //F //T` and **the port confirmed free by `netstat`** before `.next` was deleted, that being the only reliable check here since `TaskStop` reports success while leaving the Next child listening. **Left open:** a book card gets **no quick-copy button** — `isCopyableType` is false for anything with an upload kind, so the cover counts as the payload even though a book has a link worth copying; **search does not match on `author`**, since `searchItems`'s `OR` list was not extended; the edit drawer still cannot replace a book's cover, as it cannot for any file; books are **not** `PRO_TYPE_SLUGS`-labelled despite carrying an image, which is the storage argument that gates Files and Images; and a book dropped on the listing gets a cover and a filename-derived title with no summary, author or link, all of which are the drawer's job afterwards. **Production was brought up to date by hand, not by the workflow.** `npm run db:migrate` in step 3 of the workflow applies to `development` only — per CLAUDE.md nothing here reads or writes production without being told to — so the production branch had neither half of what this feature needs, and the user applied them manually after the merge. Worth knowing that it is **two separate things**, and that only one of them a migration can ever carry: the `author` column, which is migration `20260829104651_add_item_author` and is what `npm run db:deploy` would apply; and the **Book `ItemType` row**, which is data, lives in `prisma/seed.ts`, and no migration touches — the same reason it had to be inserted into `development` by a one-off script above. Any future system type has that second half, and a deploy alone will not put it there. The user confirms production's migration bookkeeping is already handled, so `_prisma_migrations` records `20260829104651_add_item_author` as applied and the next `prisma migrate deploy` will skip it rather than failing on a column that already exists — which is the trap when a column is added with raw SQL, and the fix for it is `prisma migrate resolve --applied <migration>`. Everything the previous entries list stays open: there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature (`collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id) plus the four pure functions the pagination feature added (`parsePageParam`, `pageCount`, `rowsOnPage`, `pageLinks`) are still uncovered.
- **2026-08-29 — Stripe phase 1: core billing infrastructure.** The plumbing for Pro subscriptions ($8/mo, $72/yr) with **every consumer still absent** — no checkout, no webhook, no gate, no UI, and no behaviour change anywhere. Seven new files, four edited, **no migration** (`User.isPro`, `stripeCustomerId` and `stripeSubscriptionId` have been in the schema since `20260814120000_init` and had never been read or written), and **one dependency**, `stripe` (^22.6.0). That absence is the point of the split rather than an oversight: everything here is provable with `npm test`, where phase 2 needs the Stripe CLI, a browser and a card number. **The phase is drawn where it is because `vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`, and everything this adds is in `src/lib/`** — so all of it is reachable, which is not true of the webhook route or the settings card. **`src/lib/usage-limits.ts` is the piece the phase exists to get right, and its shape is chosen so it can be tested at all.** It is pure functions over a count and a boolean — it does *not* query, the caller fetches the count and passes it in — because `src/lib/prisma.ts` throws at import time when `DATABASE_URL` is unset, so a module reaching it can only be tested behind `vi.mock("@/lib/prisma")` and its tests then assert a mock's behaviour rather than the rule's. Keeping this one free of that means the tests import it directly. Three rules in it are easy to get wrong and are all pinned by tests: **the boundary is `>=`, not `>`** (an account holding exactly the limit is *at* it, so the next one is refused); **being over the limit is a real state, not an impossible one** — a Pro account that cancels keeps its 60 items against a cap of 50, so `remaining` clamps at 0 rather than reporting a negative nothing can render; and **the messages are built from the constants** rather than restating them, so the copy cannot drift from the rule it explains. `FREE_ITEM_LIMIT` (50) and `FREE_COLLECTION_LIMIT` (3) live here rather than in `pricing-cards.tsx`, which states them as display strings today with nothing enforcing them — phase 2 points that page at these. **One deviation from the plan worth knowing:** it puts these in a new `src/constants/limits.ts`, and the spec puts them in this module instead; the spec wins, since the numbers and the predicates reading them are the same idea and splitting them is how the two drift. **`UsageLimit.remaining` is `Infinity` for a Pro account**, since the spec's surface types it `number` and `null` was therefore unavailable. The interface documents that callers must branch on `limit === null` before rendering it, **and that `Infinity` does not survive JSON** — which is the second and better reason `limit` is the field to ask, given a server action's result is serialized. **`isPro` goes on `CurrentUser`, deliberately not on the session token.** The research prompt behind the plan proposed syncing it into the JWT on every session validation; that was rejected. `useSession()` and `SessionProvider` appear nowhere in `src/`, so there is no client session to keep in sync, and `getCurrentUser()` already reads the row on every request behind React's `cache()` — two more columns on a `findUnique` that is already running cost nothing, where a token claim would cost a query *and* add a second, staler source of truth for a value a **webhook changes behind the visitor's back**. A plain reload after checkout is then enough. **`hasBilling`, not `stripeCustomerId`**: the same reduction `hasPassword` already makes for `passwordHash`, destructured out in the same statement, so the customer id never reaches a component's props. **`configuredOrigin()` is new rather than a reuse of `appOrigin()`, and the distinction is a security one.** `appOrigin(request)` takes a `Request` a server action does not have, and falls back to the request's own origin — which Next derives from the caller's `Host` header. Stripe's `success_url` / `cancel_url` / `return_url` must never come from that: it is the exact attack `appOrigin` was written to prevent (the auth-audit entry records it as a [MEDIUM] finding), and a spoofed `Host` would send a paying visitor to the attacker's domain after checkout. The new function reads `APP_URL` and **throws when unset in every environment, with no fallback at all**, so `APP_URL` becomes strictly required for billing. **`webhookSecret()` throws rather than returning undefined — fail closed, and deliberately unlike `rateLimit`, which fails *open* on missing Upstash config.** An unguarded rate limit is a nuisance; an unverified webhook is a request from anyone claiming to be Stripe, and what it claims is that an account is now Pro. `priceIdFor()` answers `undefined` instead, because its caller has somewhere better to put the failure — a checkout that cannot resolve a price answers the visitor. **The API version is pinned to `2026-08-26.dahlia`**, read out of the installed SDK's own `apiVersion.d.ts` rather than taken from the plan's illustrative `2026-08-27.clover`; the SDK's types accept only the version it ships against, so a stale pin is a build error rather than a runtime surprise, which is the good failure mode. Note `node -p "require('stripe/package.json').version"` **fails** — the package's `exports` map does not expose `./package.json` — so read the version from `package.json` or the package's `VERSION` file instead. **`startCheckoutSchema` takes a plan enum and never a price id**, the same reasoning `createItem` uses for `typeSlug` over `itemTypeId` and `saveUploadPreferences` for its fixed offered set: the client names a choice, the server resolves it to a value, and a raw `price_…` in the payload would let a caller subscribe themselves to any price in the Stripe account, a $0 one included. A test pins that a price id does not parse. **Two smaller calls.** `StartCheckoutInput` was written and then removed — it has no consumer until phase 2's action, and an unused export is what the auth-phase-2 entry records removing `RegisterInput` for. And `setStripeCustomerId` returns `boolean` where the plan had it `void`: it is an `updateMany` guarded on its `count` like every other write in `lib/db/`, and discarding that count would make it the one write in the module that can silently do nothing. `applySubscriptionState` is keyed on `stripeCustomerId` rather than a user id from the webhook payload — the payload is Stripe's, and the customer id is the only field in it this app has itself stored — and uses `updateMany` so a customer with no matching row (a test-mode event replayed against production) is a reportable no-op rather than a `P2025` throw that fails the webhook and makes Stripe retry forever. **`.env.example` was reconciled rather than appended to**: five Stripe placeholders were already sitting in the working tree from an earlier session and were rewritten in the house style, where every entry explains *why*. **`STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` is kept but explicitly marked unused** — checkout is a server-side redirect to a hosted Checkout Session, so no page loads Stripe.js and nothing reads it — following the `R2_PUBLIC_URL` precedent, on the grounds that a subscription integration with no publishable key in sight reads as an omission rather than a decision. A closing note records that `APP_URL` is now strictly required. Plan §0.2 also records that a real `sk_test_…` had briefly been in that working-tree change and has since been cleared; `HEAD` never held it, so there is no history to purge. **16 new tests take the suite 373 → 389**, and **both mutation checks the spec asks for ran and isolated exactly their targets**: `used < limit` → `used <= limit` failed only the two at-the-limit tests (item and collection), and dropping `Math.max(0, …)` failed only the two over-limit ones. Both were reverted, **and the restore was verified with `grep` rather than `git diff`** — the file was untracked at that point, so `git checkout` would have restored nothing, which the file-title entry records learning the hard way. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` and `npm run build` are all clean, with no dev server live and `.next` deleted first. **Nothing was verified in the browser and there is nothing to see** — no route, component or behaviour changed. `src/lib/stripe.ts` and `src/lib/db/billing.ts` reach a live SDK and Prisma respectively and stay **unproven until phase 2 runs them against `stripe listen`**; `priceIdFor` and `webhookSecret` are pure enough for `vi.stubEnv` if something is wanted sooner, and since Vitest loads no `.env` their unconfigured branches are the default state. **Deliberately left for phase 2:** `PRO_TYPE_SLUGS` gaining `books` and the `isProType` predicate — the sidebar renders its PRO badge straight from that set, so adding the slug is a **visible UI change** the moment it lands and belongs with the gate that justifies it; every gate in `createItem`, `createCollection` and `POST /api/upload`; the webhook route, the billing actions and the settings card; and pointing `pricing-cards.tsx` at these constants. Note the phase-2 spec at @context/features/stripe-phase-2-spec.md, and that a **Stripe Dashboard setup** (test-mode products, prices and a webhook endpoint) is a prerequisite no code can do for you — plan §6. Everything the previous entries list stays open: there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature (`collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id) plus the four pure functions the pagination feature added (`parsePageParam`, `pageCount`, `rowsOnPage`, `pageLinks`) are still uncovered. Spec: @context/features/stripe-phase-1-spec.md · Plan: @docs/stripe-integration-plan.md
- **2026-08-29 — Stripe phase 2: integration & UI.** Everything phase 1 left unconsumed. Pro is something an account can buy, and the free tier is something the server enforces. Four new files, seventeen edited, **no migration** (`isPro`, `stripeCustomerId` and `stripeSubscriptionId` have been in the schema since `20260814120000_init`) and **no new dependency** — `stripe` came with phase 1. **The webhook lives at `src/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts`, not the `api/stripe/webhook` the spec and plan both name** — asked for during implementation, so `stripe listen --forward-to` and any dashboard endpoint must point at `/api/webhooks/stripe`; `.env.example`'s comment was corrected, and the plan document still carries the old path. **The webhook is the security boundary and the only thing that writes `isPro`.** Raw body via `request.text()` so the bytes stay the bytes that were signed, `constructEventAsync` (the SubtleCrypto form Stripe documents for route handlers), four handled events and a 200 for everything else, since a non-2xx makes Stripe retry an event we were never going to act on. Every write is keyed on **`stripeCustomerId`** — the one field in Stripe's payload this app has itself stored; the `userId` in the subscription metadata is Stripe's copy of ours and using it would mean trusting the request to name the row it updates. `applySubscriptionState` is an `updateMany` guarded on `count`, so a customer with no matching row is a no-op rather than a `P2025` that fails the delivery and makes Stripe retry forever. **Entitlement is an allow-list** (`active`, `trialing`) rather than a list of exclusions, so a status Stripe adds later withholds Pro rather than granting it, and `stripeSubscriptionId` is **cleared** when Pro stops because the column is `@unique` and a cancelled id left behind blocks the same account re-subscribing. Idempotent by construction — absolute state derived from the subscription, never a delta — so no `processedEvents` table. **Deliberately outside the proxy matcher**, with a comment saying so: the proxy answers an unauthenticated request with a redirect, which Stripe reads as a failed delivery. **One TypeScript finding worth keeping:** the three `customer.subscription.*` events are reached through a `switch` on `event.type` rather than a cast — `event.data.object` is a union across every event type, the first draft's `as Stripe.Subscription` was rejected outright, and a cast would have kept compiling if `HANDLED` ever grew to include an event whose payload is not a subscription. **`startCheckout` / `openBillingPortal` are server actions.** A request names a **plan**, never a price: the price id is the environment's to resolve, the same reasoning `createItem` uses for `typeSlug` over `itemTypeId`, and a raw `price_…` in the payload would let a caller subscribe themselves to any price in the Stripe account including a $0 one — a test pins that a price id sent as a plan does not parse. The Stripe customer is created once and reused, so a returning subscriber keeps one billing history; both return URLs come from phase 1's `configuredOrigin()`, which has **no `Host` fallback at all**. **The settings card renders second and deliberately shows no amounts** — this app holds Stripe *price ids*, never the amounts behind them, so a figure there would be a second place for the real price to drift from, and Stripe's own checkout page states it before anything is charged; the select reads "Monthly" / "Yearly — two months free", which is a ratio rather than a number. Both calls carry the `.catch(() => null)` a rejected *request* needs (a failed *write* answers `{ success: false }`), navigation is `window.location.assign` since the destination is not this app, and `?checkout=success` is worded as **received rather than applied**, because the webhook granting Pro can still be in flight on that first render. A `checkoutOutcome` helper on the page ignores any other value, so the notice cannot be summoned by a hand-typed URL. **Pro without a Stripe customer is a real state and the card handles it** — that is exactly how the seeded demo account is set up, so it shows Pro with "No subscription to manage" rather than a portal button that would fail. **The gates refuse on create only**; existing content stays readable, editable and deletable. `createItem` refuses a Pro type and then the item cap, `createCollection` the collection cap, and `POST /api/upload` answers **403 ahead of its rate limit** — a free account should be told it needs Pro, not told to wait for a window that will refuse it again. Both actions swapped `getCurrentUserId()` for the `cache()`d `getCurrentUser()`, so the whole row costs what the id did, and **the count query is not made at all for a Pro account**. `books` joined `files` and `images` in `PRO_TYPE_SLUGS`, which is what makes the Pro slugs and the upload-holding types **the same set** and is the only reason the upload route can check `isPro` alone: it is handed an `UploadKind` and never the item type slug, so a free type in that group would force the payload to start carrying one. Commented in both places that **if a free type ever gains an upload this is the line that breaks first, and it breaks permissively** — the upload is authorised and only `createItem` refuses, leaving an object in the bucket with no item pointing at it. Note books shipped free and creatable, so unlike files and images this **takes an existing capability away**, and it puts a `PRO` badge on Books immediately in both the sidebar and the create picker, since both render straight from that set — no code needed for either. `UploadNotAllowedError` joined `UploadRateLimitedError` in `src/lib/upload-file.ts` and the drop zone stops the batch on either, so a free account dropping twenty files gets one notice rather than twenty. **The race is accepted and recorded in a comment**: two creates in flight can both read one under the cap and both write, taking a free account one over. Postgres has no cheap constraint — the only exact fix is a transaction around a count and an insert — and one over is not worth paying for on every create. **`pricing-cards.tsx` renders the `FREE_*` constants instead of the literals**, so the page cannot promise a number the server refuses, and its CTAs are session-aware: signed out both go to `/register`, signed in Go Pro goes to `/settings` (a marketing page should not silently start a payment flow) and Get Started to `/dashboard`. That made `(marketing)/page.tsx` async, which costs nothing — the layout is already `force-dynamic` and `getCurrentUser` is `cache()`d, confirmed by `/` still being `ƒ` in the route table. **The plan's `?plan=pro` was deliberately dropped**: nothing reads it, and a dead URL parameter is what the homepage feature already rejected once for the footer links. **`22` new tests take the suite 390 → 412** — 15 over the two actions and 7 over the gates — and **the mutation check the spec named ran and isolated its target**: deleting the `user.isPro` early return in `startCheckout` failed exactly one test, and the file was restored from a copy. **Four existing tests in `items.test.ts` and `collections.test.ts` had to change, and that is the feature's behaviour showing up rather than churn**: both mocked `@/lib/db/user` with `getCurrentUserId` alone, both needed `countItems` / `countCollections` mocked, and the four upload cases now need a Pro account because `files` is a Pro type — `asFileType()` sets it, with a comment saying why. One test also revealed an ordering fact worth knowing: **`createItemSchema` demands a file of a file type and runs before the Pro gate**, so a free account submitting a File item with no file is told "Upload a file first." rather than that it needs Pro — moot in practice, since `POST /api/upload` refuses it before there is a key to send, and the test says so. **Demo is now `isPro: true`** — the seed was changed with the reason in a comment (5 seeded collections against a cap of 3, so a free demo could not create one) and the **development row was updated by hand**, since `npm run db:seed` would have rebuilt demo's content, wiped hand-set favorites and pins and rewritten the password. Confirmed one row via `RETURNING`; the owner's own GitHub account was left free, which makes it the natural account to test both the gates and a real checkout against. Note the Neon MCP `run_sql` tool works again — its schema now takes `project_id` / `branch_id` in snake_case, where the Book feature recorded it rejecting the camelCase its declaration then required. **`npm test` 412, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` all clean**, with `/api/webhooks/stripe` in the route table as dynamic; the dev server was killed by PID with **the port confirmed free by `netstat`** before building, that still being the only reliable check, and the "background command failed with exit code 1" notification that followed is the kill, not a build problem — the thirteenth entry to record that artefact. **Nothing was verified against Stripe, and that is the honest state of this merge.** The manual checklist was deferred on request ("we will do the webhooks later"): `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` is still empty in `.env`, so the handler refuses every delivery by design, and **the webhook, the billing card and the drop-zone stop have never been run against a real event** — all three are outside what `vitest.config.mts` collects, so unit tests cannot stand in for it. The `sk_test_…` key and both `price_…` ids are already configured; production keys are being handled outside this work. Before it can be believed: `stripe listen --forward-to localhost:3000/api/webhooks/stripe`, its `whsec_…` into `.env`, then the spec's checklist — the security section especially (no signature → 400, altered body → 400, `stripe trigger` twice → both 200 with identical final state, secret unset → refuses). **Test cards were recorded in three places** on request — the spec, the working file and the gitignored `/feature browser` action: `4242 4242 4242 4242` succeeds and `4000 0000 0000 0002` is a generic decline, any future expiry, any 3-digit CVC, any 5-digit ZIP. The decline earned its own checklist section because it asks what the happy path cannot — that a failed attempt leaves no `stripeSubscriptionId` behind to block the retry, which the `@unique` column would otherwise turn into what looks like a Stripe problem. **Left open:** the card cannot say which plan an account is on, when it renews, or that a payment failed, because `isPro` is a boolean — `subscriptionStatus` / `stripePriceId` / `currentPeriodEnd` is a migration and out of scope (plan §5.12); an out-of-order delivery is the one idempotency risk left, a stale `updated` landing after a `deleted` would resurrect Pro, and the fix is a `subscriptionUpdatedAt` column; there is no admin path to grant Pro without Stripe beyond editing the row, which is how demo is set up; and the create dialog still offers the Pro types to a free account rather than disabling them, so the refusal comes from the server. Everything the previous entries list stays open: there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature (`collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id) plus the four pure functions the pagination feature added (`parsePageParam`, `pageCount`, `rowsOnPage`, `pageLinks`) are still uncovered. Spec: @context/features/stripe-phase-2-spec.md · Plan: @docs/stripe-integration-plan.md
- **2026-08-29 — Free-tier create gating & Pro upsell.** The free tier stops being something only the server knows about: a free account at the item (50) or collection (3) cap sees a locked create button rather than a create that fails, the three Pro types sort last in the sidebar, and every gated control opens one shared upsell dialog offering the two plans. Three new files (`src/components/billing/billing-provider.tsx`, `upgrade-dialog.tsx`, `src/constants/pricing.ts`), thirteen edited, **no migration** (`prisma/schema.prisma` untouched, so `db:status` was not run), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive** — `dialog`, `button`, `badge` and `sonner` were all already in place. **This is the client half only; the server gates are unchanged and remain the rule.** `createItem`, `createCollection` and `POST /api/upload` still refuse, and the counts the buttons read are a snapshot of the render that produced them — a create refreshes the route, which is what brings them back up to date. **Two decisions were confirmed at `/feature load` and both shaped the implementation.** The request said "disable the button" *and* "on click show a popup", which contradict each other — a genuinely `disabled` button takes no click and the upsell would be unreachable — so every gated control carries **`aria-disabled` and a lock icon but is never `disabled`**, keeping it in the tab order and making the click the thing that raises the dialog. And the upsell covers **both** triggers: the two caps, and a free account choosing a Pro type from the create picker or the sidebar. **One instance of the dialog, not one per button.** `BillingProvider` mounts inside `SidebarProvider` in `AppShell` so it wraps the sidebar as well as the body, holds `{ isPro, items, collections, requestUpgrade }`, and owns the single `UpgradeDialog` — the same reasoning `ItemDrawerProvider` already carries for its sheet: the pages rendering create buttons are server components, and a dialog per button would put a portal behind every one to show at most one. **`AppShell` became the place the caps are read**, and the short-circuit matters: **Pro queries neither count**, the same skip `createItem` makes, so a paying account pays nothing for the gate; both `countItems` and `countCollections` are already `cache()`d, so a page rendering the same number pays for one query. `ClientUsage` was added to `src/lib/usage-limits.ts` as `Omit<UsageLimit, "remaining">` — `remaining` is `Infinity` for Pro, and rather than depend on whether that survives the RSC boundary it simply does not cross it; the gates ask `allowed` and the copy uses `limit` and `used`. **The sidebar's locked row still navigates when it holds items**, which is a deliberate softening of "a Pro type opens the upsell from the sidebar": an account that was Pro and lapsed still owns its files, and replacing that page with a dialog would be taking content away rather than gating what can be added — so the upsell replaces the link only when `itemCount === 0`, a number the sidebar already had. That row becomes a `<button>`, which needed `button.sidebar-link` to flatten the browser's own box back to a link's. **In the create picker a locked radio stays selectable rather than `disabled`** — the click raises the upsell and the controlled `checked` is what puts the selection back, so nothing has to be undone by hand. **Ordering is two lists that must agree**: `TYPE_SLUG_ORDER` in `src/lib/db/item-types.ts` (the sidebar) and `CREATABLE_TYPES` in `src/constants/item-types.ts` (the picker) both moved `links` ahead of `files`, which makes the tail **exactly `PRO_TYPE_SLUGS`** — commented in both places, since the three lists now encode one idea. **Plan amounts moved into `src/constants/pricing.ts`**, read by both the upsell and the marketing pricing card, which had them hardcoded. The module says outright that they are **display copy and not the source of truth** — what is charged is the amount behind the Stripe price id, which this app never reads — so this reduces the places that can drift rather than adding one; the settings billing card still quotes no figure at all, for the same reason it always did. **Goal four turned out to be mostly an audit.** Both create forms and the drop zone already toasted on a failed action and already carried the `.catch(() => null)` a rejected *request* needs. What was missing is that a **client-side validation** failure only set the inline error — which sits at the top of a body that scrolls, so on a long form it can be off-screen when the submit button is not — so both forms now toast it as well. **The existing ordering test caught the reorder**, which is the best evidence it was non-vacuous, and was updated (its fixture also predated `books`); a new `puts every Pro type after every free one` assertion was added and **mutation-checked** — restoring the old order failed exactly those two tests and nothing else, and the file was restored from a copy with `npm test` green again. Suite **412 → 413**. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` and `npm run build` all clean, and all six new CSS hooks were confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and `/feature browser` is opt-in. Three things want an eye on first run: the upsell's two-column plan grid at 390px, whether the locked sidebar row reads as gated rather than broken, and the picker's locked radio putting the selection back after the dialog opens. **One process note:** a dev server was live when the build was due; the user was asked, PID 39120 was killed with `taskkill //F //T` and **the port confirmed free by `netstat`** before `.next` was deleted — still the only reliable check here, since `TaskStop` reports success while leaving the Next child listening. The commit message went through `git commit -F` rather than a heredoc, the history recording two silent heredoc failures on prose containing apostrophes — and **it happened again this session**, on the script that writes this very entry, which is now the third time. **Left open:** the drop zone still answers a refused upload with a toast rather than the upsell, so the one "not allowed" path that does *not* offer the plans is the bulk one; the counts are a per-render snapshot, so a second tab creating items leaves this tab's buttons live until it refreshes (the server still refuses, which is why that is cosmetic); nothing gates *editing* an item into a Pro type, because nothing can change an item's type; and the upsell quotes amounts the app cannot verify against Stripe, as recorded above. Everything the previous entries list stays open: the Stripe webhook, billing card and drop-zone stop have **still never been run against a real Stripe event** (`STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` is unset, so the handler refuses every delivery by design), there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, the `(app)` route group is still not adopted, and the testable surface from the add-items-to-collections feature (`collectionIdsSchema`'s dedupe and absent-means-empty rules, `ownsAllCollections`, and both item actions' refusal of a foreign collection id) plus the four pure functions the pagination feature added (`parsePageParam`, `pageCount`, `rowsOnPage`, `pageLinks`) are still uncovered.
- **2026-08-30 — Upgrade flow tidy-up: one upsell dialog, a top-bar trigger.** The settings billing card stops starting checkout itself and hands off to the shared upsell dialog instead, so every route to Pro now goes through one component. One new file (`src/components/billing/upgrade-trigger.tsx`), four edited, **no migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**; `npm test` stayed at **413 passing**, the diff adding no importable logic. **`UpgradeReason` gained a fourth kind, `{ kind: "plan" }`** — an upgrade asked for outright rather than one prompted by a gate, which the other three all are — with the header reading **"Upgrade"** and the body "Pick a plan and continue to checkout. Cancel any time." **The consequence worth knowing: the settings card now shows amounts.** It deliberately quoted none, because this app holds Stripe *price ids* and never the amounts behind them, so a figure there was a second place for the real price to drift from; the dialog reads `PLAN_PRICING`, the same display constants the marketing pricing section uses, so settings is now consistent with every other place the dialog opens rather than being the one page that said nothing. `startCheckout` is unchanged and still the only thing that talks to Stripe — the plan `<select>` and the direct call simply moved out of the card, and `.settings-row-actions` went with them rather than being left orphaned. **`UpgradeTrigger` is a client component of its own** because `TopBar` is a server component and this needs `useBilling()` — the same reason the two create dialogs construct their own triggers rather than taking one as a prop, and the same `asChild`-clone trap the profile-page improvements entry recorded. It **renders nothing for a Pro account**: there is nothing left to sell, and the settings card is where a subscription is managed. **It is text-only and therefore deliberately not wrapped in `.action-label`** — that class hides the label below the breakpoint, which works only for a button with an icon left to show, so this one keeps its text on mobile while the two create buttons beside it collapse to icons. The favorites star was removed from the top bar in the same pass, so `/favorites` is now reachable only from the dashboard's stat card and the sidebar. The Pro plan badge became solid `--type-snippet` with white text rather than a 12% tint of it. **Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` (413) and `npm run build` all clean, with the dev server stopped and **the port confirmed free by `netstat`** before `.next` was deleted — still the only reliable check here, since `TaskStop` reports success while leaving the Next child listening. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests. Two things want an eye on first run: the top bar now carries three buttons competing for width, and the Upgrade label staying visible at 390px while its neighbours collapse. **A separate finding from the same session, not fixed here:** checkout on the deployed Vercel app answers "Could not reach the server. Try again.", which is the `.catch(() => null)` in `settings-billing.tsx` and can *only* mean the server action's promise rejected — every Stripe and env misconfiguration inside `startCheckout` is caught and answers "Could not start checkout. Try again." instead. So it is transport-level: a stale build id after a redeploy (404), an uncaught throw at `getCurrentUser()` (500 — and `.env.production` still flags `DATABASE_URL` as the *direct* Neon host rather than the `-pooler` one), or the proxy 307ing the action POST, since its matcher covers `/settings` and a server action posts to the current route. The status code on that POST tells them apart. Worth knowing regardless that **`.env.production` is gitignored and Vercel never sees it**, so the five `STRIPE_*` variables and `APP_URL` have to be set in the Vercel project's environment. **Left open:** everything the previous entries list — the Stripe webhook has still never been run against a real event in production, the drop zone still answers a refused upload with a toast rather than the upsell, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
- **2026-08-30 — Account deletion removes uploaded files from R2.** Deleting a single item removed its object; deleting a whole account did not, so every file, image and book cover that account uploaded was orphaned the moment the row went. Two new files (`scripts/sweep-orphans.ts`, `src/lib/account.test.ts`), eight edited, **no migration** (`prisma/schema.prisma` changed only a comment), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. **The bug was worse than it looks and that shapes the fix:** `Item` cascades from `User`, so the rows holding the keys — `fileUrl` is the only column that holds one, book covers included — disappear at the same moment the objects become orphans, which is why `deleteAccount` collects them *before* the `deleteMany` and why the comment there tells the next reader not to move the read down next to its use. **The ordering was decided rather than inherited:** objects first, refusing the deletion when the store is unreachable — which is what `deleteItem` does — was rejected because it lets a vendor outage block someone from leaving, a bad answer to an erasure request and a worse one under GDPR than a logged orphan. So the account always goes; a failed object delete is swallowed, logged with the count and `npm run r2:sweep`, and `count === 0` returns before the sweep so a second submission of the dialog does not duplicate the log noise. The deliberate consequence is that an R2 failure still leaves an orphan — this makes them the exception rather than the rule and gives them a cleanup path. **`deleteFiles` was split into two exports, a small deviation from the spec.** The spec puts the `ownsObjectKey` filter inside `deleteFiles(keys)`, but the sweep has no single owner to check against, its keys coming from a listing diffed against the database — so `deleteObjects(keys)` does the bulk work and `deleteFiles(userId, keys)` filters and delegates. The filter should never drop anything, since the keys come from that user's own rows, but `fileUrl` is a stored string and it is the one check standing between a data bug and deleting someone else's file. **The trap in the bulk delete is that a partial failure is a 200 carrying an `Errors` array rather than a throw**, so a `try`/`catch` alone reads it as success and the best-effort sweep silently becomes no effort; chunking is at the S3 API's 1,000-key limit, which R2 implements. **Part B, `npm run r2:sweep`,** follows `scripts/prune-users.ts`: lists everything under `uploads/` with pagination, diffs against the `fileUrl` of every live item, reports the orphans with a total size, dry run by default. **The 24-hour age threshold is the detail most likely to be missed and it destroys user data if it is** — the object is written before the item exists, so anything picked in a dialog still open looks exactly like an orphan; an object with **no** `LastModified` counts as too new, since the point is not to delete anything whose age cannot be ruled out. It refuses under `NODE_ENV=production` unless `--allow-production`, which is a deviation from `db:prune`'s outright refusal and deliberate: the backlog this reclaims is mostly *in* production, so a permanent refusal would stop it doing the thing it was written for. **The single most important thing this feature found, and it is not a code bug: development and production share one bucket.** `.env` and `.env.production` both name `devstash-files` while pointing at different Neon branches, and the sweep diffs the bucket against whichever database it is given — so run under `.env` it treats every *production* user's file as an orphan. The live dry run reported **64 objects, 40 referenced, 4 skipped as too new, 20 orphans totalling 126 MB**, and **two of the three orphan prefixes belong to no user in the development database** (one alone is ~124 MB, including the 100 MB `.txt` from the direct-to-R2 feature). They are either deleted-account leftovers or live production files; CLAUDE.md forbids reading production to find out, so the question was left open rather than guessed at. **`npm run r2:sweep -- --yes` must not be run until that is resolved** — separate buckets per environment is the real fix, failing that the sweep should union the referenced keys from both databases. The header warning in the script covers the case, but a warning is not a safeguard. **Verified end to end in the browser against the dev branch and the real bucket**, which is what the whole feature rests on: a throwaway account registered through the UI, two files uploaded through the create dialog (stored with `fileSize: 35`, R2's own size rather than the client's claim), both objects confirmed present with `headFile`, the account deleted through the confirmation dialog, and **both objects then `null`** — with the user row, its items and the `VerificationToken` rows under **both** identifiers gone, the 8 system item types and demo's 104 items untouched, and the sweep afterwards reporting **zero** for that prefix on totals byte-identical to before, so the deletion left no new orphans. Also confirmed no regression in the R2 read paths: a 104 KB PDF through `/api/items/[id]/file` with correct headers, 21/21 image previews loading, 0 console errors, no overflow at 390px. **That verification needed one approved write to the development branch** — a new account lands free and unverified, and uploads are Pro-gated since Stripe phase 2, so the spec's own verification path predates the gating and cannot be walked without it; one `UPDATE` set `emailVerified` and `isPro` on an account registered seconds earlier, and that account is now deleted, so nothing from the pass remains. **Tests went 413 → 428** — 8 over `deleteAccount` (which had **no test file at all**) and 7 over the bulk delete, and **both mutation checks isolated exactly their target**: moving the `getUserFileKeys` call below the `deleteMany` failed only the call-order test, and disabling the `Errors` branch failed only the partial-failure test. **The near-miss worth remembering: `src/lib/r2.test.ts` already existed and was overwritten.** The spec's "files to create" table did not mention it and it was written fresh, destroying 11 tests for `objectKey` / `ownsObjectKey`; what caught it was a baseline test count that did not add up (417 rather than the expected 428), not the suite going red — the new file passed on its own. Restored from `git show HEAD:` and merged, with `git diff` confirming the only removals were two import lines. **Check whether a test file exists before writing one**, and treat a test count that does not reconcile as a real signal. **Two tooling notes.** Python invoked from the Bash tool is the *Windows* interpreter, so it reads `/tmp` as `C:\tmp` while Git Bash's `/tmp` is under `AppData\Local\Temp` — a file written by `cp` and read by Python silently does not exist; use repo-relative paths for anything crossing between them. And Playwright's `browser_file_upload` refuses a path outside the allowed roots (the repo and `.playwright-mcp`), so a fixture in the scratchpad cannot be uploaded — put it in `.playwright-mcp` and delete it afterwards, remembering that two `.yml` files there are **tracked** and a blanket `rm -rf` has destroyed them once before. **What could not be verified:** `browser_run_code_unsafe` would not accept code in this build (every form returned a `SyntaxError` on the first token), so there was no `page.route()` to fail the request with and **the forced-`deleteFiles`-rejection path was never exercised in the browser** — it rests on two unit tests, one of which asserts the log message names the count and the recovery command. The first attempt to click Delete Account was also refused by the auto-mode classifier and was not worked around; it succeeded once the element description made the intent explicit. **Also done:** the `fileUrl` comment in `prisma/schema.prisma` said "Cloudflare R2 URL" and has held a *key* since the first upload feature, and §7 of @context/features/privacy-page-content.md was rewritten — it had to disclose the orphaned files as a limitation, which was the whole reason this is a prerequisite for AI feature 2 (@context/features/ai-controls-spec.md); the pre-publish checklist item demanding this fix is gone with it. **Left open:** the shared bucket above, which is the one thing wanting a decision; `deleteObjects` has no retry, so a transient R2 error orphans the batch until the next sweep; the sweep is unbounded in memory, holding every key in a `Set`, which is fine at 64 objects and worth revisiting at six figures; and `npm run r2:sweep -- --yes` **silently no-ops under PowerShell**, which eats the `--` so the script reads it as a dry run — run it through the Bash tool or use `--%`, as the email-verification entry records costing real time on `db:prune`. Everything the previous entries list stays open: there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, the edit drawer still cannot replace a file, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unused, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted. Spec: @context/features/account-deletion-r2-spec.md
- **2026-08-31 — A development R2 bucket, and the bulk-drop upload fix.** Development moved onto its own bucket (`devstash-dev`), and the bulk drop zone stopped failing every dropped file. Done outside the feature workflow and committed straight to master as `637fef0` — one new file (`scripts/reset-dev-uploads.ts`), three edited, **no migration** and **no new dependency**. **The bucket split is the fix for what the account-deletion feature had surfaced the day before:** `.env` and `.env.production` both named `devstash-files` while reading different Neon branches, so an object key said nothing about which environment owned it and `npm run r2:sweep -- --yes` was unsafe — every production file looks like an orphan when diffed against the development database. **Two things had to be done in the Cloudflare dashboard and neither is something the app's own credentials can do.** The R2 API token was **scoped to the old bucket**, which was diagnosed rather than guessed: the same key listed `devstash-files` fine, returned `AccessDenied` on `devstash-dev`, and was refused `ListBuckets` — the signature of a per-bucket token. And **a new bucket has no CORS policy**, which the history already records as failing with an opaque `net::ERR_FAILED` naming nothing; `PutBucketCors` answers 403 with an object read/write token, so the dashboard is the only route. Both were fixed by the user. **Verified with a throwaway script** (written, run, deleted) exercising every path the app uses against the new bucket: read permission, a presigned PUT signing `content-length;content-type;host` with **no stray checksum param**, a real upload, `headFile` size match, `getFile`, an over-length body refused with **403** (so the cap is R2's rule and not the form's), the **CORS preflight** answering `allow-origin http://localhost:3000`, bulk delete, and the object then gone. All ten passed. **`npm run dev:reset-uploads` is the migration path**, following `prune-users.ts`: once `R2_BUCKET_NAME` points somewhere new, every `fileUrl` in the database names a key the new bucket does not hold, so the rows have to go too or every file row, image tile and book cover breaks. It selects on **`fileUrl IS NOT NULL` rather than a list of type slugs**, so files, images, book covers and any future upload type are covered without editing it; dry run by default, `--yes` to execute, **refuses under `NODE_ENV=production` with no override** (unlike `r2:sweep`, since this deletes content rather than reclaiming unreferenced objects), re-runs `ownsObjectKey` per row, and **prints the bucket and the database host before anything else** — which-bucket-which-database being the exact thing the split exists to make unambiguous. **Objects are deleted before rows, the opposite of `deleteAccount` and deliberately so:** there the account must go even when R2 is unreachable, whereas here a failed object delete should stop the run with the rows intact, those rows being the only record of which keys to retry. **The upload bug was reported as "single file works, multiple files fail", and that framing is wrong in an instructive way** — it is not single versus multiple but **dialog versus drop zone**, the dialog being the single-file path and the drop zone the batch one; a single *dropped* file failed identically. The cause: **`itemFields.author` is `.nullable()` but not `.optional()`**, so it accepts `null` and rejects `undefined`. `ItemCreateForm` spreads `ItemFormValues`, which carries `author: ""`; **`ItemDropZone` builds its payload by hand** and never got the field when the book feature added it. So every drop failed `createItemSchema` with "Invalid input: expected string, received undefined" — and because the object is PUT to R2 *before* `createItem` runs, **each failure orphaned a file**, which is exactly what "files are uploaded in R2 but not saved in DB" was describing. The fix is one field plus a comment saying the payload is hand-built and will not inherit the next one. **The durable half is five regression tests** pinning the drop payload against `createItemSchema` in `src/lib/validations/item.test.ts`: the drop zone is a component and cannot be collected by Vitest, but its *payload shape* can be, and the shape is where the drift was — so the next omission breaks `npm test` rather than silently orphaning files. **Mutation-checked**: removing `author` again fails three of them, the first being the one that matters. Suite **428 → 433**, with `tsc`, `eslint` and the build clean. **Two process notes.** `git add -A` swept the commit-message file into the previous feature's commit; it was caught on the `git status` immediately after and amended out before anything was merged or pushed, and this commit staged explicit paths instead — worth doing every time. And a differential test (same credentials, two buckets) settled the token question in one run where reasoning from the error message alone would have left "wrong account?", "wrong endpoint?" and "wrong bucket name?" all open. **Left open:** development's 15.4 MB of file/image/book objects **stays in `devstash-files`**, since `dev:reset-uploads` now targets the new bucket — reclaimable later with `r2:sweep` pointed at the old one, but only once the question below is answered; **the 20 orphans / 126 MB in `devstash-files` are still unclassified**, two of the three prefixes belonging to no development user and therefore either deleted-account leftovers or live production files, so `--yes` there remains unsafe; and **`optionalText` still rejects `undefined` for all five optional text fields** while `collectionIds` and `file` accept it, so the next hand-built payload can reproduce this exact bug — making them consistent would kill the class, at the cost of absent-means-null on updates, which is safe today (the one edit caller sends every field) but a worse failure mode than a loud error. Deliberately left as the user's call rather than changed unilaterally.

- **2026-08-31 — AI foundation (AI feature 1 of 6).** The plumbing every AI feature routes through, with **every consumer deliberately absent** — no action, no component, no settings card, no privacy page, and nothing that calls OpenAI. Eight new modules plus four test files, seven edited, **one migration** (`20260831114644_add_ai_preferences`, `User.aiPreferences Json?`), **one dependency** (`openai` ^7.8.0) and no new ShadCN primitive. The app builds and behaves identically before and after. **That is the point of the split, and it is the same one Stripe phase 1 made:** everything lands in `src/lib/`, which is exactly what `vitest.config.mts` collects, so **all of it is provable with `npm test`** where the features that follow need a real API key and a browser. **`src/lib/openai.ts`** follows `stripe.ts`, `resend.ts` and `r2.ts`: one instance per process with the key read **lazily**, so importing cannot crash a build or a request that never talks to OpenAI. Two non-default settings, each a decision — `timeout: 30_000`, because the SDK default is 10 minutes and on a serverless host the platform's own limit fires first and the visitor gets nothing useful; and `maxRetries: 1`, because every retry is a second full-price call and the SDK retries on 429, which for a limit measured per account is exactly when retrying is wrong. `AI_MODEL` is a constant rather than a literal at four call sites. **`src/lib/ai/run.ts` is the single call path and it never throws** — every SDK error is mapped to a discriminated failure, so a feature has one shape to handle and no way to leak an SDK error into a toast. Three things in it are easy and expensive to get wrong. **`gpt-5-nano` is a reasoning model**, so `temperature`, `top_p`, `presence_penalty`, `frequency_penalty`, `logprobs`, `logit_bias` and `max_tokens` are **unsupported and error** rather than being ignored — steering is `reasoning.effort` and `text.verbosity` only, one added by habit would break all four features at once, and a test asserts the call object carries none of the eight. **The system prompt goes in `instructions` and never into `input`**, which keeps the cache prefix static and also keeps the untrusted content textually separate from the instructions about it; the content is wrapped in `<content>` delimiters the prompts name explicitly. And **`output_parsed` can be `null`** when the model refuses or the parse fails — a real branch with its own message. An `AuthenticationError` (or `PermissionDeniedError`) means **our** key is wrong: it logs loudly and answers generically, the same split `startCheckout` already makes for an unconfigured Stripe price, and a test asserts the visitor-facing string contains no mention of a key while the log does. **`src/lib/ai/spend.ts` is a global monthly ledger in Redis**, integer micro-dollars, one key per calendar month — integers because floating-point accumulation over thousands of increments drifts. **Global rather than per-account deliberately:** the requirement is that the owner's bill is bounded, and a per-account cap does nothing about fifty accounts spending a dollar each. **Check before, increment after**, because a call's cost is not known until its response reports the tokens — so **the cap can be overshot by at most one call**, which is stated in a comment as a real property rather than hidden as a rounding error. **The arithmetic that matters: cached tokens are subtracted from the input before pricing.** The API reports them as a *detail of* `input_tokens` rather than in addition to it, so pricing both counts bills the cached share twice, once at the cached rate and again at ten times that — and it **fails in the safe direction**, over-counting so the cap trips early, which is exactly why it could sit unnoticed for a long time and why it was the mutation check the spec named. **`checkSpend` fails CLOSED**, a deliberate inconsistency with `rateLimit`, which still fails open and is untouched by this feature: if the ledger cannot be read the spend is unknown, and spending an unknown amount against a hard cap is precisely what the cap exists to prevent. The codebase already has this split — `rateLimit` fails open while `webhookSecret()` throws — and the consequence to accept is that **an Upstash outage disables AI entirely** and nothing else. `recordSpend` never throws, since a ledger write that fails must not turn a suggestion the visitor already has into an error they see; the increment is simply lost, which is one more reason the dashboard budget limit is the real guarantee. It sets a TTL **only on the first write of the month** — expiring on every call would push the window forward forever and the key would never fall out. **`src/lib/ai/truncate.ts`** applies a 24,000-character budget before any call, because the cost risk is not the per-call price (a tagging call is a hundredth of a cent) but that `Item.content` is `@db.Text` and an uploaded file may be 100 MB. It takes the **head**, which matters most for explaining code — a file's imports and top-level structure are where an explanation starts — and cuts on characters rather than tokens, a tokenizer being another dependency to keep in step with the model for a budget that is deliberately loose. **`src/lib/validations/ai.ts`** holds the request and output schemas together, which is the point: the output schema is what constrains the model *and* what the answer is validated against, the model being a remote service returning untrusted JSON like any other. Every action takes an **item id and nothing else** — the action re-reads the item with the session's user in the `where`, so a caller can neither have the model work on someone else's text nor inflate the bill by posting their own megabyte, and a test asserts a megabyte of `content` sent alongside the id is *stripped*. The tag cap is **enforced rather than trimmed**: a model returning forty tags has not done the task, and silently keeping the first eight would hide that. **`User.aiPreferences`** follows `uploadPreferences` exactly — a strict write schema plus a lenient field-by-field reader, so a value written by a future version or edited by hand costs that one preference rather than resetting the object — and is read by `getCurrentUser`, so the off-switch check every later action makes costs no query of its own. **It defaults on**, because nothing here calls OpenAI in the background, so "enabled" is permission to act on a click rather than consent to anything happening unprompted. `AiPreferences` is a **`type` and not an `interface`**, the Prisma `Json` input type wanting an index signature that TypeScript infers for an alias but never for an interface — the trap the editor-preferences feature recorded. **Three deviations from the spec, all deliberate.** The preferences schemas got **their own `src/lib/validations/ai-preferences.ts`** rather than sharing `ai.ts`: the spec's file table put `AiPreferences` in `types/ai.ts` while also saying "following `uploadPreferences` exactly", and that pattern is a dedicated validations module — so the type stays runtime-free in `types/ai.ts` as specified and the schemas sit beside `editor-preferences.ts` and `upload-preferences.ts`. **`DESCRIPTION_MAX_LENGTH` and `TAG_MAX_LENGTH` are now exported from `item.ts`** so the output schemas reuse them rather than restating the numbers, which is what makes "a suggestion that cannot be stored is never offered" true by construction. And **the ledger builds its own Redis client** rather than sharing `rate-limit.ts`'s, whose getter logs "rate limiting is disabled" — the wrong thing to say here — and whose whole contract is failing open where this one fails closed; two HTTP clients in a process is a cheap price for not blurring those behaviours into one module. **56 new tests take the suite 433 → 489**, with `tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` clean and `db:status` reporting the migration applied. **Both mutation checks isolated exactly their target and were reverted:** removing the cached-token subtraction failed only the test naming that rule, and the run wrapper's forbidden-parameter test is written so an added `temperature` fails immediately. **The one manual check the spec asks for passes**: with `OPENAI_API_KEY` genuinely unset, the module imports fine, `getOpenAI()` throws only when a client is actually built, and `runStructured` maps that to `{ ok: false, reason: "unavailable" }` — so an unconfigured environment degrades to "AI unavailable" rather than breaking a build or a request. **One environment note:** `npx prisma generate` was run by hand after `db:migrate`, as two previous features record needing — the generated client does not always pick up a new column and `tsc` then fails on lines that have nothing wrong with them. A dev server was live when the build was due; the user was asked, the PID was killed and **the port confirmed free by `netstat`** before `.next` was deleted, that still being the only reliable check since `TaskStop` reports success while leaving the Next child listening. **Left open:** `AI_MONTHLY_BUDGET_USD` was **not in `.env`** when this shipped (**corrected 2026-08-31: it is now set to 5 in both `.env` and `.env.production`, and in the Vercel project's environment**) — unset falls back to $5 and never to unlimited, so nothing is broken, but the fallback is a safety net rather than an intended number; **the OpenAI dashboard budget limit is still the only control that actually stops the money**, and everything here is the second line of defence behind it; the four prompts in `prompts.ts` had **never been sent to a model** when this shipped, so their quality was unproven (**corrected 2026-08-31: they have since been exercised live — the OpenAI dashboard shows 10 requests and 8.122K input tokens**); `prompt_cache_key` is plumbed but will not fire for tagging or summaries, prompt caching needing a ≥1,024-token prefix that a well-written tagging instruction does not reach — and padding one to get there loses money; and nothing caches a result against the item, so a repeat suggestion on an unchanged item pays again. Everything the previous entries list stays open: development's 15.4 MB still sits in `devstash-files` and its 20 orphans are still unclassified, `optionalText` still rejects `undefined` for the five optional text fields, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, downloads still stream through `/api/items/[id]/file`, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted. Spec: @context/features/ai-foundation-spec.md · Plan: @docs/ai-integration-plan.md

- **2026-08-31 — Privacy page & the AI off switch (AI feature 2 of 6).** The two things that had to exist **before anything in this app calls OpenAI**: a page saying what gets sent and where, and a switch that stops it. Four new files, nine edited, **no migration** (`User.aiPreferences` landed in feature 1 — this feature is that column's first writer), **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. **They ship together deliberately:** a disclosure saying *"we send your content to OpenAI"* is a weaker document than one that also says *"and here is the switch that stops it"*, and building the page now and the switch a month later would have meant a month of the weaker version. **This feature still makes no AI calls** — feature 3 is the first thing that reaches OpenAI, and the ordering is the whole point: **no call ever precedes the page describing it.** The consequence, named so it does not read as an oversight: for one commit the app has a privacy page and a settings switch for features that do not exist yet, which is the right way round. **`/privacy` is a server component in the existing `(marketing)` route group and is deliberately absent from the proxy matcher** — someone deciding whether to sign up has to be able to read it — verified answering **200 signed out** with the copy rendering. It **restores the Privacy footer link** the homepage feature deleted along with six others rather than point them at 404s, with a note that it should come back when the page existed; Terms stays absent, being a different document nobody has written. **`saveAiPreferences` follows `saveUploadPreferences` exactly**: the account comes from the session and **never** the payload, so a request can name the setting it wants but not whose it is; the **strict** schema is what keeps a partial object out of the column, the lenient reader existing for values written *around* the app rather than as a licence to write half a set through it; and it is **not rate limited**, matching the item, collection and editor actions rather than the profile ones, which are throttled because each attempt costs a bcrypt. **The card carries the `.catch(() => null)` that a failed *request* needs** — the action answers a failed *write* with `{ success: false }` but **rejects** when it cannot be reached, and without the catch the switch keeps showing a value the server never stored. **That defect has shipped and been fixed four times in this project**, and the spec said not to make it five; it has not. It is optimistic with the previous value restored on failure, matching the upload card, and holds its own state rather than reading `BillingProvider` — which carries the value *down* to the controls that hide themselves, so a round trip back up would be a second source of truth for one boolean. **`aiEnabled` rides the same `BillingProvider` path `isPro` takes**, with the distinction commented because it matters: a Pro-gated control renders **inert** and offers an upgrade, while an AI control with this false **does not render at all** — an off switch that leaves disabled buttons scattered around has not really turned anything off, and that makes the server check every future action does belt-and-braces for a stale page, which is what it should be.
