The document itself was written first, as advice rather than as a spec, and §2 (streaming) and §4 (caching tags/summaries/optimize results) are deliberately out of scope — §2 is its own feature and wants a measurement from this one before it is worth building. **Where the time actually went, in order of size:** reasoning tokens, because `gpt-5-nano` is a reasoning model that generates them *before* any answer and bills them at the **output** rate, which makes `reasoning.effort` the one knob that moves latency and cost together; nothing being streamed; and prefill, since `AI_CHARACTER_BUDGET` was 24,000 characters (~6,000 tokens) on **every** call including tagging. **Worth knowing so it is not chased: prompt caching cannot help here** — it needs a ≥1,024-token static prefix and the observed requests run ~812 input tokens in total, so `prompt_cache_key` is plumbed on all four calls and is currently decorative; padding a prompt to reach the threshold costs more than the discount returns. **§1 — explain drops to `effort: "low"` and keeps `verbosity: "medium"`, and the split is the point rather than a compromise between them.** Verbosity governs how much answer is produced and this is the one feature whose answer *is* the product, so it keeps the higher setting; effort governs how long the model deliberates first, and on a model this small the extra deliberation was not buying an answer worth the wait. The original justification for `medium` still argues for the higher verbosity and never argued on its own for the higher effort. **Nothing already cached is invalidated, which was checked rather than assumed:** `explanationSourceHash` covers the code and the language hint, `freshExplanation` also compares `AI_MODEL`, and **neither sees `effort`** — so answers produced at the old setting stay served, which is right (they are still good answers) and deliberately not worth a forced invalidation, with Regenerate there for anyone who disagrees. Explain's *budget* is unchanged too, so the digest — computed over the raw content, before truncation — is unaffected from either direction. **§3 — `TAG_CHARACTER_BUDGET` (3,000) and `SUMMARY_CHARACTER_BUDGET` (6,000)** joined the existing constant in `src/lib/ai/truncate.ts`, which is now documented as the ceiling *and* as what explain and optimize send: those two are the features that genuinely want the whole artifact, since an explanation of half a file is an explanation of a different file and a rewrite that never saw the end of a prompt drops its requirements. Tagging reads the opening and its eight words cannot use more; summarising reads further in and still nothing like the whole file. `truncateForAi` already took a `budget` parameter and nothing had ever passed one. **`describeItem` takes the budget as a parameter rather than being copied into two helpers**, because it is the function that keeps the privacy page's "only the title, description and content are sent" true and there should go on being one of it; truncation stays head-first and still applies to the **assembled block**, so a long content field cannot push the title or the language hint out of what is sent. The draft tag path shares `suggest` and therefore shares the tag budget, which is right — a draft is not a reason to send more than the stored item would. **Two existing assertions described behaviour that is deliberately changing and were updated rather than deleted** (explain's `effort: "medium"`, and the two tag paths measured against `AI_CHARACTER_BUDGET`), with the explain test renamed to say what the split now is so a future raise reads as the latency regression it would be. **The one new test pins the ordering through real behaviour** — tags below summary below the ceiling — rather than reading the constants back, which would only have tested a literal. **Both changes were mutation-checked and each isolated exactly its target**: restoring `effort: "medium"` failed one test, and making `describeItem` ignore its budget failed the three budget tests; both were restored from a copy and the suite re-confirmed green. `npm test` 626, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src` and `npm run build` all clean. **The user verified it on localhost before the merge** and judged the explain answers still good at the lower effort, which is the only question a green build could not answer — if it had read thinner, the fix was to revert one line and keep §3. **Two process notes.** `context/current-feature.md` was **overwritten with the Write tool without being read first**, which destroyed all 82 History entries; caught immediately on the `git diff --stat` afterwards (+27/−2 was the expected shape, and anything else would have shown) and restored with `git show HEAD:… | tail -n +12`. Read a file before overwriting it, and check the stat after — the working file is the one place in this repo where a careless write loses something irreplaceable. And the dev server was live at build time; the user had asked to test on localhost first, so it was killed by PID only once they were done, with **the port confirmed free by `netstat` filtered on `LISTENING`** before `.next` was deleted — the "background command failed with exit code 1" notification that followed is the kill of the earlier background dev task, not a build problem, which is the sixteenth entry to record that artefact. **Left open:** **no measurement was taken** — the improvement is reasoned from where the tokens go and confirmed by feel, not timed, so the number §2 wants before it is worth building still does not exist; the two new budgets are first guesses that no data supports yet, and tagging at 3,000 characters is the one most likely to be too tight, the failure mode being blander tags on a long item rather than an error; nothing streams, so explain still shows a spinner until the whole answer lands; and tags, summaries and optimized prompts still have no cache, so asking twice about the same unchanged input pays twice. Everything the previous entries list stays open: the OpenAI dashboard budget limit is still the only control that actually stops the money, production still needs `npm run db:deploy` to carry `20260831180910_add_item_explanation` across, sign out still has no pending state, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unreferenced, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted. Advice: @docs/ai-latency-improvements.md §1 and §3
- **2026-08-31 — Image lightbox with zoom and a draggable minimap.** Clicking an image in the item drawer opens it full screen, where the wheel, a pinch or the toolbar zooms it from fit to 5x and a drag pans it; once past fit a minimap appears top right showing the whole picture with the visible part boxed, and pressing or dragging that box moves the view. Three new files (`src/lib/image-zoom.ts` + its test, `src/components/items/image-lightbox.tsx`), two edited, **no migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive** — `dialog` came with the profile improvements. **Presentation only:** no query and no server change, the popup showing the same `/api/items/[id]/file` response the drawer already rendered, which has always been the full-size original rather than a thumbnail. It covers Image items **and** Book covers by construction, both rendering through the one `ItemFile` branch. **Four decisions were taken at load, all recommended and confirmed.** Gallery tiles **keep opening the drawer** — that is the gallery's only route to an item's tags, collections, download and delete, so putting the lightbox on the tile would have taken something away; it opens from the drawer preview instead. **Hand-rolled on the existing `dialog`** rather than `yet-another-react-lightbox` or `react-medium-image-zoom`, each of which ships a stylesheet the no-utility-classes rule and the `--type-*` palette would have had to fight. Fit to 5x, continuous on the wheel, a 0.5 step on the buttons, double click toggling fit and 2x. And **the geometry went into `src/lib/`** so `vitest.config.mts` can collect it — a component alone is untestable by configuration, and the clamping is the part with rules worth pinning. **The dialog fills the viewport and paints its own dark ground**, which is why it is controlled rather than left to Radix: what reads as a backdrop *is* the content, so the primitive's own outside-click never fires and the stage handles the dismissing click itself, ignoring one that ends a drag. **The wheel listener is attached by hand as non-passive** — React registers `onWheel` passively at the root, so `preventDefault` there is a no-op and a ctrl-wheel would zoom the whole browser instead of the picture. **The zoom-about-a-point maths is what makes the wheel feel right** (`offsetForZoom` holds whatever is under the cursor still), and the wheel step is multiplicative rather than fixed so one notch is the same proportion at 1x as at 4x. **`clampOffset` is what stops the picture being dragged off into a corner**, and it is measured against `containSize`, which caps its ratio at 1 — the CSS has to agree, which is why the image is `object-fit: scale-down` and not `contain`: a picture smaller than the stage stays at its own size, and if the two disagreed the image could be dragged past an edge the bounds do not think is there. **The bug worth remembering, found by measuring rather than by eye.** The first pass sized the image with `max-width: 100%; max-height: 100%`, and on a 3804x9200 cover the element rendered **1039x2513 in a 1039x604 stage** — four screens deep, with the picture running off the bottom. `max-height: 100%` had computed to **`none`**: the stage takes its height from the dialog's grid row rather than from a `height` of its own, so a percentage max-height had no definite containing block to resolve against. **Two further attempts failed for reasons worth knowing**: `height: 100%` hit the identical wall, and `position: absolute; inset: 0` alone did nothing because an `img` is a **replaced element** and keeps its intrinsic size rather than stretching to its insets. What works is absolute positioning **plus** explicit `width` / `height: 100%`, the percentages now resolving against a positioned ancestor with a used height; all three facts are in the comment on the rule so it is not "simplified" back. **The minimap needed the inverse mapping and its own component.** `viewportRect` gives the visible part as fractions of the whole; dragging it names *a place in the image* where dragging the picture names *a distance to move by*, so `offsetForCentre` inverts it — and the round trip between the two is the test that matters, because if they ever disagree the picture jumps away from the box on the first press. A press **inside** the box drags from where it was grabbed, so the box does not snap out from under the pointer; a press **outside** jumps straight there, that press being a request to look somewhere else. **`stopPropagation` is genuinely required here** and is not the no-op it would be elsewhere in this codebase: the minimap is a *child* of the stage and both pan on the same pointer events, so without it a drag moved the picture twice over — which would have read as "too fast" rather than as obviously broken, and is exactly what the 20px-drag measurement below was written to catch. `click` and `dblclick` are stopped too, the stage reading those as dismiss and zoom-toggle. **A second defect was caught by reading rather than by symptom**: `zoomTo` called `setOffset` *inside* the `setZoom` updater, and React is free to invoke an updater twice — StrictMode does — which applied the zoom-about-a-point correction twice and threw the picture away from the cursor it was anchored to. Both values are now computed and set separately. That is the same trap the upload-preferences card records, and it is now recorded twice. **32 new tests take the suite 626 to 658**, and the sign in `viewportRect` was **mutation-checked**: flipping the minus to a plus failed exactly the two tests naming that rule and nothing else, and the file was restored **from a copy rather than with `git checkout`**, which does nothing for a file that is still untracked — a trap this history already records. `clampOffset` normalises a negative zero, which is harmless in a transform and surprising anywhere the offset is compared. **Verified in the browser against the Neon dev branch as demo**, reading the DOM rather than screenshots, because a layout bug this specific cannot be diagnosed any other way: at fit all three real images (3804x9200, 1536x1600, 3832x9200) render at exactly the stage size with the bottom edge on the viewport bottom and no page overflow, at 390px too; at 200% the minimap appears with a box reading 100% wide by 50% tall at top 25%, correct for a portrait; a 3000px drag clamps at -301.8 against an exact bound of 302.4; **a 20px drag in the minimap moved the image -75.3px against an expected -75.3, where a leaked press would have given -150.6**; a press low in the minimap jumps and a grab-and-drag returns to the top, with a 500px overshoot staying clamped and the zoom untouched; a drag released over the empty stage leaves it open while a plain click there closes it; Escape closes the lightbox only, the drawer survives, and focus returns to the trigger; and with both closed `body` pointer-events is back to `auto` and the page is genuinely clickable again. Zero console errors or warnings. Every new rule was confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS, which rewrote `transform-origin: center` to `50%`, grouped the two reduced-motion overrides, and as ever emitted a plain fallback declaration ahead of each `color-mix` — and note the built stylesheet is **not** the first `.css` chunk but the large one, so a grep against `head -1` finds nothing and looks exactly like a rule that did not survive. **Left open:** **the minimap is `aria-hidden` and there is no keyboard path to panning at all** — it is a second route to a gesture that is itself pointer-only, so hiding it is honest rather than a regression, but arrow-key panning on the stage is what would actually close that gap and is the obvious follow-up; on a 390px screen the minimap for a 1:3.7 portrait is 88x213, about a third of the stage height, which is correct and bounded but cannot be capped by height in CSS without either squashing the picture or sizing the box in JS; there is no next/previous within the gallery, so the lightbox shows one image and closing is how you reach another; and the drawer thumbnail keeps its own 24rem cap, untouched. Everything the previous entries list stays open: the OpenAI dashboard budget limit is still the only control that actually stops the money, production still needs `npm run db:deploy` to carry `20260831180910_add_item_explanation` across, sign out still has no pending state, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unreferenced, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
- **2026-09-01 — Fix: the image lightbox did not fit a phone screen.** Reported against a Samsung S22, where the full-screen image was not respecting the screen dimensions. **One file** (`src/app/globals.css`), no new file, no migration, no new dependency, no component change, and the suite unchanged at **658 passing** — the diff is four declarations. **The cause: the dialog was centred against a different viewport than it was sized by.** The ShadCN `dialog` positions itself `fixed; top: 50%; left: 50%` and pulls back half its own size, and a fixed element measures percentages against the **layout** viewport, which tracks the viewport with the address bar *hidden*, while the box was sized `100dvh` — the viewport as it currently *is*. Centring a short box inside a taller one pushes half the difference off each end, so the toolbar went up under the address bar and the picture down under the gesture bar; on a phone carrying both bars that is roughly 60px lost at each end, and it is invisible on a desktop browser, which has no chrome that steals height. Fixed by anchoring to the top left (`top: 0; left: 0`) instead of centring. **The centring is undone with `translate: none` and not `transform: none`** — Tailwind v4 states those utilities through the `translate` property. That was not guessed: the very first measurement taken while building the lightbox read `transform: "none"` on the dialog while it sat at `top: 324px` in a 648px viewport, which only makes sense if the offset came from `translate`. `width: 100vw` went too, in favour of the primitive's own `w-full` with `max-width: none`: a percentage of the containing block is the viewport width without `vw`'s scrollbar caveat. **A second cause was covered and then had to be uncovered, which is the part worth remembering.** An unparseable unit drops the whole declaration, so a browser that does not understand `dvh` gets `height: auto`, the dialog is sized by its content and the picture keeps its intrinsic size — the same failure the desktop version had for a different reason, and Samsung Internet only understood these units from v21. The fix shipped `height: 100vh` followed by `height: 100svh`, and **checking the built stylesheet showed Lightning CSS had deleted the fallback**, judging it redundant for the project's browser targets. Rather than fight the toolchain or leave a declaration the build removes, the fallback was dropped and the comment now records the consequence honestly: every browser in those targets understands `svh`, and one older than them gets no height here at all. So what actually shipped addresses the centring, not the unit — which is the likelier cause anyway, and the one that explains a symptom on a current phone. **`svh` rather than `dvh`** is a deliberate change of unit: it is the height that fits whatever the address bar is doing, where `dvh` is whatever it happens to be doing at that moment. **Verified only as far as it can be here, and that is the honest state of this merge.** There is no S22 to test on, and an emulated viewport has no address bar that steals height, so **the reported symptom could not be reproduced and the fix is unconfirmed on a real device** — what was measured is that the dialog still covers the viewport exactly at 1039x700, 390x780, 360x640 and 360x540, in every case sitting at 0,0 at the full width and height with `translate: none`, the toolbar at y=0, the image bottom on the viewport bottom and no overflow on either axis. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` and `npm run build` are all clean, and the built rule was read back after Lightning CSS to confirm `top:0;left:0;translate:none;height:100svh;max-width:none`. **One environment note.** Testing on the phone needs `npx next dev -H 0.0.0.0` — the `dev` script is a bare `next dev`, which binds to localhost and is unreachable from the LAN — and the machine answered on `192.168.2.3:3000`. That session was abandoned: over the LAN the homepage rendered without its background or content and the buttons did nothing. **That is unexplained and was deliberately not investigated**, on the grounds that production is the real check; it is worth knowing before anyone repeats the exercise, and worth ruling out as a dev-server-over-LAN artefact rather than assuming it says anything about the app. Note also that emailed links are built from `APP_URL`, so they point at localhost from a phone session, while sign-in derives its callback from the request origin and works. **Left open:** the fix is unverified on the device it was reported from, which is the first thing to check on production; the unit fallback is gone, so a browser older than the project's targets still gets a content-sized dialog; and everything the previous entries list stays open — the minimap is still `aria-hidden` with no keyboard path to panning, there is still no next/previous within the gallery, the OpenAI dashboard budget limit is still the only control that actually stops the money, production still needs `npm run db:deploy` to carry `20260831180910_add_item_explanation` across, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unreferenced, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
- **2026-09-01 — Fix: a long title made the image lightbox wider than the screen.** Reported on a Samsung S22 in Chrome, where the picture ran off the side from the moment the popup opened. **One file** (`src/app/globals.css`), three declarations, no new file, no migration, no new dependency, no component change, and the suite unchanged at **658 passing**. **The image was never the cause, which is the whole lesson of this one.** The dialog is a grid and only its **rows** were ever stated, so the column was an implicit `auto` track — and an `auto` track sizes to the max-content of its widest item and **is free to exceed its container**. The widest item is the toolbar, whose max-content includes the *untruncated* title. Measured at 360px, a long filename made the column **822px** on a 360px screen: the dialog's `clientWidth` was 360 against a `scrollWidth` of 822, the stage inherited the track width, and the image — `width: 100%` of the stage — hung 462px off the side. **`min-width: 0` on the title could never have helped**, and it was already there: tracks are sized before the bar's flex children are given any chance to shrink, so the constraint never got a say. Fixed by stating the column as **`minmax(0, 1fr)`**, which cannot exceed the container, plus `min-width: 0` on the bar so the title actually reaches its ellipsis, and `overflow: hidden` on the dialog as a backstop for anything that tries the same thing later. **Why three rounds of mobile fixes missed it, which is worth more than the fix.** The first two reports were reasoned about rather than reproduced — the centring-against-the-wrong-viewport fix that preceded this was real and is still right, but it was never confirmed on the device, and this second symptom arrived immediately after. What finally worked was refusing to guess a third time: **three questions** established that it was the fullscreen popup rather than the drawer thumbnail, that it was wide *immediately* rather than after a pinch (which killed the leading theory, that the browser was page-zooming a gesture our handler had not captured), and that it was Chrome rather than Samsung Internet (which killed the CSS-support theory). A fourth question — **does the rest of the app look wide too** — was the decisive one: "only the lightbox" rules out the layout viewport being wider than the screen, because that would have made every page wide, and so proves the overflow comes from *inside* the dialog. That is what turned an unreproducible device report into a hypothesis testable at 360px in a desktop browser. **Two testing gaps are worth naming.** Every image verified up to this point was portrait or square — **a wide image had never been opened at all** — and the fix for that was to serve a synthetic one through `page.route()` (an SVG with explicit `width`/`height`, so `naturalWidth` is whatever the test wants) rather than uploading anything: nothing was written to the database or to R2. Wide, ultrawide and tall all turned out to fit correctly, which is what pointed the finger away from the image and at the container. And the titles on the seeded test items are short (140px), which is precisely why a desktop viewport and every earlier phone measurement passed — the bug needs a narrow screen *and* a long title together. **Verified by reproducing it and then watching it stop**, with a long title forced into the DOM, at 360x640, 390x780 and 1039x700, against images of 4000x800, 8000x400, 800x4000 and 3804x9200: in every case the dialog's `scrollWidth` now equals its `clientWidth` equals the viewport width, the image's right edge sits on the screen edge, the title ellipsises, all five controls are on screen and the document has no horizontal overflow. Zoom, pan and the minimap are unaffected (200%, the minimap box tracked to 0%, the image at +327.9). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` and `npm run build` are all clean, and the built rules were read back after Lightning CSS to confirm `grid-template-columns:minmax(0,1fr)`, `overflow:hidden` and the bar's `min-width:0`. **Left open:** this is the first of the three mobile fixes that is reproduced rather than reasoned about, but **it is still unconfirmed on the S22 itself** — the preceding centring fix is likewise unconfirmed, and both want checking on production; the minimap is still `aria-hidden` with no keyboard path to panning; there is still no next/previous within the gallery; and everything the previous entries list stays open — the OpenAI dashboard budget limit is still the only control that actually stops the money, production still needs `npm run db:deploy` to carry `20260831180910_add_item_explanation` across, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unreferenced, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
- **2026-09-01 — Camera capture on mobile, with HEIC conversion.** A phone can take a photo and save it as an Image item, where before it could only pick one that already existed. Three new files (`src/lib/heic.ts` + its test, `src/components/items/camera-button.tsx`), six edited, **no migration**, **no new ShadCN primitive**, and **one dependency** — `heic-to` (^1.5.2). **The reason the camera was never on offer had nothing to do with the camera.** `acceptAttribute` in `src/lib/file-constraints.ts` returned the extension list alone — `.png,.jpg,…` — and **Android's picker matches on media types**, so an extension-only `accept` gave Chrome nothing to match a camera against and it fell back to a plain file browser. It now emits extensions *and* the media types the constraint already held, which is inert on desktop and is probably the single most important line in the change. On top of that a **"Take photo" button carries `capture="environment"`**, which asks for the rear camera directly rather than leaving the camera as one option in the OS sheet. **It is hidden by `@media (pointer: coarse)` rather than by a check in JavaScript**, and that is a deliberate call: there is no way to ask whether a device has a camera without prompting for permission, and a media query costs no hydration and cannot mismatch. **Scope is the create dialog and the images listing.** The dialog covers Image items *and* Book covers, which share `FileUpload`. The listing matters more than it looks: `ItemDropZone` was drag-and-drop only, and **a phone cannot drop a file**, so that page offered a phone nothing at all — the button reuses the zone's existing upload-and-create loop rather than a second pipeline, which is why it lives inside that component instead of in the page heading beside the create dialog. **HEIC was chosen to be converted rather than refused, against the recommendation at load, and the number quoted when that choice was made was wrong.** The option was put as "roughly half a megabyte of wasm"; `heic-to` unpacks to **24 MB** in `node_modules` and its build is **2.86 MB**, the wasm being inlined as base64 — about six times what was said. It was flagged as soon as it was measured rather than after the fact, and kept, because the **dynamic `import()` puts it in a chunk of its own** — confirmed in the build, where it is a single 2.86 MB chunk against a largest app chunk of 0.27 MB — so it is fetched only by someone who has actually taken a HEIC photo, who would otherwise be refused outright. The conversion runs **before validation**, so what is checked, uploaded and stored is the JPEG, and `jpegName` swaps the extension because validation decides the stored content type from the extension rather than from what the browser reports. **HEIC is recognised by extension as well as by reported type**, and that is the branch that carries: Android hands these over as `application/octet-stream` or as an empty string often enough that trusting the type alone would let the original through to be refused by validation instead — **mutation-checked**, and dropping the extension test failed exactly the one case naming it. The HEIC extension and type sets live in `file-constraints.ts` rather than in `heic.ts` so the dependency runs one way only: `heic.ts` imports from the constraints module, which stays free of anything browser-only and safe for the upload route to import. `describe()` in the drop zone no longer refuses a HEIC up front, since what it would be judging is not what gets uploaded, and a `converting` status joined the batch's row states. **13 new tests take the suite 658 → 672**, and one existing assertion was **rewritten rather than deleted** — it pinned the old extensions-only accept string, which is the contract that deliberately changed. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` and `npm run build` are all clean, and the three new rules were confirmed present in the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS. **Verified only as far as it can be here, and the gap is the feature itself.** There is no camera in this browser and no HEIC file to hand, so **whether Android now offers the camera, and whether conversion actually works on a Samsung photo, are both untested** — they need the S22, and since the LAN dev server does not work on that phone (recorded two entries ago) that means production. What is proven is the accept string, the HEIC predicates and the name swap, all by unit test, plus that the decoder really is code-split. **Left open:** the conversion path has never been run against a real HEIC, so a failure there would surface as the "could not be read" message with no way to tell a decoder problem from a corrupt photo; there is no progress indication while a large photo is converting, only a "Converting photo…" line, and a multi-megapixel HEIC on a mid-range phone is not instant; the button is offered on `(pointer: coarse)`, so a touchscreen laptop shows it and a phone in desktop mode would not; and nothing resizes or strips EXIF from a camera photo before upload, so a 12 MP JPEG is stored at full size and its location metadata with it — worth a decision now that photos come from a camera rather than a file the user chose deliberately. Everything the previous entries list stays open: both lightbox fixes are still unconfirmed on the S22, the minimap is still `aria-hidden` with no keyboard path to panning, the OpenAI dashboard budget limit is still the only control that actually stops the money, production still needs `npm run db:deploy` to carry `20260831180910_add_item_explanation` across, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unreferenced, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
- **2026-09-01 — Fix: the item drawer's action labels collapse on mobile.** Five labelled buttons — Favorite, Pin, Copy, Edit, Delete — did not fit a phone, so the last of them fell off the row. **One source file** (`src/components/items/item-drawer-actions.tsx`), **no CSS change**, no migration, no dependency, no new primitive, and the suite unchanged at **672 passing** — the diff adds nothing importable. **The labels were bare text nodes inside the buttons, which is why this needed a component change at all**: CSS cannot hide a text node, so each is now wrapped in `<span className="action-label">`, the house pattern six other components already use (`collection-actions.tsx`, `pagination.tsx`, both create dialogs, and the two editor bars). **No rule was added and none was needed** — `.action-label { display: none }` already sits in the `max-width: 47.99rem` block, and the icon-only row measures about 196px against a drawer that is the full viewport width below 704px, so the existing `gap` and padding have room to spare; a rule of its own would only have been a seventh thing to keep in step. **The consequence worth recording: `display: none` also removes the label from the accessibility tree**, so a collapsed button would have had no accessible name at all — hence an `aria-label` on each of the four repeating its visible text, with the reasoning in the component's doc comment so it is not stripped later as redundant. **Delete needed nothing**: it was already icon-only with `aria-label="Delete item"`, which is exactly why it was the one button that survived a narrow screen and the shape the other four now match. **That same gap still exists for every other `.action-label` consumer** — the collection actions row, the pagination steps and the two editor copy buttons all lose their accessible name below the breakpoint — not fixed here, but now written down once. **"The slide" was read as the item drawer rather than the image lightbox**, and the reading was checked rather than assumed: the lightbox toolbar is already icon-only with `aria-label`s, so it has no labels to remove, and its four controls plus the zoom readout come to roughly 200px, which fits 360px without help. One boundary is deliberately conservative: the drawer is `width: 100%; max-width: 44rem`, so at a 767px viewport it is 704px wide and the labels hide anyway — harmless, and it keeps the drawer on the same breakpoint as the rest of the app rather than inventing a second one. **Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` (672) and `npm run build` all clean, with the dev server killed by PID and **the port confirmed free by `netstat` filtered on `LISTENING`** before `.next` was deleted — still the only reliable check here, and the "background command failed with exit code 1" notification that follows is the kill rather than a build problem, the seventeenth entry to record that artefact. **Not verified in the browser** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests. The one thing worth a look is the row at 360px with a favorited and pinned item, since both lit states now have to read from the icon alone. **Left open:** the edit mode's Cancel/Save row is text-only and untouched, being two buttons that fit; the accessible-name gap on the other six `.action-label` consumers, as above; and everything the previous entries list — both lightbox mobile fixes are still unconfirmed on the S22, the camera and HEIC paths have still never run against a real device or a real HEIC, the minimap is still `aria-hidden` with no keyboard path to panning, the OpenAI dashboard budget limit is still the only control that actually stops the money, production still needs `npm run db:deploy` to carry `20260831180910_add_item_explanation` across, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unreferenced, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
- **2026-09-01 — A spinner on the link that was clicked.** Clicking an in-app link showed nothing until the server answered, so it was unclear the click had registered. Two new components in one file (`src/components/layout/link-pending.tsx`), seven files edited, **no migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**; `npm test` stayed at **672 passing**, the diff adding nothing importable. **Why it was invisible: there is no `loading.tsx` anywhere under `src/app`.** Every page is `force-dynamic` and awaits at least some data *outside* its Suspense boundary — the type page awaits the item type and the page count for its heading and pagination — so the browser stays on the old page with nothing moving for a whole round trip. That is also why there is **no delay before the spinner appears**: nothing here resolves fast enough to flash, and a delay would leave a gap where the icon has gone and the spinner has not arrived. **A global top progress bar was built first, and rejected on sight.** It worked — one document-level click listener saw every in-app anchor, so no link, card, pagination step or menu item had to be touched at all, and anything added later would have been covered for free — and it was rolled back uncommitted because the feedback landing at the top of the window rather than on the thing that was clicked is not what the complaint was about. Worth knowing that the mechanism is sound if a bar is ever wanted for something else: a bubble-phase `click` listener on `document` (bubble, not capture, so `defaultPrevented` means what it says), skipping modified clicks, `target`, `download`, cross-origin and same-URL hrefs, with `usePathname` + `useSearchParams` as the signal that the new page landed. **What replaced it rests on one constraint: `useLinkStatus` reports only the nearest ancestor `<Link>`**, so the spinner has to be a *child* of each link — which is why this touches six components where the bar touched one, and why it cannot be a prop on a link or a wrapper around it. **Two shapes fall out of that and they are the whole design.** A link with a leading icon has the icon **swapped** for the spinner (`LinkPendingIcon`, which takes the resting icon as `children` and returns one or the other), and that is what makes the no-layout-shift goal free rather than fought for: **every one of those slots is already 1rem** — the sidebar type icons and folders are `size={16}`, the pagination chevrons are `size={16}`, and the recent-collection dot is 0.5rem with 0.25rem margins either side, which its own CSS comment says exists to make it occupy an icon slot. A link that is the empty anchor stretched over a card has nothing to swap, so `LinkSpinner` is centred over the card behind a scrim and the whole card reads as loading rather than one corner of it. The three stretched anchors (`.collection-card-open`, `.stat-card-open`, `.item-card-open`) take `display: grid; place-items: center` unconditionally, which costs nothing while they are empty, and the scrim arrives with `:has(.link-spinner)`. **A page number is the one link with neither an icon nor a card**, so the digit is what gives way — wrapped in `.pagination-page-number` and hidden by `:has`, with the box already `min-width: 2rem` so it holds its size through the swap. **One refactor was forced and is worth knowing about**: the sidebar's type rows share a `contents` fragment between the `<Link>` and the `<button>` a locked Pro type renders, and `useLinkStatus` outside a Link has nothing to report — so the leading icon was lifted out of `contents` into its own `icon` const, and only the Link branch wraps it. **The account menu links are deliberately out of scope, and this is the finding most likely to be re-discovered:** Radix closes the dropdown on select, so the entire subtree unmounts the instant the link is clicked and a spinner in a `DropdownMenuItem` could never be seen. The menu closing is already the feedback — the same argument the sign-in spinner entry made for leaving sign out alone. The sidebar has the same property on mobile, where `closeOnMobile` shuts the drawer on the way out, so the swap there is a desktop affordance. **The spin is grouped onto the existing `.spinner` rule rather than restated**, so `.link-spinner` inherits the `prefers-reduced-motion` override for free; both comments were amended to say so, and the reduced-motion one now records why a static loader is still honest here — the icon has already been replaced, which is the part that says the click landed. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` and `npm run build` are all clean, and every new rule was read back out of the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS — which **groups the three `:has` selectors into one `:is(...)`** and, as ever, emits a plain `background: var(--background)` fallback ahead of the `color-mix` one inside an `@supports`, so a grep for a single rule finds two. **Not verified by `/feature browser`** — per standing preference the workflow stops at a green build and tests, and the dev server was left up through implementation so the swap could be checked by hand. **Left open:** nothing covers a navigation started from code, so the `router.push` in the register form and the `router.replace` after a reset still show nothing — the forms have their own pending states, but a `useTransition` there would be the matching fix; **item cards are untouched** because they open the drawer rather than navigating, which means the dashboard and every type listing have cards that do not spin sitting beside collection cards that do; the whole surface is components and therefore **out of scope for Vitest by configuration** (`vitest.config.mts` collects only `src/lib/**` and `src/actions/**`), so the swap, the scrim and the reduced-motion branch all rest on the browser; and the real fix for the underlying slowness is still a `loading.tsx` per route, which this deliberately does not add. Everything the previous entries list stays open: both lightbox mobile fixes are still unconfirmed on the S22, the camera and HEIC paths have still never run against a real device or a real HEIC, the OpenAI dashboard budget limit is still the only control that actually stops the money, production still needs `npm run db:deploy` to carry `20260831180910_add_item_explanation` across, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unreferenced, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.

- **2026-09-01 — Findings from a UI layout review.** Six findings from a Playwright pass over the running app, signed in as demo, walked at 1440px and 390px across the marketing home, both auth pages, the dashboard, three type pages, collections index and detail, favorites, profile, settings, the item drawer and the search palette. One new file (`src/components/auth/github-auth-form.tsx`), nine edited, **no migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**; `npm test` stayed at **672 passing**, the diff adding nothing importable. **The sidebar finding was reported as "no active highlighting" and that framing was wrong in a useful way** — `data-active` is set correctly in `sidebar.tsx` and does render, confirmed live on `/items/snippets` and in the collapsed rail. The defect is that `globals.css` gave active and hover the **same** `--sidebar-accent` background with only a text-colour change between them, so against the coloured type icons and PRO badges the current row was indistinguishable from whatever the pointer was over. Fixed with a heavier label; **an inset 2px accent bar was implemented, shown, and taken back out on request as too loud**, which is worth recording so it is not proposed again as the obvious fix. **Dashboard and Favorites gained sidebar rows**, which was the larger half of that finding: neither had one at all — the brand was the only route to the dashboard and a dashboard stat card the only route to favorites — so those two pages left the whole sidebar with nothing marked, which is most of why the highlighting read as absent. "View all collections" now takes `data-active` too. **Profile and Settings were deliberately left in the account menu** rather than duplicated into the nav, that placement being a deliberate design the settings-page entry records; the consequence is that those two pages still highlight nothing, and it is now a decision rather than an oversight. **`/register` had no GitHub button** while `/sign-in` had one, so someone who wanted GitHub had to guess their way across, with nothing telling them that signing in there would create their account. `GitHubSubmit` was local to `sign-in-form.tsx` because it had exactly one caller; with a second it was **extracted rather than copied**, the same move `.action-label` and `ItemListSkeleton` made when a second consumer appeared, so both pages now render one line and cannot drift. **One action behind both** — `signInWithGitHub` — because OAuth has no separate registration: the adapter creates the row on a first sign-in, so only the wording differs and the label is the only prop. **A long unbroken title overflowed the item list on mobile**: `/items/snippets` at 390px measured `.dashboard-main` at `scrollWidth 416` against `clientWidth 375`, caused by the seeded `AiExplanationPanelProps`. The fix is `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on `.item-card-title`, and **`break-word` would not have worked** — the title is a bare text node beside the pin and star, so it is an **anonymous flex item** that cannot be given `min-width: 0` and will not shrink below its min-content width, and only `anywhere` reduces that width. That is the same anonymous-flex-item trap the file-row entry records for `text-overflow`, in its second form. The class is shared with the dashboard's Pinned and Recent lists, so one line covers every listing. **The prompt optimizer was already in the pricing section** and this was prominence rather than an omission — it arrived as the second half of `"Explain this code, prompt optimizer"`, selling the newest AI feature as an afterthought to another one, and was named three different ways across three surfaces (`ai-section.tsx` "Prompt optimizer", pricing "prompt optimizer", `upgrade-dialog.tsx` "prompt rewriting"). It now has its own bullet and all three say *prompt optimizer*. Two smaller fixes: the Editor font-size description read "The size code is set in, in pixels", and `data-scroll-behavior="smooth"` on `<html>` silences the dev-mode warning the marketing page's `html:has(.marketing)` rule raised on **every** navigation — the only recurring console noise in the app, everything else visited being clean. **Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` (672) and `npm run build` all clean, with every new rule read back out of the built stylesheet after Lightning CSS and the removed bar confirmed absent from it. **One process note, and it is the reason to keep gating rather than printing.** After the accent bar was removed a build was attempted and **correctly aborted**: a dev server had come back up on port 3000 (PID 35584) started outside this session, and the command gated on the `netstat` check rather than merely printing it — which is exactly the failure the file-upload entry records, where a check printed and the build proceeded anyway. `TaskStop` remains unreliable here, so the sequence is `taskkill //PID //F //T` and then `netstat` filtered on `LISTENING` until the port is genuinely free. The "background command failed with exit code 1" notification that follows each kill is the kill and not a build problem — the eighteenth entry to record that artefact. **The UI reviewer also left 15 untracked `.png` files in the repo root**, deleted before branching; note that `.playwright-mcp` holds two **tracked** `.yml` files committed on 2026-08-18, so artefact cleanup must never be a blanket `rm -rf` there. **Not re-verified in the browser after the change** — the user checked it on the running dev server, which is what produced the request to remove the bar. **Left open:** Profile and Settings still highlight nothing, as above; the active state now rests on a font-weight change plus text colour, which is subtler than the bar and may want revisiting if it still reads as weak; contrast ratios were not measured numerically and the full tab order was not walked on every page; and the black `/items/images` tile is the known `object-cover` crop on a very tall screenshot, already recorded as an accepted trade-off. Everything the previous entries list stays open: the OpenAI dashboard budget limit is still the only control that actually stops the money, production still needs `npm run db:deploy` to carry `20260831180910_add_item_explanation` across, sign out still has no pending state, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unreferenced, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
- **2026-09-01 — Server action deduplication.** Three findings from a scan of `src/actions/` for duplicated shape, done as one change. Three new files (`src/lib/preferences-action.ts`, `src/lib/action-guard.ts`, `src/types/action.ts`), twelve edited, **no migration**, **no new dependency** and **no new ShadCN primitive**. **Pure moves, and `npm test` staying at 672 passing is what covers them** — nothing about behaviour changed, so a green suite over untouched tests is the evidence rather than a weak result. **(1) The three settings-card actions were the same fifty lines three times over** — AI, editor and upload preferences, identical down to the comments (*"False for a session whose account is gone — the same conflation the item and collection writes make…"* appeared verbatim in all three), differing only in the schema, the column written and two message strings. `savePreferences` is that function once, and each action file is now a ~25-line wrapper naming only what is its own. **The helper has to live in `lib/`, and that is not a style choice**: a `"use server"` module may only export async functions, so a helper exported from an action file compiles and then fails at runtime — the exact error the auth-phase-3 entry records paying for once. The three result types now point at a shared `WriteResult`, keeping their names. **(2) `SIGNED_OUT` was declared verbatim in all eight action modules** and is now one export in `src/constants/messages.ts` — the second instance of the pattern that file's own doc comment describes for `UNREACHABLE` (17 copies), and a small enough one to have actually collapsed; `grep` confirms the string appears exactly once in `src/`. **(3) `writeItemFlag` had the right idea and stopped at the module boundary.** It extracted session → write → `MISSING` → catch-and-log for the two item flags in `items.ts`, while `collections.ts` hand-wrote the same skeleton three more times — `setCollectionFavorite` being an exact structural match. It is now `writeFlag` in `src/lib/action-guard.ts` beside a broader `withSession`, which takes the session check and the try/catch/log and lets the body decide what a success looks like — so it serves the actions that answer with data as well as the ones that answer with nothing, and leaves room for the checks that are *not* shared (`updateItem` refusing a foreign collection id, `deleteItem` removing an R2 object first) by keeping them in the body rather than around it. **Five call sites instead of two.** **Two things deliberately left alone, both flagged rather than discovered later.** `createItem` and `createCollection` read `getCurrentUser` for `isPro` where the helper resolves `getCurrentUserId`, so forcing them through would have meant a second variant of the helper for two callers. And **`deleteItem` keeps its inner `try`**, so a failed R2 delete still reports *which half* failed and keeps the item — the wrapper's catch is only the outer one. The item and collection results that carry `data` are untouched: `types/item.ts` records keeping `CreateItemResult` and `UpdateItemResult` apart on purpose so the two actions can diverge, and collapsing them onto one generic would undo a decision rather than a duplication. **Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint src`, `npm test` (672) and `npm run build` all clean, with the route table identical before and after. `items.ts` went 365 → 311 and `collections.ts` 190 → 147; the net is −292/+277 across 15 files, which understates it, since 152 of the additions are the three new shared modules and their reasoning. **One process note:** a dev server was live when the build was due and **the build command gated on the `netstat` check rather than printing it** — the failure the file-upload entry records, where a check printed and the build proceeded anyway. The user was asked, PID 35352 was killed with `taskkill //F //T`, the port was confirmed free by `netstat` filtered on `LISTENING`, and only then was `.next` deleted; the "background command failed with exit code 1" notification that followed is the kill of the earlier background dev task, not a build problem — the nineteenth entry to record that artefact. The commit message went through `git commit -F` rather than a heredoc, the history recording three silent heredoc failures on prose containing apostrophes. **Left open — the other three findings from the same scan, none of which were in scope here.** `ai.ts` is 663 lines and roughly 250 of them are not actions: the gate machinery (`Guarded` / `guard` / `authorize` / `allowSpend`) and the prompt-input builders (`describeItem` / `describeCode`) are pure logic in a `"use server"` file, and `describeItem` in particular is the function keeping the privacy page's "only the title, description and content are sent" claim true — it belongs beside `prompts.ts` and `truncate.ts`, and moving it would put it where Vitest can reach it directly rather than only through the actions. `explainCode` and `optimizePrompt` share a four-step opener (read item → `MISSING` → type predicate → empty-content refusal) differing only in the predicate and two strings. And **`allowSpend` at `ai.ts:591` looks parallel and is not** — `const [a, b] = [await rateLimit(…), await rateLimit(…)]` evaluates left to right, so every AI call makes two sequential Redis round trips where `Promise.all` would halve it. Also noted while reading: `firstIssueMessage` lives in `validations/auth.ts` but has five non-auth consumers (items, collections, billing, ai, profile), which is a generic Zod helper wearing an auth name. Everything the previous entries list stays open: the OpenAI dashboard budget limit is still the only control that actually stops the money, production still needs `npm run db:deploy` to carry `20260831180910_add_item_explanation` across, sign out still has no pending state, there is no way to remove a single item from a collection, `R2_PUBLIC_URL` is still unreferenced, nothing sorts or filters the listing pages, no CI runs the suite, and the `(app)` route group is still not adopted.
